<!-- Generated from the supplied PDF by scripts/index-rulebook.py. -->

## 000. Golden and Silver Rules {#rule-000}


### 001. Golden Rule {#rule-001}


<div id="rule-002" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-002" aria-label="Link to rule 002">002.</a> Card text supersedes rules text. Whenever a card fundamentally contradicts the rules, the card's indication is what is true.</p>
</div>

### 050. Silver Rule {#rule-050}


<div id="rule-051" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-051" aria-label="Link to rule 051">051.</a> Card text uses different terminology than rules. Card text should be interpreted according to these rules, not as though it were text within these rules.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-052" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-052" aria-label="Link to rule 052">052.</a> Card, when written in card effects, is shorthand for "Main Deck card." Runes, legends, and battlefields are not considered cards when executing the abilities and effects of game objects. They are considered cards for the purposes of these rules.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-053" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-053" aria-label="Link to rule 053">053.</a> Cards refer to themselves in the first person.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-053-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-053-1" aria-label="Link to rule 053.1">053.1.</a> * Units and legends say "I," "me," etc.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-053-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-053-2" aria-label="Link to rule 053.2">053.2.</a> * Gear and spells say "this."</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-053-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-053-3" aria-label="Link to rule 053.3">053.3.</a> * Battlefields say "here."</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-053-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-053-4" aria-label="Link to rule 053.4">053.4.</a> * Cards may refer to themselves by their name for clarity. This is shorthand for the above terms.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-054" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-054" aria-label="Link to rule 054">054.</a> “Can’t beats Can”</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-054-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-054-1" aria-label="Link to rule 054.1">054.1.</a> Cards that forbid actions or effects, as a broad method of determination, supersede cards that allow or permit that same action or effect.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-054-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-054-2" aria-label="Link to rule 054.2">054.2.</a> If a card specifies that an action can “only” be performed under certain circumstances, it cannot be performed under any other circumstances.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-055" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-055" aria-label="Link to rule 055">055.</a> When executing card text, do as much as you can, ignoring impossible instructions.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-055-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-055-1" aria-label="Link to rule 055.1">055.1.</a> If all of a card’s instructions are impossible, it is still played and resolved, but nothing happens.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-056" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-056" aria-label="Link to rule 056">056.</a> Cards a player owns may never be placed into a non-Board zone belonging to another player.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-056-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-056-1" aria-label="Link to rule 056.1">056.1.</a> Non-Board zones corresponding to a player include Main Deck, Rune Deck, Trash, Hand, Chosen Champion zone, and Banishment.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-056-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-056-2" aria-label="Link to rule 056.2">056.2.</a> If a card would enter such a zone, it goes to its owner’s corresponding zone instead.</p>
</div>

## 100. Game Concepts {#rule-100}


### 101. Deck Construction {#rule-101}


<div id="rule-102" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-102" aria-label="Link to rule 102">102.</a> Riftbound is a Trading Card Game where a player must provide their own cards to play against other players.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-103" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103" aria-label="Link to rule 103">103.</a> To play Riftbound, a player must have a Main Deck, a Rune Deck, a Champion Legend, and a number of Battlefields determined by the Mode of Play. These are collectively referred to as a player’s deck.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-1" aria-label="Link to rule 103.1">103.1.</a> Cards in a player’s deck or rules of a Game Mode may indicate additional choices to be made while building the deck.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A unit reads in part “If [Name] is in your deck, choose 3 different legends in addition to your starting legend.” This decision is made while building the deck.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: The rules of a game mode read in part “Choose an additional Chosen Champion with a different Champion Tag and Domain than your Champion Legend. Place it in the Champion Zone at the start of the game alongside your other Chosen Champion. Your deck’s Domain Identity includes that additional Chosen Champion’s Domain, if applicable.” The Chosen Champion chosen this way is decided while building the deck.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-2" aria-label="Link to rule 103.2">103.2.</a> 1 Champion Legend</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 103.2.a">103.2.a.</a> This is placed in the Legend Zone at the start of the game.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 103.2.b">103.2.b.</a> This will dictate the Domain Identity of the deck.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-2-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-2-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 103.2.b.1">103.2.b.1.</a> Cards included in your deck must abide by your Domain Identity.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-2-b-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-2-b-2" aria-label="Link to rule 103.2.b.2">103.2.b.2.</a> Your deck’s Domain Identity is dictated by the domains of your Champion Legend. In the default card frame, these domains appear as symbols in the legend’s upper left corner.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-2-b-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-2-b-3" aria-label="Link to rule 103.2.b.3">103.2.b.3.</a> If a card has a single Domain, then that card is permitted in the Domain Identity that corresponds to the same Domain.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-2-b-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-2-b-4" aria-label="Link to rule 103.2.b.4">103.2.b.4.</a> If a card has more than one Domain, then that card is permitted only in a Domain Identity that contains all of the indicated Domains on that card.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-2-b-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-2-b-5" aria-label="Link to rule 103.2.b.5">103.2.b.5.</a> If a card has no Domain, then that card is permitted in decks of any Domain Identity.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-2-b-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-2-b-6" aria-label="Link to rule 103.2.b.6">103.2.b.6.</a> Some Game Effects may allow a player to add cards to their deck irrespective of those cards’ Domains. Those cards are considered part of their deck’s Domain Identity, regardless of their Domain.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-3" aria-label="Link to rule 103.3">103.3.</a> A Main Deck of at least 40 cards: A Chosen Champion Unit, as well as Units, Gear, and Spells</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 103.3.a">103.3.a.</a> Chosen Champion</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-3-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-3-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 103.3.a.1">103.3.a.1.</a> This will be placed in the Champion Zone at the start of the game.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-3-a-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-3-a-2" aria-label="Link to rule 103.3.a.2">103.3.a.2.</a> Must be a champion unit with a champion tag that matches the tag on your Champion Legend.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Loose Cannon has the tag Jinx. Therefore, a player could choose Jinx, Rebel or Jinx, Demolitionist as their Chosen Champion, because they also have the tag Jinx.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Tibbers has the tag Annie, but it is a signature unit, not a champion unit. It cannot be your Chosen Champion, even if your Champion Legend has the tag Annie.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-3-a-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-3-a-3" aria-label="Link to rule 103.3.a.3">103.3.a.3.</a> A player’s Chosen Champion is both the specific card chosen for this slot during Deck Building and also any Champion Unit with the same name as the specific card selected for this specific slot during the course of play.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player chose Jinx, Rebel as their Chosen Champion. One copy of Jinx, Rebel starts the game in their Chosen Champion zone. Any additional copies of Jinx, Rebel in their Main Deck, their hand, their trash, or on the board also count as their Chosen Champion for any game rules and effects that care about Chosen Champion status during the game.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-3-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-3-b" aria-label="Link to rule 103.3.b">103.3.b.</a> Your Main Deck can include up to 3 copies of the same named card.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-3-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-3-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 103.3.b.1">103.3.b.1.</a> This includes your Chosen Champion.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A deck could include Volibear, Furious as its Chosen Champion and still include 2 more copies of Volibear, Furious in their Main Deck.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-3-b-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-3-b-2" aria-label="Link to rule 103.3.b.2">103.3.b.2.</a> Cards have different names even if they represent the same character.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A deck could include 3 copies of Yasuo, Remorseful and 3 copies of Yasuo, Windrider, because they have different names.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-3-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-3-c" aria-label="Link to rule 103.3.c">103.3.c.</a> Subject to Domain Identity.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-3-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-3-d" aria-label="Link to rule 103.3.d">103.3.d.</a> Your deck may only contain 3 total Signature cards that have the same Champion tag as your Champion Legend.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-3-d-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-3-d-1" aria-label="Link to rule 103.3.d.1">103.3.d.1.</a> Regardless of name, a deck may only contain a sum total of 3 Signature cards.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-3-d-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-3-d-2" aria-label="Link to rule 103.3.d.2">103.3.d.2.</a> All of the Signature cards must have the Champion tag that corresponds to the Champion Legend of the deck.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-3-d-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-3-d-3" aria-label="Link to rule 103.3.d.3">103.3.d.3.</a> Signature cards are not Champion units and cannot be placed in the Champion Zone.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-3-e" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-3-e" aria-label="Link to rule 103.3.e">103.3.e.</a> Subject to card legality of the Format being played.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-4" aria-label="Link to rule 103.4">103.4.</a> Rune Deck</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 103.4.a">103.4.a.</a> 12 Rune Cards</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-4-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-4-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 103.4.a.1">103.4.a.1.</a> Cards in the Rune Deck must be of the Domain Identity of your Champion Legend.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-4-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-4-b" aria-label="Link to rule 103.4.b">103.4.b.</a> Must be shuffled and kept separate from the Main Deck.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-5" aria-label="Link to rule 103.5">103.5.</a> Battlefields</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-5-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-5-a" aria-label="Link to rule 103.5.a">103.5.a.</a> The number will be dictated by your Mode of Play.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-5-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-5-b" aria-label="Link to rule 103.5.b">103.5.b.</a> Subject to Domain Identity if applicable.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-103-5-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-103-5-c" aria-label="Link to rule 103.5.c">103.5.c.</a> Cannot include more than one of a Battlefield of the same name when there are more than one required for the deck.</p>
</div>

### 104. Setup {#rule-104}


<div id="rule-105" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-105" aria-label="Link to rule 105">105.</a> Spaces</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-106" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-106" aria-label="Link to rule 106">106.</a> The Play Area is a collection of logical Zones that are defined by their behaviors and relationships to Players. These Zones are grouped into The Board and Non-Board Zones.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-107" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-107" aria-label="Link to rule 107">107.</a> The Board</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-107-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-107-1" aria-label="Link to rule 107.1">107.1.</a> Bases</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-107-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-107-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 107.1.a">107.1.a.</a> Each player has their own Base.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-107-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-107-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 107.1.b">107.1.b.</a> Each Base is a Location.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-197">197</a>. Locations for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-107-1-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-107-1-c" aria-label="Link to rule 107.1.c">107.1.c.</a> Permanents and Runes controlled by a player reside in that player's Base.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-107-1-c-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-107-1-c-1" aria-label="Link to rule 107.1.c.1">107.1.c.1.</a> Permanents attached to Permanents controlled by a player may also be found in that player’s Base, regardless of their controller.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-107-1-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-107-1-d" aria-label="Link to rule 107.1.d">107.1.d.</a> Permanents and Runes in Bases are Public Information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-107-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-107-2" aria-label="Link to rule 107.2">107.2.</a> Battlefield Zone</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-107-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-107-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 107.2.a">107.2.a.</a> The Battlefield Zone houses multiple Battlefields.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-107-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-107-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 107.2.b">107.2.b.</a> Each Battlefield is individually a Location.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-197">197</a>. Locations for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-107-2-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-107-2-c" aria-label="Link to rule 107.2.c">107.2.c.</a> Battlefields and permanents at Battlefields are Public Information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-107-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-107-3" aria-label="Link to rule 107.3">107.3.</a> Facedown Zones</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-107-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-107-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 107.3.a">107.3.a.</a> Each Battlefield is associated with a sub-zone called a Facedown Zone, a single logical space that a card can be put facedown in through various Game Effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-107-3-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-107-3-b" aria-label="Link to rule 107.3.b">107.3.b.</a> Each Facedown Zone has a maximum occupancy of one card.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-107-3-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-107-3-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 107.3.b.1">107.3.b.1.</a> The maximum occupancy of a Facedown Zone can increase or decrease.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-107-3-b-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-107-3-b-2" aria-label="Link to rule 107.3.b.2">107.3.b.2.</a> If the maximum occupancy of a Facedown Zone decreases to a number less than the number of cards currently located there, the controller of that Facedown Zone must put a number of cards equal to the difference in the trash from the Facedown Zone.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-107-3-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-107-3-c" aria-label="Link to rule 107.3.c">107.3.c.</a> Cards can only be placed in or occupy the Facedown Zone if the controller of the card also controls the associated Battlefield.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-107-3-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-107-3-d" aria-label="Link to rule 107.3.d">107.3.d.</a> If a player loses Control of a Battlefield, any cards in the Facedown Zone associated with that Battlefield are removed during the next Cleanup.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-318">318</a>. Cleanups for more information.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-422">422</a>. Hide for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-107-3-e" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-107-3-e" aria-label="Link to rule 107.3.e">107.3.e.</a> Facedown Zones are not locations.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-107-3-f" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-107-3-f" aria-label="Link to rule 107.3.f">107.3.f.</a> Facedown Zones are Public Zones, though facedown cards located there are Private.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-107-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-107-4" aria-label="Link to rule 107.4">107.4.</a> Legend Zone</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-107-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-107-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 107.4.a">107.4.a.</a> The space for each player's Champion Legend.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-107-4-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-107-4-b" aria-label="Link to rule 107.4.b">107.4.b.</a> This is not a location.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-107-4-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-107-4-c" aria-label="Link to rule 107.4.c">107.4.c.</a> The Champion Legend here is a Game Object.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-119">119</a>. Game Objects for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-107-4-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-107-4-d" aria-label="Link to rule 107.4.d">107.4.d.</a> The Champion Legend cannot be removed, moved, or displaced from this zone.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-107-4-d-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-107-4-d-1" aria-label="Link to rule 107.4.d.1">107.4.d.1.</a> Some effects may add a legend card or token to the Legend Zone. These legends are not Champion Legends.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-107-4-d-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-107-4-d-2" aria-label="Link to rule 107.4.d.2">107.4.d.2.</a> Non-Champion legends may be removed from the Legend Zone. They cannot exist in any zone other than the Legend Zone or Banishment.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108" aria-label="Link to rule 108">108.</a> Non-Board Zones</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-1" aria-label="Link to rule 108.1">108.1.</a> The Chain</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 108.1.a">108.1.a.</a> Cards and abilities are placed here as part of the process of being played.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-327">327</a>. Chains for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 108.1.b">108.1.b.</a> Cards and abilities on the Chain are Public Information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-2" aria-label="Link to rule 108.2">108.2.</a> Trashes</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 108.2.a">108.2.a.</a> Each player has their own Trash.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 108.2.b">108.2.b.</a> This space is where cards are placed when they are killed or discarded, when they finish being executed as spells, when they are moved to the trash as an effect, etc.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-2-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-2-c" aria-label="Link to rule 108.2.c">108.2.c.</a> Cards in each player’s Trash are unordered. Their sequence does not matter, and they may be reorganized.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-2-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-2-d" aria-label="Link to rule 108.2.d">108.2.d.</a> Cards in a player's Trash are Public Information.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-128">128</a>. Privacy for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-3" aria-label="Link to rule 108.3">108.3.</a> Champion Zones</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 108.3.a">108.3.a.</a> Each player has their own Champion Zone.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-3-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-3-b" aria-label="Link to rule 108.3.b">108.3.b.</a> The space each player puts their Chosen Champion at the start of the game.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-3-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-3-c" aria-label="Link to rule 108.3.c">108.3.c.</a> The Chosen Champion cannot be returned to this zone by normal means.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-3-c-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-3-c-1" aria-label="Link to rule 108.3.c.1">108.3.c.1.</a> If a Chosen Champion is instructed to be returned to this zone, it can only do so if there is not a card already in this zone.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-3-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-3-d" aria-label="Link to rule 108.3.d">108.3.d.</a> The Chosen Champion can be played from here as normal, following the rules of Playing a Card.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-349">349</a>. Playing Cards for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-3-e" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-3-e" aria-label="Link to rule 108.3.e">108.3.e.</a> Cards in a player’s Champion Zone are Public Information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-4" aria-label="Link to rule 108.4">108.4.</a> Main Deck Zones</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 108.4.a">108.4.a.</a> Each player has their own Main Deck Zone.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-4-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-4-b" aria-label="Link to rule 108.4.b">108.4.b.</a> The space for the player's Main Deck.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-4-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-4-c" aria-label="Link to rule 108.4.c">108.4.c.</a> Primarily houses the face-down Main Deck during gameplay.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-4-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-4-d" aria-label="Link to rule 108.4.d">108.4.d.</a> The number of cards in a player’s Main Deck is Public Information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-4-e" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-4-e" aria-label="Link to rule 108.4.e">108.4.e.</a> The order of cards in the Main Deck is Secret Information during the course of play.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-128">128</a>. Privacy for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-5" aria-label="Link to rule 108.5">108.5.</a> Rune Deck Zones</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-5-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-5-a" aria-label="Link to rule 108.5.a">108.5.a.</a> Each player has their own Rune Deck Zone.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-5-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-5-b" aria-label="Link to rule 108.5.b">108.5.b.</a> The space for the player's Rune Deck.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-5-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-5-c" aria-label="Link to rule 108.5.c">108.5.c.</a> Primarily houses the face-down runes during gameplay.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-5-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-5-d" aria-label="Link to rule 108.5.d">108.5.d.</a> The order of runes in the Rune Deck is Secret Information during the course of play.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-128">128</a>. Privacy for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-6" aria-label="Link to rule 108.6">108.6.</a> Banishments</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-6-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-6-a" aria-label="Link to rule 108.6.a">108.6.a.</a> Each player has their own Banishment.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-6-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-6-b" aria-label="Link to rule 108.6.b">108.6.b.</a> This space is where cards are placed when they are banished by a spell or effect.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-6-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-6-c" aria-label="Link to rule 108.6.c">108.6.c.</a> Represents cards that have been removed from play in a more difficult-to-recover way, or a temporary space to hold cards while effects are being processed.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-6-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-6-d" aria-label="Link to rule 108.6.d">108.6.d.</a> The cards in the banishment are unordered. Their sequence does not matter, and they may be reorganized.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-6-e" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-6-e" aria-label="Link to rule 108.6.e">108.6.e.</a> The cards in any player's Banishment are Public Information.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-128">128</a>. Privacy for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-7" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-7" aria-label="Link to rule 108.7">108.7.</a> The Hand</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-7-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-7-a" aria-label="Link to rule 108.7.a">108.7.a.</a> Each player has their own Hand.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-7-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-7-b" aria-label="Link to rule 108.7.b">108.7.b.</a> This is the zone that cards go to when Drawn.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-7-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-7-c" aria-label="Link to rule 108.7.c">108.7.c.</a> The cards in a player's hand are Private Information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-7-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-7-d" aria-label="Link to rule 108.7.d">108.7.d.</a> The cards in a player’s hand are Unordered. Their sequence does not matter and they may be reorganized.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-7-e" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-7-e" aria-label="Link to rule 108.7.e">108.7.e.</a> The number of cards in a player's hand is Public Information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-108-7-f" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-108-7-f" aria-label="Link to rule 108.7.f">108.7.f.</a> The hand can be targeted as a zone, and cards there can be affected by spells and effects when specified.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-109" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-109" aria-label="Link to rule 109">109.</a> All Game Objects in the collective Play Areas are Public Information.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-119">119</a>. Game Objects for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-109-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-109-1" aria-label="Link to rule 109.1">109.1.</a> Any player may view, or be provided with the details of the face-up information printed on the cards in the Play Area.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-109-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-109-2" aria-label="Link to rule 109.2">109.2.</a> The State of all Game Objects in the Play Area is also Public Information.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Examples: If a Unit is Buffed If a Gear is Exhausted Etc.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-110" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-110" aria-label="Link to rule 110">110.</a> Setup Process</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-111" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-111" aria-label="Link to rule 111">111.</a> Each player separates their Champion Legend and places it in the Legend Zone.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-112" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-112" aria-label="Link to rule 112">112.</a> Each player separates their Chosen Champion and places it in the Champion Zone.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-113" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-113" aria-label="Link to rule 113">113.</a> Each player sets aside their Battlefields.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-113-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-113-1" aria-label="Link to rule 113.1">113.1.</a> Your Mode of Play will dictate how these are used and placed into the Battlefield Zone.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-483">483</a>. Modes of Play for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-114" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-114" aria-label="Link to rule 114">114.</a> Each player shuffles their Main and Rune Decks, separately, then places them into their respective Zones.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-114-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-114-1" aria-label="Link to rule 114.1">114.1.</a> The Main Deck is placed in the Main Deck Zone.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-114-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-114-2" aria-label="Link to rule 114.2">114.2.</a> The Rune Deck is placed in the Rune Deck Zone.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-115" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-115" aria-label="Link to rule 115">115.</a> Determine Turn Order using any fair random method agreed on by all players.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-115-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-115-1" aria-label="Link to rule 115.1">115.1.</a> Turn Order is established as a repeating set of the players.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-115-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-115-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 115.1.a">115.1.a.</a> The Mode of Play will specify how to determine a First Player.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-115-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-115-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 115.1.b">115.1.b.</a> If not otherwise specified, Seating then determines the sequence, play proceeding clockwise from the First Player.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-115-1-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-115-1-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 115.1.b.1">115.1.b.1.</a> The First Player is the player who becomes the Turn Player first.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-115-1-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-115-1-c" aria-label="Link to rule 115.1.c">115.1.c.</a> The Turn Order generates a looping queue of turns, starting with the First Player and repeating until the game ends.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-115-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-115-2" aria-label="Link to rule 115.2">115.2.</a> See the selected Mode of Play for any adjustments to each player's first turn.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-483">483</a>. Modes of Play for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-116" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-116" aria-label="Link to rule 116">116.</a> Players each draw 4.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-117" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-117" aria-label="Link to rule 117">117.</a> In turn order, players perform their Mulligan.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-117-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-117-1" aria-label="Link to rule 117.1">117.1.</a> A player may choose up to two cards in their hand. They set those cards aside.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-117-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-117-2" aria-label="Link to rule 117.2">117.2.</a> Then, that player draws as many cards as they set aside.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-117-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-117-3" aria-label="Link to rule 117.3">117.3.</a> Finally, that player Recycles the cards that were set aside.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-417">417</a>. Recycle for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-118" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-118" aria-label="Link to rule 118">118.</a> Begin play with the First Player taking their turn.</p>
</div>

### 119. Game Objects {#rule-119}


<div id="rule-120" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-120" aria-label="Link to rule 120">120.</a> A Game Object is any game piece that can produce one or more Game Effects or grant prerequisites for players to take Game Actions.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-121" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-121" aria-label="Link to rule 121">121.</a> A Game Object does not include nor preclude any inherent properties beyond the ability to produce, or act as the prerequisite for, Game Effects and Game Actions.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-122" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-122" aria-label="Link to rule 122">122.</a> A Game Object can be a literal object or a logical object.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-123" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-123" aria-label="Link to rule 123">123.</a> Game Objects include, but are not limited to, the following: Main Deck cards in any zone Runes in any zone Legends Battlefields Tokens in any zone Abilities of any type on the chain Counters and other status markers</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-124" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-124" aria-label="Link to rule 124">124.</a> A Game Object that changes zones to or from a Non-Board Zone becomes a new object for the purposes of tracking that object.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-124-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-124-1" aria-label="Link to rule 124.1">124.1.</a> Whenever a Game Object changes zones to or from a Non-Board Zone, all Temporary Modifications of all kinds cease to be tracked on it in all capacities.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Examples: Damage is cleared. Counters are removed. Granted Keywords are no longer granted. Statuses are cleared.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-124-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-124-2" aria-label="Link to rule 124.2">124.2.</a> A Game Object can have any of the following statuses (non-exhaustive): Attached, Attacking, Buffed, Banished, Controlled, Defending, Empowered, Equipped, Exhausted, Facedown, Readied, Replaced, Revealed, Stunned, and any applied Layer effects.</p>
</div>

### 125. Cards {#rule-125}


<div id="rule-126" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-126" aria-label="Link to rule 126">126.</a> Riftbound games are composed of players playing the cards in their respective decks.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-127" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-127" aria-label="Link to rule 127">127.</a> Ownership</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-127-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-127-1" aria-label="Link to rule 127.1">127.1.</a> For gameplay purposes, a card's Owner is the player who brought it into the game, either as their Champion Legend, one of their Battlefields, as part of their Main Deck or Rune Deck, or Created during the process of play. The legal owner of a card doesn't matter during gameplay.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Bo didn't bring a deck, so Alice loans him a deck. During the game, Bo is considered the owner of those cards, even though legally they belong to Alice.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-128" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-128" aria-label="Link to rule 128">128.</a> Privacy</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-128-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-128-1" aria-label="Link to rule 128.1">128.1.</a> A card will always exist in one of the many Zones described during Setup.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-128-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-128-2" aria-label="Link to rule 128.2">128.2.</a> A card will have a different level of Privacy based on its state or where it resides at a given time.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-128-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-128-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 128.2.a">128.2.a.</a> Unless specified otherwise by the state of the card, a card’s Privacy is the same as the zone it is located in.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-128-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-128-3" aria-label="Link to rule 128.3">128.3.</a> Secret: This level of Privacy indicates that no player may read or look at the face of the card.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-128-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-128-4" aria-label="Link to rule 128.4">128.4.</a> Private: This level of Privacy indicates that only the controller of a card on the board or the owner of a card in any other zone may read or look at the face of the card.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Cards in a player's hand are owned by that player. Only that player may read or look at their faces.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: If a player controls a facedown card at a battlefield, that player and only that player may read or look at that card's face, regardless of who owns that card.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-128-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-128-5" aria-label="Link to rule 128.5">128.5.</a> Public: This level of Privacy indicates that any player may read or look at the face of the card.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-128-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-128-6" aria-label="Link to rule 128.6">128.6.</a> A player cannot be compelled to perform an action on cards whose privacy is secret or private if that action specifies a type or quality of card.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A card effect reads, “Play a unit from your hand, ignoring its costs.” Although the effect does not say “may”, the player can choose not to perform the action even if they have a unit in their hand, because the effect specifies a card type.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-128-6-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-128-6-a" aria-label="Link to rule 128.6.a">128.6.a.</a> If an effect would compel a player to perform an action on cards whose privacy is secret or private, and if that effect specifies a type or quality of card, the player in question may choose to ignore that instruction. Ignored instructions are deemed impossible.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-129" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-129" aria-label="Link to rule 129">129.</a> Back Side</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-129-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-129-1" aria-label="Link to rule 129.1">129.1.</a> The back side of a card is the side with the Riftbound logo.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-129-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-129-2" aria-label="Link to rule 129.2">129.2.</a> There is a unique pattern of back side for each of the three categories of card: Main Deck cards, Rune Deck cards, and Battlefields and Champion Legends.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-129-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-129-3" aria-label="Link to rule 129.3">129.3.</a> The back side of a card is presented to conceal information for Private and Secret information cards. For example, cards in the Main Deck and cards in hand have their back side presented.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-129-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-129-4" aria-label="Link to rule 129.4">129.4.</a> The term facedown is used to describe the state during regular play of having a card's back side presented on the Board. A facedown card’s front side is considered Private Information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-130" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-130" aria-label="Link to rule 130">130.</a> Front Side</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-130-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-130-1" aria-label="Link to rule 130.1">130.1.</a> The front side of a card is the opposite of its back.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-130-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-130-2" aria-label="Link to rule 130.2">130.2.</a> This is also referred to as a front face.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-130-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-130-3" aria-label="Link to rule 130.3">130.3.</a> This can be identified in situations where the back is not easily discernible by the presence of the Play Cost in the upper left corner of the card.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-130-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-130-4" aria-label="Link to rule 130.4">130.4.</a> Cards, when played, are played with their front face presented.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-130-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-130-5" aria-label="Link to rule 130.5">130.5.</a> The term face up is used to describe the state during regular play of having a card's front face presented</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-130-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-130-6" aria-label="Link to rule 130.6">130.6.</a> Cards are considered to be Public Information while in this state, even if obscured by other cards in a pile. Example: The Trash is Public Information because all cards there have their front face presented even while stacked.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-131" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-131" aria-label="Link to rule 131">131.</a> Cost</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-131-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-131-1" aria-label="Link to rule 131.1">131.1.</a> Main Deck cards have a Cost, listed in the upper left corner of the front face of the card.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-165">165</a>. Rune Pools for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-131-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-131-2" aria-label="Link to rule 131.2">131.2.</a> Energy Cost</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-131-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-131-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 131.2.a">131.2.a.</a> The numeral listed in the Cost element in the upper left corner of the card.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-131-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-131-3" aria-label="Link to rule 131.3">131.3.</a> Power cost</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-131-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-131-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 131.3.a">131.3.a.</a> The symbols, listed vertically, in the Cost element in the upper left corner of the card.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-131-3-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-131-3-b" aria-label="Link to rule 131.3.b">131.3.b.</a> This section may not be present on every card.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-132" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-132" aria-label="Link to rule 132">132.</a> Name</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-132-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-132-1" aria-label="Link to rule 132.1">132.1.</a> Each card has a name that identifies it uniquely.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-132-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-132-2" aria-label="Link to rule 132.2">132.2.</a> This is usually located in the middle of the card.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-132-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-132-3" aria-label="Link to rule 132.3">132.3.</a> Cards that are printed in different languages but represent the same card are considered to have the same name for the purposes of deckbuilding and gameplay.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Chemtech Enforcer in English and its counterpart in Chinese are considered the same card, despite the Name element on the card reading differently.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-132-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-132-4" aria-label="Link to rule 132.4">132.4.</a> Some cards have both a short name and a subtitle. For all purposes, including rules and deckbuilding, such a card’s name is “[Short Name], [Subtitle]”.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Kai’Sa, Evolutionary and Kai’Sa, Survivor both have the short name Kai’Sa, but they have different names. You can include 3 of each in your deck under normal deckbuilding rules. If one of them is your Chosen Champion, the other is not.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-133" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-133" aria-label="Link to rule 133">133.</a> Category</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-133-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-133-1" aria-label="Link to rule 133.1">133.1.</a> A card can have one or more Categories and Sub-Categories based on the properties of its front and back sides.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-133-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-133-2" aria-label="Link to rule 133.2">133.2.</a> These Categories and Sub-Categories dictate the behaviors of the card during play.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-133-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-133-3" aria-label="Link to rule 133.3">133.3.</a> Spells and other effects can refer to categories, sub-categories, supertypes, card types, tags, and other characteristics inclusively or exclusively.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A "non-unit card" is any card that is not a unit.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A "unit" is any game object that is a unit, regardless of any other categories it belongs to.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-133-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-133-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 133.3.a">133.3.a.</a> When characteristics are referred to in this way, it refers to any Game Object with the appropriate characteristic on the board, unless the zone is specified implicitly or explicitly by the effect doing the referring or the nature of the characteristic.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A "Yeti" refers to a game object with the Yeti tag on the board.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A "spell" refers to a spell on the chain, because spells cannot exist as game objects on the board.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A "spell in your trash" refers to a spell card in your trash because the effect specifies the zone in question.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-133-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-133-4" aria-label="Link to rule 133.4">133.4.</a> Main Deck Cards begin the game in the Main Deck or (in the case of a Chosen Champion) the Champion Zone.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-133-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-133-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 133.4.a">133.4.a.</a> Permanents</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-133-4-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-133-4-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 133.4.a.1">133.4.a.1.</a> An umbrella sub-category encompassing Main Deck Game Objects that remain on the board after being played.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-133-4-a-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-133-4-a-2" aria-label="Link to rule 133.4.a.2">133.4.a.2.</a> Unit and Gear are permanent types.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-133-4-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-133-4-b" aria-label="Link to rule 133.4.b">133.4.b.</a> Spells</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-133-4-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-133-4-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 133.4.b.1">133.4.b.1.</a> A sub-category encompassing Main Deck cards with the spell type, which do not remain on the board after being played.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-133-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-133-5" aria-label="Link to rule 133.5">133.5.</a> Rune Deck Cards begin the game in the Rune Deck.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-133-5-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-133-5-a" aria-label="Link to rule 133.5.a">133.5.a.</a> Runes</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-133-5-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-133-5-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 133.5.a.1">133.5.a.1.</a> A sub-category encompassing cards with the rune type. These are channeled rather than played. They remain on the board after being channeled, but they are not permanents, as they are not Main Deck cards.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-133-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-133-6" aria-label="Link to rule 133.6">133.6.</a> Other Cards are not part of either the Main or Rune Decks and begin the game in a zone determined by their type.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-133-6-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-133-6-a" aria-label="Link to rule 133.6.a">133.6.a.</a> Battlefields</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-133-6-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-133-6-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 133.6.a.1">133.6.a.1.</a> A sub-category encompassing cards with the battlefield type. They are not played or channeled. They start the game on the board.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-133-6-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-133-6-b" aria-label="Link to rule 133.6.b">133.6.b.</a> Legends</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-133-6-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-133-6-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 133.6.b.1">133.6.b.1.</a> A sub-category encompassing cards with the legend type. They are not played or channeled. They start the game in the Legend Zone and cannot typically leave it during play.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-133-7" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-133-7" aria-label="Link to rule 133.7">133.7.</a> Supertypes are Categories that may apply to game objects of multiple types. They are listed before a card’s type.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-133-7-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-133-7-a" aria-label="Link to rule 133.7.a">133.7.a.</a> Champion is a supertype that applies exclusively to units. It impacts the rules for deckbuilding.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-103-2-a">103.2.a</a>. Chosen Champion for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-133-7-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-133-7-b" aria-label="Link to rule 133.7.b">133.7.b.</a> Signature is a supertype that may apply to game objects of any card type. It impacts the rules for deckbuilding.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule 103.2.d. for more information about Signature cards.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-133-8" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-133-8" aria-label="Link to rule 133.8">133.8.</a> Tags are Categories that may apply to game objects of multiple types. They are listed after a card’s type.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-133-8-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-133-8-a" aria-label="Link to rule 133.8.a">133.8.a.</a> Tags have no innate rules meaning, but may be referenced by game rules and card effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-133-8-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-133-8-b" aria-label="Link to rule 133.8.b">133.8.b.</a> Tags used to link Legends, Champion Units, and Signature cards are known as Champion Tags.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-134" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-134" aria-label="Link to rule 134">134.</a> Domain</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-134-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-134-1" aria-label="Link to rule 134.1">134.1.</a> Most cards belong to one or more of six Domains, identified by one or more symbols in the lower right corner.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-134-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-134-2" aria-label="Link to rule 134.2">134.2.</a> Each Domain has an associated color, a unique symbol, and a shorthand used to represent that symbol in written text.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-134-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-134-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 134.2.a">134.2.a.</a> Fury is associated with the color red and represented by a circular symbol with three projecting points. Its shorthand is [R].</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-134-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-134-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 134.2.b">134.2.b.</a> Calm is associated with the color green and represented by a symbol resembling a leaf. Its shorthand is [G].</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-134-2-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-134-2-c" aria-label="Link to rule 134.2.c">134.2.c.</a> Mind is associated with the color blue and represented by a symbol resembling a sun and moon. Its shorthand is [B].</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-134-2-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-134-2-d" aria-label="Link to rule 134.2.d">134.2.d.</a> Body is associated with the color orange and represented by a blocky diamond-shaped symbol. Its shorthand is [O].</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-134-2-e" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-134-2-e" aria-label="Link to rule 134.2.e">134.2.e.</a> Chaos is associated with the color purple and represented by a hexagonal symbol with swirls emanating from its center. Its shorthand is [P].</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-134-2-f" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-134-2-f" aria-label="Link to rule 134.2.f">134.2.f.</a> Order is associated with the color yellow and represented by an angular winged symbol. Its shorthand is [Y].</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135" aria-label="Link to rule 135">135.</a> Rules Text</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-1" aria-label="Link to rule 135.1">135.1.</a> All cards have a section that describes how they affect the game. This section is known as the Rules Text of the card.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2">135.2.</a> This section may contain:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.a">135.2.a.</a> Abilities</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.a.1">135.2.a.1.</a> Abilities are discrete pieces of text that describe what a card may or must do.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-360">360</a>. Abilities for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-a-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-a-2" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.a.2">135.2.a.2.</a> They may appear on any card.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.b">135.2.b.</a> Instructions</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.b.1">135.2.b.1.</a> Instructions are a type of text that appears on spells and within abilities.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-b-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-b-2" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.b.2">135.2.b.2.</a> They describe actions to be performed by one or more players.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-b-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-b-3" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.b.3">135.2.b.3.</a> Instructions will self-describe when they are to be executed. If there is no timing described, they will execute during resolution.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: An instruction says “as you play me, kill a unit as an additional cost.” That instruction will execute as the card is being played. If the card is given Repeat and the Repeat cost is paid, this instruction will not execute because the Repeat execution does not happen until resolution of the spell, after this instruction has executed.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-b-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-b-4" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.b.4">135.2.b.4.</a> Instructions can usually be recognized by the way they are written. An instruction is written in the imperative mood, using the base form of a verb and omitting the subject unless necessary.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-b-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-b-5" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.b.5">135.2.b.5.</a> A single instruction is made up of a game action and its complement.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-b-5-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-b-5-a" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.b.5.a">135.2.b.5.a.</a> The complement of a game action is made up of the players or Game Objects that perform that game action, that that game action is performed on, the condition under which it is performed, and the duration for its effect to last. Some game actions omit a complement entirely.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Void Seeker is made up of two instructions. The first is “deal 4 to a unit at a battlefield.” Here “deal 4” is the game action, while “a unit at a battlefield,” is its complement. The second instruction, “draw 1,” has no complement.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Teemo, Strategist’s defend trigger reads “When I defend, choose an enemy unit here and reveal the top 5 cards of your Main Deck. Deal 1 to that unit for each card with Hidden revealed this way, then recycle the revealed cards.” There are four instructions here: “choose an enemy unit here,” “reveal the top 5 cards of your Main Deck,” “deal 1 to that unit for each card with Hidden revealed this way,” and “recycle the revealed cards.”</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-b-5-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-b-5-b" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.b.5.b">135.2.b.5.b.</a> If a conditional clause appears in instructions prior to one or more clauses with multiple instructions, that condition applies to all instructions in subsequent clauses in the same sentence.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A spell reads in part “If you’ve gained a point this turn, draw 1, then discard 1.” In order for either of the instructions that follow the “If you’ve gained a point this turn,” conditional clause to execute, the condition must be true.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Rogue Assassin is a legend that reads in part “[Action][&gt;] [E]: If it's your turn, move a friendly unit in a showdown to base and if I'm [Empowered], ready it.” The first conditional clause in the activated ability applies to all subsequent instructions in the same sentence. The second conditional clause, “if I’m [Empowered],” only applies to the instruction that immediately follows it.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-b-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-b-6" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.b.6">135.2.b.6.</a> Instructions do not include trigger conditions or the events or actions that a replacement effect defines as being eligible to be replaced.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: An ability reads “When you play me, draw 1.” The trigger condition for this ability is not an instruction. “Draw 1,” is the only instruction included in the ability.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: An ability reads “If a unit would die, banish it instead.” The text “If a unit would die,” is not an instruction of the ability.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: An ability reads “As I’m revealed from your Main Deck, Add [2].” The text “As I’m revealed from your Main Deck,” is not an instruction of the ability.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-b-7" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-b-7" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.b.7">135.2.b.7.</a> Instructions of a spell or ability may reference actions taken during Finalization. They will do so by checking whether “you” performed those game actions. These instructions check whether the game action was performed, not by whom. If another player gains control of the spell or ability after the game action was performed during finalization, it will still be considered true for the purposes of these instructions.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Rampage is a spell that reads “As you play this, you may pay [C] as an additional cost.” and “Choose a friendly unit and an enemy unit. If you paid the additional cost, give the friendly unit +2 [Might] this turn. They deal damage equal to their Mights to each other.” If another player gains control of Rampage, although they are not the one who paid the cost, the “if you paid the additional cost,” condition will still be fulfilled.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Back Off is a spell that reads in part “If you played this from your hand, draw 1.” On resolution, Back Off checks if it was played from hand. If another player gained control of Back Off, as long as the card was played from hand, the condition will be true and the controller of the spell will draw 1.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-c" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.c">135.2.c.</a> Keywords</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-c-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-c-1" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.c.1">135.2.c.1.</a> Keywords are short words or phrases that represent longer abilities or instructions.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-800">800</a>. Keywords for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-c-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-c-2" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.c.2">135.2.c.2.</a> Keywords may or may not have reminder text.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-d" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.d">135.2.d.</a> Reminder Text</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-d-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-d-1" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.d.1">135.2.d.1.</a> Reminder text appears in italics and parentheses.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-d-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-d-2" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.d.2">135.2.d.2.</a> Its purpose is to summarize relevant rules, including keywords and other rules.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-d-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-d-3" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.d.3">135.2.d.3.</a> The presence, absence, or exact wording of reminder text has no effect on game function.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-e" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-e" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.e">135.2.e.</a> Symbols</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-e-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-e-1" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.e.1">135.2.e.1.</a> Rules text sometimes contains symbols that express game concepts. Each symbol also has a shorthand used to represent it in written text.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-e-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-e-2" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.e.2">135.2.e.2.</a> The cost of Exhausting a Permanent is represented by the “exhaust symbol,” which resembles a card turning sideways. Its shorthand is [E]. (Some previous rules information has given its shorthand as [T].)</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-e-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-e-3" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.e.3">135.2.e.3.</a> Might is represented by the “Might symbol,” which resembles a sword and shield. Its shorthand is [M]. (Some previous rules information has given its shorthand as [S].)</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-e-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-e-4" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.e.4">135.2.e.4.</a> Power of a specific Domain is represented by its domain symbol and the corresponding shorthand.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-134">134</a>. Domain for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-e-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-e-5" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.e.5">135.2.e.5.</a> Power of any Domain is represented by a swirling rainbow symbol. Its shorthand is [A].</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-e-5-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-e-5-a" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.e.5.a">135.2.e.5.a.</a> When required as a cost, [A] can be paid by Power of any Domain.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-e-5-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-e-5-b" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.e.5.b">135.2.e.5.b.</a> When Added to a player’s Rune Pool, [A] can be spent to pay a Power cost of any Domain.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-e-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-e-6" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.e.6">135.2.e.6.</a> Power of a domain corresponding to a card’s own Domain is not represented by a symbol, but appears in these rules similarly to other symbols. Its shorthand is [C].</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-e-6-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-e-6-a" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.e.6.a">135.2.e.6.a.</a> When a [C] shorthand is printed on a card as reminder text, it assumes that card’s usual Domain, but it still means “one Power of this card’s Domain.”</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-e-6-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-e-6-b" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.e.6.b">135.2.e.6.b.</a> A [C] shorthand on a card with no Domain is processed as [A] instead.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-e-6-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-e-6-c" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.e.6.c">135.2.e.6.c.</a> A [C] shorthand on a card with multiple Domains is processed as any power of that card’s Domains.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Defiant Dance is a Calm and Chaos spell. Its cost is [1][C]. The power component of that cost can be paid with either [G] or [P].</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Tibbers is a Fury and Chaos Unit. If Tibbers is granted the Accelerate keyword, the power component of the optional additional cost can be paid with either [R] or [P].</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-e-7" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-e-7" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.e.7">135.2.e.7.</a> A keyword associated with an ability is indicated by the [&gt;] symbol.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-e-7-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-e-7-a" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.e.7.a">135.2.e.7.a.</a> This could be a dependent keyword like [Legion][&gt;] or [Level][&gt;], a triggered ability with a keyworded trigger condition like [Deathknell] or it could be a permissive keyword associated with an ability like [Action][&gt;] or [Reaction][&gt;].</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-2-e-7-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-2-e-7-b" aria-label="Link to rule 135.2.e.7.b">135.2.e.7.b.</a> Whatever instruction or ability comes after the [&gt;] is modified by the keyword that comes before the [&gt;]. It will either be inactive, in the case of a dependent keyword; the trigger effect, in the case of a keyword triggered ability; or it will receive the permission granted by the keyword in the case of a permissive keyword.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-3" aria-label="Link to rule 135.3">135.3.</a> Rules text can be blank.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-4" aria-label="Link to rule 135.4">135.4.</a> A card’s printed Rules Text is Inactive while that card is Attached to another card.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-716">716</a>. Attachment for more information.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-720">720</a>. Inactive for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 135.4.a">135.4.a.</a> The presence of text, rules, Keywords, and other effects can still be referenced and detected by other game effects, even while Rules Text is Inactive.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-135-4-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-135-4-b" aria-label="Link to rule 135.4.b">135.4.b.</a> Any granted or appended Rules Text on a card is still Active even if that card is Attached to another card.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-136" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-136" aria-label="Link to rule 136">136.</a> Effect Text</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-136-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-136-1" aria-label="Link to rule 136.1">136.1.</a> Some cards have a separate section of text below the Rules Text. This is referred to as the Effect Text.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-136-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-136-2" aria-label="Link to rule 136.2">136.2.</a> Effect Text can contain additional Abilities.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-136-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-136-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 136.2.a">136.2.a.</a> Effect Text can be blank.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-136-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-136-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 136.2.b">136.2.b.</a> Effect Text is inactive unless the card with the Effect Text is Attached to another card.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-716">716</a>. Attachment for more information.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-720">720</a>. Inactive for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-136-2-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
