<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-136-2-c" aria-label="Link to rule 136.2.c">136.2.c.</a> The abilities in the Effect Text section of a card are appended to the Rules Text of the card to which the card with the Effect Text is Attached.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-136-2-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-136-2-d" aria-label="Link to rule 136.2.d">136.2.d.</a> Effect Text may refer to “this” or to the name of the Attached game object that appended the Effect Text. Doing so refers to the Attached game object and not the Top-Most Card, even if the Top-Most Card shares a name with the Attached game object.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Guardian Angel’s effect text reads “If I would die, kill Guardian Angel instead. Heal me, exhaust me, and recall me.”</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Brutalizer’s effect text reads “If this was attached to me this turn, I have an additional +2 [M].” The “this” in the effect text refers to the attached Brutalizer.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-137" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-137" aria-label="Link to rule 137">137.</a> Might Bonus</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-137-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-137-1" aria-label="Link to rule 137.1">137.1.</a> Some cards have a Might Bonus in their lower right corner, expressed as an operator plus an integer.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-137-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-137-2" aria-label="Link to rule 137.2">137.2.</a> A card’s Might Bonus can be +0.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-137-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-137-3" aria-label="Link to rule 137.3">137.3.</a> A card’s Might Bonus modulates the Might of the card to which the card that has the Might Bonus is Attached.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-716">716</a>. Attachment for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-137-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-137-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 137.3.a">137.3.a.</a> The Might Bonus is applied while Attached and stops applying as soon as the card with the Might Bonus is no longer Attached.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-137-3-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-137-3-b" aria-label="Link to rule 137.3.b">137.3.b.</a> If the card to which a card with a Might Bonus is Attached has no Might value, the Might Bonus is ignored.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-138" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-138" aria-label="Link to rule 138">138.</a> Flavor Text</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-138-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-138-1" aria-label="Link to rule 138.1">138.1.</a> Flavor text is text that appears at the bottom of some cards to provide aesthetic enjoyment. It conveys no gameplay information.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: The card Void Seeker features a quote from the character Kai'Sa. It does not have the Kai'Sa tag and has no gameplay connection to the Champion unit Kai'Sa.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-138-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-138-2" aria-label="Link to rule 138.2">138.2.</a> Flavor text is located at the bottom of the section provided for rules text, usually in a shaded bar. It appears in italics.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-139" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-139" aria-label="Link to rule 139">139.</a> Illustration</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-139-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-139-1" aria-label="Link to rule 139.1">139.1.</a> Each card has an illustration. It conveys no gameplay information.</p>
</div>

### 140. Units {#rule-140}


<div id="rule-141" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-141" aria-label="Link to rule 141">141.</a> Unit is:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-141-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-141-1" aria-label="Link to rule 141.1">141.1.</a> A Game Object</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-141-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-141-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 141.1.a">141.1.a.</a> While on the Board:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-141-1-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-141-1-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 141.1.a.1">141.1.a.1.</a> Units are at one of several Locations while on the Board: a Battlefield or their Base.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-141-1-a-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-141-1-a-2" aria-label="Link to rule 141.1.a.2">141.1.a.2.</a> Units and their details are Public Information while on the Board.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-141-1-a-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-141-1-a-3" aria-label="Link to rule 141.1.a.3">141.1.a.3.</a> Units can be chosen, affected, or manipulated by spells, affects, or game actions that specify Units.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-141-1-a-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-141-1-a-4" aria-label="Link to rule 141.1.a.4">141.1.a.4.</a> Units can be Killed.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-429">429</a>. Kill for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-141-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-141-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 141.1.b">141.1.b.</a> While in the Trash:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-141-1-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-141-1-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 141.1.b.1">141.1.b.1.</a> Units are treated as Cards, similar to when in the Hand.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-141-1-b-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-141-1-b-2" aria-label="Link to rule 141.1.b.2">141.1.b.2.</a> They retain the properties of being a Unit, but are not on the Board and thus cannot take actions or be affected by spells, abilities, or game actions that target Units on the Board.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-141-1-b-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-141-1-b-3" aria-label="Link to rule 141.1.b.3">141.1.b.3.</a> Units can be affected by spells and game effects that target Units in the Trash.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-141-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-141-2" aria-label="Link to rule 141.2">141.2.</a> A Card Type</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-141-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-141-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 141.2.a">141.2.a.</a> This is a unique identifier that some spells or abilities will use to restrict what they can choose or affect.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-141-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-141-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 141.2.b">141.2.b.</a> The card type is relevant in all zones.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-142" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-142" aria-label="Link to rule 142">142.</a> Damage is a marked value that is applied to Units.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-142-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-142-1" aria-label="Link to rule 142.1">142.1.</a> Damage is not a Game Object.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-142-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-142-2" aria-label="Link to rule 142.2">142.2.</a> Damage is a value tracked per-Unit.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-142-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-142-3" aria-label="Link to rule 142.3">142.3.</a> Damage is marked on Units by players.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-142-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-142-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 142.3.a">142.3.a.</a> The player responsible for the Deal action that caused the Damage to be marked is the player who marked that Damage.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-142-3-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-142-3-b" aria-label="Link to rule 142.3.b">142.3.b.</a> Game Effects may refer to that player’s Damage. This means the Damage marked by that player.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A unit reads in part “Your damage can’t be prevented.” This refers to the damage marked by that player.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-142-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-142-4" aria-label="Link to rule 142.4">142.4.</a> Damage tracks how close a Unit is to being Killed.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-429">429</a>. Kill for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-142-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-142-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 142.4.a">142.4.a.</a> Lethal Damage is the amount of marked Damage that will cause a unit to die in a cleanup.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-142-4-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-142-4-b" aria-label="Link to rule 142.4.b">142.4.b.</a> Lethal Damage for a Unit is a non-zero amount greater than or equal to that Unit’s Might.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A unit has 5 [M] and 3 damage marked on it. Frigid Touch is played targeting that unit. When it resolves, the unit’s Might becomes 3, and it will have lethal damage marked on it.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A unit has 0 [M]. In order to have lethal damage marked on it, it must have at least 1 damage marked on it.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-142-4-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-142-4-c" aria-label="Link to rule 142.4.c">142.4.c.</a> Some effects may alter this amount. These effects will refer to the amount of damage needed to kill a unit.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Elder Dragon’s passive ability reads “Any amount of your damage is enough to kill enemy units.” This alters the Lethal Damage value for enemy units that have damage marked by you.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-142-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-142-5" aria-label="Link to rule 142.5">142.5.</a> Damage can be Healed.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-419">419</a>. Heal for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-143" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-143" aria-label="Link to rule 143">143.</a> Units have multiple Intrinsic Properties unique to them:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-143-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-143-1" aria-label="Link to rule 143.1">143.1.</a> Tag: A Unit has zero or more Tags representing one or more champions, regions, factions, or species it belongs to.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-143-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-143-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 143.1.a">143.1.a.</a> These have no intrinsic rules or behaviors by themselves.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-143-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-143-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 143.1.b">143.1.b.</a> Spells, abilities, and game actions can reference these types as part of their execution.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-143-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-143-2" aria-label="Link to rule 143.2">143.2.</a> Might: The combat statistic of a Unit. Used to determine a Unit's contribution to Combat, as well as when it is Killed by damaging effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-143-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-143-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 143.2.a">143.2.a.</a> A Unit’s Might can be referenced by cards and effects in one of three forms: A Unit has a Printed Might, that is the value in the top right corner of the unit card/token; a Base Might, that is a trait that is alterable by effects in the Trait-Altering layer; and an Effective Might, that is the value calculated in the arithmetic layer including all Might altering Game Effects.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-475">475</a>. Layers for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-143-2-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-143-2-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 143.2.a.1">143.2.a.1.</a> Card text may refer to the latter two of these three forms. When cards or these rules refer to a Unit’s Effective Might, they will do so by simply referring to that unit’s “Might.”</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A card reads in part “Choose a unit. Its base Might becomes 5 this turn.” This applies an effect in the trait-altering layer.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A card reads in part “Choose a unit. Increase its Might to 5 this turn.” This applies an effect in the arithmetic layer.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-143-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-143-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 143.2.b">143.2.b.</a> If a Unit ever has nonzero damage marked on it equalling or exceeding its Might, it is Killed.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-143-2-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-143-2-c" aria-label="Link to rule 143.2.c">143.2.c.</a> If a unit's Might is ever less than 0, it is treated as 0 when referenced by spells and abilities, and when summing Might to be assigned as damage in the Combat Damage Step.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-467">467</a>. The Combat Damage Step for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-143-2-c-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-143-2-c-1" aria-label="Link to rule 143.2.c.1">143.2.c.1.</a> Although the unit’s Might is treated as 0, it is not 0. Effects that calculate Might increases and decreases use the actual value of the unit’s Might.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-143-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-143-3" aria-label="Link to rule 143.3">143.3.</a> Units can have damage marked on them.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-143-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-143-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 143.3.a">143.3.a.</a> When spells, abilities, or other game effects deal damage, Units mark that damage on them temporarily. This can be tracked with coins, dice, or other markers, or by memory.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-143-3-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-143-3-b" aria-label="Link to rule 143.3.b">143.3.b.</a> Damage is Healed from Units at two specific times:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-143-3-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-143-3-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 143.3.b.1">143.3.b.1.</a> At the end of each player's turn.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-317-2">317.2</a>. Ending Phase for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-143-3-b-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-143-3-b-2" aria-label="Link to rule 143.3.b.2">143.3.b.2.</a> During a Combat Cleanup.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-468-1">468.1</a>. for more information about Combat Cleanups.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-143-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-143-4" aria-label="Link to rule 143.4">143.4.</a> Units enter the Board exhausted.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-143-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-143-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 143.4.a">143.4.a.</a> This can be altered by Accelerate or similar game effects.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-805">805</a>. Accelerate for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-143-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-143-5" aria-label="Link to rule 143.5">143.5.</a> Units cannot be Attached to other permanents.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-435">435</a>. Attach for more information on the attach game action.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-716">716</a>. Attachment for more information on attachment.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-143-5-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-143-5-a" aria-label="Link to rule 143.5.a">143.5.a.</a> If a Unit would be Attached to a permanent, nothing happens.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-143-5-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-143-5-b" aria-label="Link to rule 143.5.b">143.5.b.</a> If a non-Unit Game Object that is Attached becomes a Unit through any means, it detaches as it becomes a Unit.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-144" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-144" aria-label="Link to rule 144">144.</a> Units have the Inherent Ability to perform a Standard Move.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-144-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-144-1" aria-label="Link to rule 144.1">144.1.</a> This action is limited in when it can be performed.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-144-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-144-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 144.1.a">144.1.a.</a> This action can be done any time during a player's Main Phase.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-144-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-144-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 144.1.b">144.1.b.</a> This action cannot be performed during a Closed State.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-144-1-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-144-1-c" aria-label="Link to rule 144.1.c">144.1.c.</a> This action cannot be performed during a Showdown or Combat.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-144-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-144-2" aria-label="Link to rule 144.2">144.2.</a> Exhausting the Unit is the Cost for this action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-144-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-144-3" aria-label="Link to rule 144.3">144.3.</a> Players may perform multiple Units' standard move simultaneously. This is treated as one game action performed on multiple Units.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-144-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-144-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 144.3.a">144.3.a.</a> When a Move like this is declared by a player, the units' Destination must be the same.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-144-3-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-144-3-b" aria-label="Link to rule 144.3.b">144.3.b.</a> When a Move like this is declared by a player, the Origins do not need to be the same.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-144-3-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-144-3-c" aria-label="Link to rule 144.3.c">144.3.c.</a> The Costs of Exhausting the Units are also paid Simultaneously.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-144-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-144-4" aria-label="Link to rule 144.4">144.4.</a> The Destinations where Units can Move to with their Standard Move are restricted:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-144-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-144-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 144.4.a">144.4.a.</a> Units may move from their Base to a Battlefield.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-144-4-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-144-4-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 144.4.a.1">144.4.a.1.</a> Units cannot Move to a Battlefield that already has units from 2 other players present, or where a Combat is ongoing that has 2 other players as participants.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-449-2">449.2</a>. For more information on valid destinations for movement.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-144-4-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-144-4-b" aria-label="Link to rule 144.4.b">144.4.b.</a> Units may move from a Battlefield to their Base.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-144-4-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-144-4-c" aria-label="Link to rule 144.4.c">144.4.c.</a> Ganking is a unique ability that affects a Unit's Standard Move</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-144-4-c-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-144-4-c-1" aria-label="Link to rule 144.4.c.1">144.4.c.1.</a> Units with Ganking may use their Standard Move to Move from Battlefield to Battlefield.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-810">810</a>. Ganking for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-145" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-145" aria-label="Link to rule 145">145.</a> Units may have Activated Abilities.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-145-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-145-1" aria-label="Link to rule 145.1">145.1.</a> Activated Abilities are Game Effects that are written as Costs followed by a ":", and then succeeded by an effect.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-376">376</a>. Activated Abilities for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-145-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-145-2" aria-label="Link to rule 145.2">145.2.</a> The Activated Ability of Units may be executed at any time during the controlling player's Main Phase during an Open State, and not during a Showdown.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-145-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-145-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 145.2.a">145.2.a.</a> This follows the same process as playing a card.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-349">349</a>. Playing Cards for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-145-2-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-145-2-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 145.2.a.1">145.2.a.1.</a> This behaves, once activated, like a spell without an associated card.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-146" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-146" aria-label="Link to rule 146">146.</a> Units have a Location.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-146-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-146-1" aria-label="Link to rule 146.1">146.1.</a> A Unit’s Location is the Base or Battlefield it currently occupies.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-197">197</a>. Locations for more information.</p>
</div>

### 147. Gear {#rule-147}


<div id="rule-148" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-148" aria-label="Link to rule 148">148.</a> Gear are:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-148-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-148-1" aria-label="Link to rule 148.1">148.1.</a> A Game Object</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-148-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-148-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 148.1.a">148.1.a.</a> While on the Board.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-148-1-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-148-1-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 148.1.a.1">148.1.a.1.</a> Gear can only be played to a player's Base unless an effect specifies otherwise.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-148-1-a-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-148-1-a-2" aria-label="Link to rule 148.1.a.2">148.1.a.2.</a> Gear and their details are Public Information while on the Board.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-148-1-a-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-148-1-a-3" aria-label="Link to rule 148.1.a.3">148.1.a.3.</a> Gear can be chosen, affected, or manipulated by spells, effects, or game actions that specify Gear.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-148-1-a-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-148-1-a-4" aria-label="Link to rule 148.1.a.4">148.1.a.4.</a> Gear can be Killed.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-429">429</a>. Kill for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-148-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-148-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 148.1.b">148.1.b.</a> While in the Trash</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-148-1-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-148-1-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 148.1.b.1">148.1.b.1.</a> Gear are treated as Cards before Game Objects, similar to the Hand.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-148-1-b-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-148-1-b-2" aria-label="Link to rule 148.1.b.2">148.1.b.2.</a> They retain the properties of being Gear, but are not on the Board and thus cannot take actions or be affected by spells, abilities, or game actions that affect Gear on the Board.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-148-1-b-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-148-1-b-3" aria-label="Link to rule 148.1.b.3">148.1.b.3.</a> Gear can be affected by spells and game effects that affect Gear in the Trash.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-148-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-148-2" aria-label="Link to rule 148.2">148.2.</a> A Card Type</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-148-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-148-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 148.2.a">148.2.a.</a> This is a unique identifier that some spells or abilities will use to restrict what they can choose or affect.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-148-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-148-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 148.2.b">148.2.b.</a> The card type is relevant in all zones.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-149" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-149" aria-label="Link to rule 149">149.</a> Gear have several Intrinsic Properties unique to them.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-149-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-149-1" aria-label="Link to rule 149.1">149.1.</a> Gear enter play Ready.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-149-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-149-2" aria-label="Link to rule 149.2">149.2.</a> Gear can only be played to a player's Base unless an effect specifies otherwise.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-149-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-149-3" aria-label="Link to rule 149.3">149.3.</a> If an unattached non-Unit Gear is at a Battlefield or in another player’s base for any reason during a cleanup, then it is recalled to its controller's Base as a corrective action.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-456">456</a>. Recalls for more information.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-318">318</a>. Cleanups for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-150" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-150" aria-label="Link to rule 150">150.</a> Gear can have the Equipment tag.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-150-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-150-1" aria-label="Link to rule 150.1">150.1.</a> These Gear are referred to as Equipment.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-150-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-150-2" aria-label="Link to rule 150.2">150.2.</a> Equipment have effect text and a Might bonus.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-136">136</a>. Effect Text for more information on effect text.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-137">137</a>. Might Bonus for more information on Might bonuses.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-150-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-150-3" aria-label="Link to rule 150.3">150.3.</a> Equipment can have the Equip or Quick-Draw keywords.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-818">818</a>. Equip for more information on the Equip keyword.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-819">819</a>. Quick-Draw for more information on the Quick-Draw keyword.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-150-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-150-4" aria-label="Link to rule 150.4">150.4.</a> Equipment are still Gear and have the same intrinsic properties and rules that Gear do.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-150-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-150-5" aria-label="Link to rule 150.5">150.5.</a> Equipment and whether a Gear has the Equipment tag is a characteristic of the Gear may be checked or referenced by other Game Effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-151" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-151" aria-label="Link to rule 151">151.</a> Gear may have Activated Abilities.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-151-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-151-1" aria-label="Link to rule 151.1">151.1.</a> Activated Abilities are Game Effects that are written as Costs followed by a ":", and then succeeded by an effect.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-376">376</a>. Activated Abilities for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-151-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-151-2" aria-label="Link to rule 151.2">151.2.</a> The Activated Ability of Gear may be executed at any time during the controlling player's Main Phase during an Open State, and not during a Showdown.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-151-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-151-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 151.2.a">151.2.a.</a> This follows the same process as playing a card.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-349">349</a>. Playing Cards for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-151-2-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-151-2-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 151.2.a.1">151.2.a.1.</a> This behaves, once activated, like a spell without an associated card.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-152" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-152" aria-label="Link to rule 152">152.</a> Gear have a Location.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-152-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-152-1" aria-label="Link to rule 152.1">152.1.</a> A Gear’s Location is the Base or Battlefield it currently occupies.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-197">197</a>. Locations for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-152-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-152-2" aria-label="Link to rule 152.2">152.2.</a> Non-Unit Gear cannot normally become located at a Battlefield unless by some special means.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Gear are played to Base unless an effect specifies otherwise. A gear played from Facedown, which is specified to be played to the Battlefield it was played from, will enter at that Battlefield. Gear can also become located at a Battlefield if they are attached to a unit who becomes located at a Battlefield. A Gear that is a Unit can move to a Battlefield.</p></blockquote>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-811">811</a>. Hidden for more information.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-716">716</a>. Attachment for more information.</p>
</div>

### 153. Spells {#rule-153}


<div id="rule-154" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-154" aria-label="Link to rule 154">154.</a> Spell is a card type.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-155" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-155" aria-label="Link to rule 155">155.</a> A spell can be played during an Open State outside of Showdowns on its controller's turn.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-156" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-156" aria-label="Link to rule 156">156.</a> A spell is controlled by the player who played it.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-157" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-157" aria-label="Link to rule 157">157.</a> A spell creates a game effect according to its instructions and is then placed in the Trash of the player who owns it.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-158" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-158" aria-label="Link to rule 158">158.</a> When a spell is successfully played, a player executes the rules text of the spell. This is called Resolving the spell.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-158-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-158-1" aria-label="Link to rule 158.1">158.1.</a> Spells have their rules text executed from top to bottom when they are Resolved.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-158-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-158-2" aria-label="Link to rule 158.2">158.2.</a> If a later part of a spell applies a Replacement Effect that alters earlier parts of the spell, apply those replacement effects as appropriate.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A spell says "Choose a unit. Kill it the next time it takes damage this turn." and "[Legion] — Kill it now instead." If the Legion condition is satisfied, the unit is killed immediately and the instruction to kill it the next time it takes damage is negated, even if the unit remains on the board somehow.</p></blockquote>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-367">367</a>. Replacement Effects for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-158-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-158-3" aria-label="Link to rule 158.3">158.3.</a> While a spell or ability on the chain is Resolving, no other spells or abilities can be finalized on the chain or resolved, including triggered abilities or game effects that would occur as a result of the execution of the spell.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-158-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-158-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 158.3.a">158.3.a.</a> Finish resolving all effects of a spell before addressing any chain items or Tasks the spell may have added or made outstanding through execution.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-159" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-159" aria-label="Link to rule 159">159.</a> Certain Keywords on spells are not executable rules text, but instead intrinsic properties of the spell.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-159-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-159-1" aria-label="Link to rule 159.1">159.1.</a> These determine inherent properties and behaviors of the spell before being played or while on the chain.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-159-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-159-2" aria-label="Link to rule 159.2">159.2.</a> These keywords are:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-159-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-159-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 159.2.a">159.2.a.</a> Action</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-159-2-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-159-2-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 159.2.a.1">159.2.a.1.</a> This indicates that, in addition to being able to be played during an Open State, this spell may also be played during Open States during Showdowns.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-806">806</a>. Action for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-159-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-159-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 159.2.b">159.2.b.</a> Reaction</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-159-2-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-159-2-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 159.2.b.1">159.2.b.1.</a> Grants all cases and rules of Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-159-2-b-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-159-2-b-2" aria-label="Link to rule 159.2.b.2">159.2.b.2.</a> In addition to all prior cases, may also be played during all forms of Closed State.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-159-2-b-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-159-2-b-3" aria-label="Link to rule 159.2.b.3">159.2.b.3.</a> This will result in this spell resolving before spells and abilities that are already on the chain.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-813">813</a>. Reaction for more information.</p>
</div>

### 160. Runes {#rule-160}


<div id="rule-161" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-161" aria-label="Link to rule 161">161.</a> Rune is a Card Type.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-161-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-161-1" aria-label="Link to rule 161.1">161.1.</a> A Rune is not a Main Deck card.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-161-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-161-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 161.1.a">161.1.a.</a> This means, despite remaining on the Board until Recycled or otherwise removed from the board, it is not a Permanent.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-161-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-161-2" aria-label="Link to rule 161.2">161.2.</a> Runes are kept in the Rune Deck.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-161-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-161-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 161.2.a">161.2.a.</a> Exactly 12 Rune cards chosen during Deck Construction.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-103-3">103.3</a>. Rune Deck for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-161-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-161-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 161.2.b">161.2.b.</a> When a Rune is Recycled it is returned to the Rune Deck, not the Main Deck.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-417">417</a>. Recycle for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-162" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-162" aria-label="Link to rule 162">162.</a> Runes produce the resources needed to pay costs.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-163" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-163" aria-label="Link to rule 163">163.</a> Runes produce Energy and Power.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-163-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-163-1" aria-label="Link to rule 163.1">163.1.</a> Energy is used to pay numeric Energy costs.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-163-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-163-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 163.1.a">163.1.a.</a> Energy has no Domain.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-163-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-163-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 163.1.b">163.1.b.</a> Energy has no type.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-163-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-163-2" aria-label="Link to rule 163.2">163.2.</a> Power is used to pay Domain-associated Power Costs.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-163-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-163-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 163.2.a">163.2.a.</a> Power has a Domain.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-163-2-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-163-2-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 163.2.a.1">163.2.a.1.</a> Power's Domain usually corresponds to the Domain of the Rune that produced it.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-163-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-163-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 163.2.b">163.2.b.</a> Some Power is Universal and can be used to pay for costs of any Domain.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-164" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-164" aria-label="Link to rule 164">164.</a> Basic Runes</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-164-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-164-1" aria-label="Link to rule 164.1">164.1.</a> There are six Basic Runes, each with a Domain corresponding to its name: Fury Rune Calm Rune Mind Rune Body Rune Chaos Rune Order Rune</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-164-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-164-2" aria-label="Link to rule 164.2">164.2.</a> A Basic Rune always has the following two Abilities:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-164-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-164-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 164.2.a">164.2.a.</a> [Reaction][&gt;] [E]: Add [1].</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-164-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-164-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 164.2.b">164.2.b.</a> [Reaction][&gt;] Recycle this: Add [C].</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-164-2-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-164-2-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 164.2.b.1">164.2.b.1.</a> The Power added this way corresponds to the Domain of the Rune that is being Recycled.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-165" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-165" aria-label="Link to rule 165">165.</a> Rune Pools</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-166" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-166" aria-label="Link to rule 166">166.</a> The Rune Pool is a conceptual collection of a player's available Energy and Power available to pay Costs.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-166-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-166-1" aria-label="Link to rule 166.1">166.1.</a> When a card adds Energy or Power, it is added to the controlling player's Rune Pool.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-166-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-166-2" aria-label="Link to rule 166.2">166.2.</a> Players must add Energy and Power to their Rune Pool in order to be able to spend it to play cards or pay for Abilities with costs.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-166-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-166-3" aria-label="Link to rule 166.3">166.3.</a> Energy and Power do not have a physical marker or tracker, although players may wish to use a physical tracker if they are retaining unspent Energy and Power over the course of their turn.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-167" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-167" aria-label="Link to rule 167">167.</a> Every player's Rune Pool empties at the start of each player's Main Phase and the end of each player's turn.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-167-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-167-1" aria-label="Link to rule 167.1">167.1.</a> Any unspent Energy or Power are lost.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-168" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-168" aria-label="Link to rule 168">168.</a> All Abilities that include the action "Add" are abilities that are adding Energy or Power to the Rune Pool.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-430">430</a>. Add for more information.</p>
</div>

### 169. Battlefields {#rule-169}


<div id="rule-170" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-170" aria-label="Link to rule 170">170.</a> Battlefields are Game Objects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-170-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-170-1" aria-label="Link to rule 170.1">170.1.</a> Battlefields are Owned by a player.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-170-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-170-2" aria-label="Link to rule 170.2">170.2.</a> Battlefields are not shuffled into Decks at the start of a game.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-170-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-170-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 170.2.a">170.2.a.</a> Battlefields are not played during the course of regular play.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-170-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-170-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 170.2.b">170.2.b.</a> Battlefields are established at the start of a game and remain in place for the duration of regular play.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-170-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-170-3" aria-label="Link to rule 170.3">170.3.</a> Battlefields cannot be Killed during the course of regular play.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-170-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-170-4" aria-label="Link to rule 170.4">170.4.</a> Battlefields cannot be Moved.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-170-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-170-5" aria-label="Link to rule 170.5">170.5.</a> Battlefields are Locations.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-170-5-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-170-5-a" aria-label="Link to rule 170.5.a">170.5.a.</a> Battlefields can be Origins of moves.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-170-5-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-170-5-b" aria-label="Link to rule 170.5.b">170.5.b.</a> Battlefields can be Destinations of moves.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-170-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-170-6" aria-label="Link to rule 170.6">170.6.</a> Any number of Units can be present at a Battlefield.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-170-7" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-170-7" aria-label="Link to rule 170.7">170.7.</a> Battlefields can be targeted by spells or game effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-170-8" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-170-8" aria-label="Link to rule 170.8">170.8.</a> Battlefields can have Passive Abilities.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-363">363</a>. Passive Abilities for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-170-9" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-170-9" aria-label="Link to rule 170.9">170.9.</a> Battlefields can have Triggered Abilities.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-382">382</a>. Triggered Abilities for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-170-10" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-170-10" aria-label="Link to rule 170.10">170.10.</a> Battlefields can have Activated Abilities.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-376">376</a>. Activated Abilities for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-170-11" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-170-11" aria-label="Link to rule 170.11">170.11.</a> Battlefields can be referenced in different states in card text:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-170-11-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-170-11-a" aria-label="Link to rule 170.11.a">170.11.a.</a> Battlefields can be “occupied.” This means they have a Unit present.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-170-11-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-170-11-b" aria-label="Link to rule 170.11.b">170.11.b.</a> Battlefields can be “uncontrolled.” This means no player controls them.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-170-11-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-170-11-c" aria-label="Link to rule 170.11.c">170.11.c.</a> Battlefields can be “open.” This means they are unoccupied and uncontrolled.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-171" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-171" aria-label="Link to rule 171">171.</a> Battlefields are not Permanents.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-172" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-172" aria-label="Link to rule 172">172.</a> The number of Battlefields on the Board is determined by the Mode of Play.</p>
</div>

### 173. Legends {#rule-173}


<div id="rule-174" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-174" aria-label="Link to rule 174">174.</a> Legends are Game Objects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-174-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-174-1" aria-label="Link to rule 174.1">174.1.</a> Legends are Owned by a player.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-174-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-174-2" aria-label="Link to rule 174.2">174.2.</a> Legends are not shuffled into Decks at the start of a game.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-174-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-174-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 174.2.a">174.2.a.</a> Legends are not played during the course of regular play.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-174-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-174-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 174.2.b">174.2.b.</a> Legends are established at the start of a game, and remain in place for the duration of regular play. Legends may be Created during the course of play. They will be Created ready unless the effect specifies otherwise.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-174-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-174-3" aria-label="Link to rule 174.3">174.3.</a> Legends cannot be Killed during the course of regular play.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-174-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-174-4" aria-label="Link to rule 174.4">174.4.</a> Legends cannot be Moved.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-174-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-174-5" aria-label="Link to rule 174.5">174.5.</a> Legends can be targeted by spells or game effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-174-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-174-6" aria-label="Link to rule 174.6">174.6.</a> Legends can have Passive Abilities.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-363">363</a>. Passive Abilities for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-174-7" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-174-7" aria-label="Link to rule 174.7">174.7.</a> Legends can have Triggered Abilities.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-382">382</a>. Triggered Abilities for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-174-8" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-174-8" aria-label="Link to rule 174.8">174.8.</a> Legends can have Activated Abilities.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-376">376</a>. Activated Abilities for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-175" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-175" aria-label="Link to rule 175">175.</a> Legends are not Permanents.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-176" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-176" aria-label="Link to rule 176">176.</a> Legends may have one or more Domains.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-176-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-176-1" aria-label="Link to rule 176.1">176.1.</a> The Champion Legend determines the Domain Identity of cards its owner can include.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-101">101</a>. Deck Construction for more information.</p>
</div>

### 177. Multiple Types {#rule-177}


<div id="rule-178" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-178" aria-label="Link to rule 178">178.</a> Game Objects can have multiple types.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-178-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-178-1" aria-label="Link to rule 178.1">178.1.</a> A Game Object that is more than one type has the properties of all of their types, except where they are mutually exclusive.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-178-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-178-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 178.1.a">178.1.a.</a> Regardless of a Game Objects’ other types, there are some properties that are true of Game Objects of certain types. These properties take precedence when properties are mutually exclusive.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-178-1-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-178-1-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 178.1.a.1">178.1.a.1.</a> If a Game Object is a Unit, regardless of its other types, it has Might, can have damage marked on it, enters exhausted, and can be played to any valid location.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-178-1-a-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-178-1-a-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 178.1.a.1.a">178.1.a.1.a.</a> It will not be recalled to base from a Battlefield in step 5 of a cleanup.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-178-1-a-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-178-1-a-2" aria-label="Link to rule 178.1.a.2">178.1.a.2.</a> If a Game Object is a Rune, regardless of its other types, it will be recycled to the Rune Deck.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-178-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-178-2" aria-label="Link to rule 178.2">178.2.</a> A Game Object that is more than one type maintains all of the permissions of all types.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-178-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-178-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 178.2.a">178.2.a.</a> If a Game Object is a Unit, it has the standard move inherent ability.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-178-2-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-178-2-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 178.2.a.1">178.2.a.1.</a> It can move and become located at a Battlefield.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-178-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-178-3" aria-label="Link to rule 178.3">178.3.</a> A Game Object that is more than one type can be affected by Game Effects that modify or interact with any of its types.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A unit that is also a gear can be affected by spells and abilities like the Ruination or Thermo Beam that say “Kill all units,” or “Kill all gear.” A unit that is also a gear can be targeted by spells and abilities like Vengeance or Rocket Barrage that say “Kill a unit,” or “Kill a gear.”</p></blockquote>
</div>

### 179. Tokens {#rule-179}


<div id="rule-180" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-180" aria-label="Link to rule 180">180.</a> Tokens are Game Objects created by spells and abilities during play.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-181" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-181" aria-label="Link to rule 181">181.</a> Tokens can be represented by anything. Printed tokens are included in Riftbound booster packs, but they are not required to play a token.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-182" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-182" aria-label="Link to rule 182">182.</a> A token's controller is the controller of the spell or ability that created it, unless the token's type innately determines control or that spell or ability specifies that a different player is the token's controller.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-183" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-183" aria-label="Link to rule 183">183.</a> A token's owner is the player who controlled the effect that created it.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-184" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-184" aria-label="Link to rule 184">184.</a> The effect that creates a token may specify the conditions or circumstances under which it enters the board. These stipulations may alter the usual steps for playing a card if the token is played.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-184-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-184-1" aria-label="Link to rule 184.1">184.1.</a> The effect may state that the token enters ready or exhausted, if that state is contrary to the default for the token's type.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-184-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-184-2" aria-label="Link to rule 184.2">184.2.</a> The effect may restrict the location to which the token may be played.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-184-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-184-3" aria-label="Link to rule 184.3">184.3.</a> The effect may grant temporary abilities or modifications to the token.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-185" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-185" aria-label="Link to rule 185">185.</a> Tokens are not cards.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-185-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-185-1" aria-label="Link to rule 185.1">185.1.</a> “Token” is an intrinsic category of Game Objects, in the same way “card” is.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-185-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-185-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 185.1.a">185.1.a.</a> Token Game Objects cannot lose their token nature by any means.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-185-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-185-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 185.1.b">185.1.b.</a> Card Game Objects cannot become tokens by any means.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-185-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-185-2" aria-label="Link to rule 185.2">185.2.</a> Tokens have some properties in common with cards.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-185-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-185-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 185.2.a">185.2.a.</a> Tokens can be played by their owner if their card type is played, following all the applicable steps for playing a card plus any restrictions or modifications from the effect that created the token.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-349">349</a>. Playing Cards for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-185-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-185-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 185.2.b">185.2.b.</a> Token units have a Might.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-185-2-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-185-2-c" aria-label="Link to rule 185.2.c">185.2.c.</a> Tokens may have one or more tags.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-185-2-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-185-2-d" aria-label="Link to rule 185.2.d">185.2.d.</a> Tokens have a type. They follow all rules for their type unless otherwise specified.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A token unit is a unit. It enters exhausted, can take the standard move action, deals damage equal to its Might in combat, is destroyed if it takes damage equal to or greater than its might, can be chosen or otherwise affected by spells or abilities that choose or affect units, etc.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-185-2-e" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-185-2-e" aria-label="Link to rule 185.2.e">185.2.e.</a> Tokens inherit the recycle destination of their type.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Token runes will be recycled to the Rune Deck. Token units, gear, and spells will be recycled to the Main Deck. Token battlefields and legends can’t be recycled.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-185-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-185-3" aria-label="Link to rule 185.3">185.3.</a> Tokens differ from cards in some ways.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-185-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-185-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 185.3.a">185.3.a.</a> Tokens do not have costs.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-185-3-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-185-3-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 185.3.a.1">185.3.a.1.</a> Although tokens do not have costs, their cost is treated as being 0 for all purposes.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-185-3-a-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-185-3-a-2" aria-label="Link to rule 185.3.a.2">185.3.a.2.</a> Tokens can have costs appended to them via applied Layer effects.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Deceiver’s conquer effect creates a 0 [M] Reflection unit token and applies a copy effect to that token. The copy effect will append all copyable traits, including the cost of the unit to be copied. This appends a cost to the Reflection token.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-185-3-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-185-3-b" aria-label="Link to rule 185.3.b">185.3.b.</a> Tokens do not have domains.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-185-3-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-185-3-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 185.3.b.1">185.3.b.1.</a> Tokens can have domains appended to them via applied Layer effects.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A card reads in part “As you play this, pick a domain. All units and gear are that domain.” If that card is in play, any unit and gear tokens will have the chosen domain appended to them.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-186" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-186" aria-label="Link to rule 186">186.</a> Tokens are Created on the board or the Chain and cannot exist elsewhere.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-186-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-186-1" aria-label="Link to rule 186.1">186.1.</a> If a token is put into any Non-Board Zone besides the chain, it ceases to exist immediately after moving to its new zone.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-187" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-187" aria-label="Link to rule 187">187.</a> The spell or ability that Creates a token specifies some of its characteristics. It may have other characteristics, as listed below.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-187-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-187-1" aria-label="Link to rule 187.1">187.1.</a> A 1 [M] Recruit token is a domainless unit token with 1 Might and the Recruit tag.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-187-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-187-2" aria-label="Link to rule 187.2">187.2.</a> A 3 [M] Sprite token with Temporary is a domainless unit token with 3 Might, the Fae tag, and the Temporary keyword.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-816">816</a>. Temporary for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-187-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-187-3" aria-label="Link to rule 187.3">187.3.</a> A 2 [M] Sand Soldier token is a domainless unit token with 2 Might and the Shurima tag.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-187-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-187-4" aria-label="Link to rule 187.4">187.4.</a> A 3 [M] Mech token is a domainless unit token with 3 Might and the Mech tag.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-187-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-187-5" aria-label="Link to rule 187.5">187.5.</a> A Gold gear token is a domainless gear token with “[Reaction][&gt;] Kill this, [E]: [Add] [A].”</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-187-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-187-6" aria-label="Link to rule 187.6">187.6.</a> A 0 [M] Reflection token is a domainless unit token with 0 Might.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-187-7" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-187-7" aria-label="Link to rule 187.7">187.7.</a> A 1 [M] Bird token is a domainless unit token with 1 Might, the Bird tag, and the Deflect keyword.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-809">809</a>. Deflect for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-187-8" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-187-8" aria-label="Link to rule 187.8">187.8.</a> A Brush battlefield token is a domainless battlefield token with “Bird, Cat, Dog, Poro, and Ivern units here have +1 [M]” and “When you score here, you may replace this with the battlefield it replaced.”</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-187-9" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-187-9" aria-label="Link to rule 187.9">187.9.</a> The Baron Pit battlefield token is a domainless battlefield token with “Units can move here from anywhere.”</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-187-10" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-187-10" aria-label="Link to rule 187.10">187.10.</a> A 1 [M] Tentacle token is a domainless unit token with 1 Might and the Bilgewater tag.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-187-11" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-187-11" aria-label="Link to rule 187.11">187.11.</a> A 0 [M] Shadow Clone token is a domainless unit token with 0 Might and “When I attack, you may banish a unit from your trash. If you do, give me [Assault 4] this turn.”</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-807">807</a>. Assault for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-187-12" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-187-12" aria-label="Link to rule 187.12">187.12.</a> A Bomb gear token is a domainless gear token with “[Deploy]” and “[Deathknell][&gt;] Deal 2 to an enemy unit here and kill all other Bombs you control here.”</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-830">830</a>. Deploy for more information.</p>
</div>

### 188. Control {#rule-188}


<div id="rule-189" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-189" aria-label="Link to rule 189">189.</a> Control is the concept of a player having influence of a Game Object and applies differently to different card types.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-190" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-190" aria-label="Link to rule 190">190.</a> Battlefields</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-190-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-190-1" aria-label="Link to rule 190.1">190.1.</a> Control is established over Battlefields through the course of play.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-190-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-190-2" aria-label="Link to rule 190.2">190.2.</a> Control is a binary state for Battlefields and an Identifier for players.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-190-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-190-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 190.2.a">190.2.a.</a> A Battlefield is Controlled or Uncontrolled.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-190-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-190-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 190.2.b">190.2.b.</a> A Battlefield is Controlled by a specific player or Controlled by no one.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-190-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-190-3" aria-label="Link to rule 190.3">190.3.</a> Control can be Contested through the course of play.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-190-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-190-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 190.3.a">190.3.a.</a> Contested is a temporary status applied to a Battlefield when a Unit controlled by a Player who does not currently Control that Battlefield Moves or otherwise becomes present there.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-190-3-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-190-3-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 190.3.a.1">190.3.a.1.</a> Units moving to or being played to a battlefield apply Contested status if that Battlefield is not already Contested and that Unit’s controller does not already control that battlefield.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-190-3-a-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-190-3-a-2" aria-label="Link to rule 190.3.a.2">190.3.a.2.</a> A Unit changing controller while located at a Battlefield will also apply Contested status to that Battlefield if it isn’t Contested and if the new controller doesn’t control that Battlefield.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-190-3-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-190-3-b" aria-label="Link to rule 190.3.b">190.3.b.</a> Once a Showdown or Combat begins at a Battlefield, it remains Contested until Control is established or re-established.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-190-3-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-190-3-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 190.3.b.1">190.3.b.1.</a> If a player who applied Contested status to a Battlefield controls no Units there and there is no ongoing Showdown or Combat at that Battlefield, then Contested status is removed in the following Cleanup.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-190-3-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-190-3-c" aria-label="Link to rule 190.3.c">190.3.c.</a> The state of a Battlefield being Contested is used to determine when Combat should occur and when a Non-Combat Showdown should occur.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-190-3-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-190-3-d" aria-label="Link to rule 190.3.d">190.3.d.</a> At this time Game Effects cannot reference this status.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-190-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-190-4" aria-label="Link to rule 190.4">190.4.</a> Control is established by having Units at a Battlefield at the end of a Showdown or Combat after applying the contested status.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-190-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-190-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 190.4.a">190.4.a.</a> If a player controls Units at a Battlefield, outside of Combat, they maintain Control of that Battlefield for as long as they have Units at that Battlefield.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-190-4-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-190-4-b" aria-label="Link to rule 190.4.b">190.4.b.</a> While a Combat or Showdown is ongoing at a Battlefield, Control of that Battlefield cannot change until instructed by steps of the Combat or Showdown.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-190-4-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-190-4-c" aria-label="Link to rule 190.4.c">190.4.c.</a> If a player has no Units at a Battlefield and the turn is in an Open state, they lose Control of that Battlefield in the following cleanup unless there is a Combat or Showdown ongoing there.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-190-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-190-5" aria-label="Link to rule 190.5">190.5.</a> Control is a constant state.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-190-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-190-6" aria-label="Link to rule 190.6">190.6.</a> Control of a Battlefield determines Control of its Abilities.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-190-6-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-190-6-a" aria-label="Link to rule 190.6.a">190.6.a.</a> While a Battlefield is Controlled, its Controller controls its Abilities unless that ability indicates another player does. The controlling player takes responsibility for adding them to the Chain if applicable, and makes all choices required by them unless otherwise specified.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-190-6-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-190-6-b" aria-label="Link to rule 190.6.b">190.6.b.</a> While a Battlefield is Uncontrolled, its Abilities are also Uncontrolled unless that ability indicates a player controls it. If there is no such indication, the Turn Player takes responsibility for adding them to the Chain if applicable, makes all choices required by them unless otherwise specified, and is treated as their Controller if any game rule or effect requires one.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: The Arena’s Greatest is a battlefield that reads “At the start of each player's first Beginning Phase, that player gains 1 point.” This ability will usually trigger while the battlefield has no controller. If it does, the Turn Player goes through the steps of adding the ability to the chain and receives priority after doing so, exactly as if they controlled the ability.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-190-6-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-190-6-c" aria-label="Link to rule 190.6.c">190.6.c.</a> If an Ability of a Battlefield indicates that a specific player makes a choice, that player is the Ability’s controller. They take responsibility for adding it to the chain if applicable and make all choices required by the ability. They and only they control the ability, regardless of who controls the Battlefield.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Abandoned Hall is a battlefield that reads “When a player plays a spell, they may give a unit they control here +1 might this turn.” The ability indicates that the player who played the spell makes the choice, so that player takes responsibility for putting the ability on the chain and makes all choices. They control the triggered ability.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-190-6-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-190-6-d" aria-label="Link to rule 190.6.d">190.6.d.</a> “You” in a battlefield’s abilities refers to the battlefield’s Controller, as does the implied “you” in instructions that don’t specify a player like “draw 1.” If the battlefield has no Controller, “you” refers to no one, and all such instructions are ignored.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-191" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-191" aria-label="Link to rule 191">191.</a> Everything Else</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-191-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-191-1" aria-label="Link to rule 191.1">191.1.</a> Players control their own Champion Legend.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-191-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-191-2" aria-label="Link to rule 191.2">191.2.</a> When a player Plays, Hides, or Creates a Card or other Game Object, they are established as that Game Object's Controller.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-191-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-191-3" aria-label="Link to rule 191.3">191.3.</a> For Spells, they are the Spell's Controller.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-191-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-191-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 191.3.a">191.3.a.</a> That player chooses targets.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-191-3-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-191-3-b" aria-label="Link to rule 191.3.b">191.3.b.</a> That player chooses modes.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-191-3-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-191-3-c" aria-label="Link to rule 191.3.c">191.3.c.</a> That player pays costs.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-191-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-191-4" aria-label="Link to rule 191.4">191.4.</a> For Permanents and Runes, when they Enter the Board, that player is assigned as that Game Object's Controller.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-191-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-191-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 191.4.a">191.4.a.</a> That player may make decisions about the Game Object's Inherent Abilities.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-191-4-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-191-4-b" aria-label="Link to rule 191.4.b">191.4.b.</a> That player may make decisions about the Game Object's Unique Abilities.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-191-4-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-191-4-c" aria-label="Link to rule 191.4.c">191.4.c.</a> That player may make decisions about any game effects or decisions necessary while the card is being played.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-191-4-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
