<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-347-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 347.2.a">347.2.a.</a> If all Players have passed once in sequence, the Showdown ends.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-347-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-347-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 347.2.b">347.2.b.</a> Otherwise, the next Player in Turn Order gains Focus.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-348" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-348" aria-label="Link to rule 348">348.</a> If all players pass Focus without playing a card or activating an ability, then the Showdown Closes.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-348-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-348-1" aria-label="Link to rule 348.1">348.1.</a> If it is a Combat Showdown, proceed with the remaining steps of Combat to resolve the phase.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-465">465</a>. The Steps of Combat for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-348-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-348-2" aria-label="Link to rule 348.2">348.2.</a> If it is a Non-Combat Showdown, do the following:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-348-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-348-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 348.2.a">348.2.a.</a> If only one player’s Units remain at the Battlefield, and if that player does not already Control the Battlefield, that player establishes Control over the Battlefield.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-188">188</a>. Control for more information on Control.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-471-1">471.1</a>. for more information on Conquering.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-348-2-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-348-2-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 348.2.a.1">348.2.a.1.</a> This results in a Conquer if that player has not yet scored that Battlefield this turn.</p>
</div>

### 349. Playing Cards {#rule-349}


<div id="rule-350" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-350" aria-label="Link to rule 350">350.</a> Playing a card is the act of a player utilizing their cards.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-350-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-350-1" aria-label="Link to rule 350.1">350.1.</a> A card is Played when it has finished this process in its entirety.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-350-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-350-2" aria-label="Link to rule 350.2">350.2.</a> Tokens are not cards, but can still be Played.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-179">179</a>. Tokens for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-351" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-351" aria-label="Link to rule 351">351.</a> Cards have different behaviors when played.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-351-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-351-1" aria-label="Link to rule 351.1">351.1.</a> Permanents become Game Objects when Played.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-351-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-351-2" aria-label="Link to rule 351.2">351.2.</a> Spells create game effects that are executed, then the card is placed in the trash after resolving.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-352" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-352" aria-label="Link to rule 352">352.</a> Cards have different states during the process of being played.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-352-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-352-1" aria-label="Link to rule 352.1">352.1.</a> When initially being played cards are Pending, as Pending Chain Items.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-352-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-352-2" aria-label="Link to rule 352.2">352.2.</a> Near the end of the process cards will cease being Pending and become Finalized Chain Items.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-353" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-353" aria-label="Link to rule 353">353.</a> The Process of Play</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-354" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-354" aria-label="Link to rule 354">354.</a> 1. Move the card from its current zone to the Chain.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-354-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-354-1" aria-label="Link to rule 354.1">354.1.</a> This Closes the State.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-307">307</a>. States of the Turn for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-354-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-354-2" aria-label="Link to rule 354.2">354.2.</a> This item becomes Pending, awaiting the finalization process (steps 2 - 5)</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-354-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-354-3" aria-label="Link to rule 354.3">354.3.</a> If another Card Effect or ability is currently resolving, continue resolving it before proceeding with any further steps of this process.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-354-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-354-4" aria-label="Link to rule 354.4">354.4.</a> If there are Tasks outstanding or currently being handled, finish those Tasks before continuing this process.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-332">332</a>. for more information on Tasks.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355" aria-label="Link to rule 355">355.</a> 2. Make relevant choices.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-1" aria-label="Link to rule 355.1">355.1.</a> If the card has an effect that specifies a choice or instructs an action that is not a cost "As I am played," or “As you play me,” those choices or actions are made or performed now.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 355.1.a">355.1.a.</a> This includes the choice of whether or not to pay an Optional Additional Cost.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-2" aria-label="Link to rule 355.2">355.2.</a> For Units, choose a valid Location where that Unit will enter upon being Played.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 355.2.a">355.2.a.</a> By default, Valid locations include the controller’s Base or a Battlefield the controller controls.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 355.2.b">355.2.b.</a> Some Game Effects may grant players permission to play Units to locations that are not normally Valid. Such locations become Valid for the purposes of Playing the Unit.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-3" aria-label="Link to rule 355.3">355.3.</a> For Spells and Abilities with a bulleted list of modes to choose from, make the appropriate choices now.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-4" aria-label="Link to rule 355.4">355.4.</a> For Spells and Abilities that Move one or more Units, choose a valid Location as the Move Destination for each Move that will be performed.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 355.4.a">355.4.a.</a> A valid Location for a Move Effect is one other than the Units’ current Location where they are allowed to be present.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-5" aria-label="Link to rule 355.5">355.5.</a> If a card requires you to specifically choose one or more Game Objects, that choice is made now.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-5-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-5-a" aria-label="Link to rule 355.5.a">355.5.a.</a> This does not include cards that affect one or more Game Objects based on criteria.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: "Stun a unit at a battlefield" is a Choice.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: "Kill all gear" is not a Choice.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-5-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-5-b" aria-label="Link to rule 355.5.b">355.5.b.</a> This does not include making choices for Triggered Abilities of permanents, or Delayed or Reflexive Triggers generated by the item being finalized, even if those abilities trigger when the chain item is played.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A unit with a triggered ability that says "When I'm played, kill a unit" does not require you to choose a target as it's played. The target will be chosen when the ability triggers.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Targon’s Peak reads “When you conquer here, ready up to 2 runes at the end of this turn.” Readying up to 2 runes at the end of the turn is the effect of a delayed trigger; any choices involved are not made when finalizing the conquer effect but when finalizing the resulting delayed trigger at the end of the turn.</p></blockquote>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-382">382</a>. Triggered Abilities for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-6" aria-label="Link to rule 355.6">355.6.</a> Targeting</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-7" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-7" aria-label="Link to rule 355.7">355.7.</a> When a card Chooses one or more specific Game Objects, Players, or Zones to affect, they are Targeted unless indicated otherwise by the rules in this section. If a card Picks one or more specific Game Objects, Players, or zones, they are not Targeted.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-8" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-8" aria-label="Link to rule 355.8">355.8.</a> In order to put a spell or ability on the chain, valid choices must be made for all targets.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-9" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-9" aria-label="Link to rule 355.9">355.9.</a> A target is a valid choice if it meets all of the following requirements:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-9-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-9-a" aria-label="Link to rule 355.9.a">355.9.a.</a> It is a permanent or rune on the board, a finalized spell or ability on the chain, a player or zone, or a game object specified explicitly or implicitly as being in some other zone. e.g., “Kill a unit” targets a unit on the board. e.g., “Recycle a unit from your trash” targets a unit card in your trash.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-9-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-9-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 355.9.a.1">355.9.a.1.</a> “Unit,” “gear,” and “rune” refer to objects on the Board unless specified otherwise.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-9-a-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-9-a-2" aria-label="Link to rule 355.9.a.2">355.9.a.2.</a> “Spell” and “ability” refer to finalized objects on the Chain unless specified otherwise.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-9-a-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-9-a-3" aria-label="Link to rule 355.9.a.3">355.9.a.3.</a> “Facedown card” refers to a card in a Facedown Zone unless specified otherwise.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-9-a-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-9-a-4" aria-label="Link to rule 355.9.a.4">355.9.a.4.</a> “Legend” refers to a legend in the Legend Zone.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-9-a-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-9-a-5" aria-label="Link to rule 355.9.a.5">355.9.a.5.</a> “Chosen Champion” and “unit in the Champion Zone” refer to a unit in the Champion Zone unless specified otherwise.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-9-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-9-b" aria-label="Link to rule 355.9.b">355.9.b.</a> It meets all targeting restrictions. e.g., A unit is a valid target for a spell that refers to a “unit at a battlefield,” “enemy unit,” “unit you control,” or “unit with Might 4 or greater” only if it meets the appropriate criteria. e.g. A unit that reads “I can’t be chosen by enemy spells or abilities.” is not a valid target for any enemy spell or ability, even if it meets other targeting restrictions.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-9-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-9-c" aria-label="Link to rule 355.9.c">355.9.c.</a> It is not the spell or ability itself. e.g., A spell that says “Counter a spell” cannot target itself. e.g., An ability of a permanent can target that permanent, because abilities and their sources are separate objects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-10" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-10" aria-label="Link to rule 355.10">355.10.</a> A game object, player, or zone mentioned in the text of a spell, activated ability, or triggered ability is a target UNLESS any of the following are true:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-10-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-10-a" aria-label="Link to rule 355.10.a">355.10.a.</a> It is in a zone whose information status is not Public. e.g., “Ready a legend” targets a legend, because the Legend Zone is Public. e.g., “Return a unit from your trash to your hand” targets a unit card in your trash, because your trash is Public. e.g., “You may play a unit from your hand, ignoring its costs” does not target a unit card in your hand, because your hand is not a public zone.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-10-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-10-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 355.10.a.1">355.10.a.1.</a> Public zones are Battlefield Zones, Bases, Trashes, Legend Zones, Champion Zones, and Facedown Zones.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-10-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-10-b" aria-label="Link to rule 355.10.b">355.10.b.</a> It is included only as part of a targeting restriction for another choice or only as a restriction or permission for a game action. e.g., “Kill a unit at a battlefield” targets a unit, but not a battlefield, because the units are targets and “at a battlefield” is a restriction. e.g., “Kill all units at a battlefield” targets a battlefield, but not any units. e.g., “Play a unit from your hand to a battlefield” doesn’t target a battlefield.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-10-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-10-c" aria-label="Link to rule 355.10.c">355.10.c.</a> It is included only as part of a cost, trigger condition, or replacement effect. e.g., “As an additional cost to play me, kill a friendly unit” doesn’t target anything. e.g., “When a friendly unit dies, kill a gear” targets a gear, but not a friendly unit. e.g., “When you play me, the next time a friendly unit would die this turn, return it to your hand instead” doesn’t target anything. The replacement effect applies when any friendly unit dies. e.g., “Choose a friendly unit. The next time it would die this turn, return it to your hand instead” targets a friendly unit, because “choose a friendly unit” is not part of the replacement effect.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-10-c-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-10-c-1" aria-label="Link to rule 355.10.c.1">355.10.c.1.</a> This includes costs within instructions, identified by phrases like “[do X] to [do Y].” The cost within that instruction is “[do X].” e.g., “When I hold, you may kill another friendly unit here to draw 1” does not target anything. e.g., “When you play me, you may spend a buff to move a friendly unit” targets the friendly unit, but not the buff.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-10-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-10-d" aria-label="Link to rule 355.10.d">355.10.d.</a> It is programmatically selected based on its characteristics rather than chosen by the spell or ability’s controller. e.g., “Kill all units at a battlefield” targets a battlefield, but does not target any units. e.g., “Kill all units at battlefields” doesn’t target anything. e.g., “Kill a unit. Its controller draws 2” targets the unit, but not its controller. e.g., “Recycle all cards in your trash” doesn’t target anything, because it affects all cards and you only have one trash.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-10-d-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-10-d-1" aria-label="Link to rule 355.10.d.1">355.10.d.1.</a> This exception applies solely to objects for which no choice is ever possible.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-10-d-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-10-d-2" aria-label="Link to rule 355.10.d.2">355.10.d.2.</a> This exception does not apply to objects that are the only valid choice at the moment a spell or ability is placed on the chain, but which would require a choice under other circumstances. e.g., “Kill a unit at a battlefield” always targets a unit, even if that unit is the only unit currently at a battlefield.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-10-e" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-10-e" aria-label="Link to rule 355.10.e">355.10.e.</a> It is part of a set of objects chosen in whole or in part by other players. e.g., “Each player kills a unit they control” does not target. Each player, including the one who played the spell, chooses a unit to kill as the spell or ability resolves.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-10-f" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-10-f" aria-label="Link to rule 355.10.f">355.10.f.</a> It is identified in an instruction that a player “must” complete. e.g., “You must recycle one of your runes” doesn’t target anything. You choose from among your runes as the spell or ability resolves. e.g., “Recycle a rune you control” targets a rune. You choose a rune you control as you put the spell or ability on the chain.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-10-g" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-10-g" aria-label="Link to rule 355.10.g">355.10.g.</a> It is a legend “you” control. e.g. “Ready your legend.” doesn’t target any legend. If a player controls multiple legends, they choose one as the spell or ability resolves. e.g. “Ready a friendly legend.” targets the friendly legend. e.g. “Exhaust an enemy legend.” targets the enemy legend.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-11" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-11" aria-label="Link to rule 355.11">355.11.</a> Some cards identify a group of Targets with Targeting Requirements that must be met by the group as a whole.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-11-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-11-a" aria-label="Link to rule 355.11.a">355.11.a.</a> As they’re finalized on the chain, such cards can choose any group of valid targets that collectively fulfill the targeting restriction.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-11-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-11-b" aria-label="Link to rule 355.11.b">355.11.b.</a> If the group of targets no longer collectively fulfill the targeting restriction as the spell or ability resolves, that spell or ability’s controller must choose a legal subset of the original targets that fulfills the targeting requirement for the spell or ability to affect.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player plays Fox-Fire, a spell that says in part “Kill any number of units at a battlefield with total Might 4 or less.” That player chooses four 1 [M] Recruit tokens at a single battlefield. As a Reaction, another player gives two of those Recruits +1 [M], so the Recruits’ Mights are 1, 1, 2, and 2. Then Fox-Fire resolves. The Recruits no longer have total Might 4 or less, so Fox-Fire’s controller must choose a legal subset of the original targets to affect. They could choose to kill the two 2 [M] Recruits, or the two 1 [M] Recruits plus one 2 [M] Recruit. The units they choose are Fox-Fire’s remaining legal targets. They can’t choose to affect units at the same battlefield that weren’t initially chosen as targets. They can, however, choose to affect units that were initially chosen as targets that left the chosen battlefield before Fox-Fire resolved as long as those units are all located at the same battlefield.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-11-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-11-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 355.11.b.1">355.11.b.1.</a> Any legal subset must include the maximum number of legal targets possible for that subset.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player plays Bellow’s Breath, a spell that reads in part “Deal 1 to up to 3 units at the same location.” They choose 3 units at the same battlefield. In reaction to Bellow’s Breath, the controller of those units plays Flash, moving two of them to base. On resolution of Bellow’s Breath, Bellow’s Breath’s controller cannot choose one unit in base and not the other. They may choose either of the legal subsets; the one unit remaining at the battlefield, or the two units in base.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-12" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-12" aria-label="Link to rule 355.12">355.12.</a> If a spell specifies that a player may perform a Game Action on some number of Game Objects, then all choices are considered targeted and chosen independently of the decision to perform the Game Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-13" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-13" aria-label="Link to rule 355.13">355.13.</a> If a card specifies that a player chooses “any number” or “up to” some number of Game Objects to be affected, they may choose any number of available targets, including zero. If they choose zero, the spell or ability can be played without any targets.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-14" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-14" aria-label="Link to rule 355.14">355.14.</a> Splitting</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-14-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-14-a" aria-label="Link to rule 355.14.a">355.14.a.</a> If a card specifies that an amount of damage may be split among some number of Units, then each Unit chosen is Targeted.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-14-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-14-b" aria-label="Link to rule 355.14.b">355.14.b.</a> The Targets are chosen when the spell or ability is finalized on the chain.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-14-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-14-c" aria-label="Link to rule 355.14.c">355.14.c.</a> A number of Targets can only be chosen up to, and not exceeding, the initial amount of damage available when the spell is played.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player playing a spell that instructs them to "Split 5 damage" may only choose up to 5 units, but may choose fewer.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-14-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-14-d" aria-label="Link to rule 355.14.d">355.14.d.</a> Each Target is valid, and contributes to Targeting Effects individually.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-14-e" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-14-e" aria-label="Link to rule 355.14.e">355.14.e.</a> The choice of how much damage is divided across the split is not decided until the resolution of the spell or ability.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-14-f" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-14-f" aria-label="Link to rule 355.14.f">355.14.f.</a> Each Target must receive a valid amount of damage.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-14-g" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-14-g" aria-label="Link to rule 355.14.g">355.14.g.</a> Valid damage is a positive integer amount, greater than or equal to 1 damage.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-418">418</a>. Deal for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-14-h" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-14-h" aria-label="Link to rule 355.14.h">355.14.h.</a> If, at resolution of the spell or effect, there are more Targets than available damage to divide, then the player who controls the effect dealing damage determines which Targets cease being Targets.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-14-h-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-14-h-1" aria-label="Link to rule 355.14.h.1">355.14.h.1.</a> That player cannot choose to have fewer Targets than they have damage to split when choosing which Targets cease being Targets.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player plays Alpha Strike, which reads in part “Choose a friendly unit. It deals damage equal to its Might split among enemy units at battlefields.” They target their 5 [M] unit and five 1 [M] enemy Recruits at battlefields. In reaction, their opponent plays Feral Strength targeting one of their recruits, and Frigid Touch targeting the 5 [M] unit. When Alpha Strike resolves, they have 3 damage to split, and must choose which targets cease being targets. They can’t choose for 4 of the units to cease being targets so that they can deal 3 to the 3 [M] recruit. They can only choose at most 2 of the targets to cease being targets.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-14-i" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-14-i" aria-label="Link to rule 355.14.i">355.14.i.</a> Any costs that were paid, or effects that were triggered as a result of those Game Objects being chosen as Targets remain in effect, paid, or otherwise triggered.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-15" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-15" aria-label="Link to rule 355.15">355.15.</a> These choices cannot be changed after this step unless instructed to by a spell or ability.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-750">750</a>. Making New Choices for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-16" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-16" aria-label="Link to rule 355.16">355.16.</a> A player may not make choices during this step that will deterministically result in illegal choices or actions later in this process unless they have no choice.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player plays a card which reads “As an additional cost to play this, kill the unit you control with the most Might. Give a friendly unit +[M] equal to the killed unit’s Might this turn. Predict 2.” They cannot choose to target their unit with the highest Might during this step of finalization.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-17" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-17" aria-label="Link to rule 355.17">355.17.</a> If a Game Effect has introduced a mandatory requirement on any of these choices, that mandatory requirement must be followed if possible. A mandatory requirement is one that specifies what choice can be made or how that choice is made, but doesn't specifically disallow other game states.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Here to Help is a spell that reads in part “You may play a unit from your hand to a battlefield you control.” If its controller decides to play a unit, they must play it to a battlefield they control.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Rek’Sai, Swarm Queen is a unit that reads “When I attack, you may reveal the top 2 cards of your Main Deck. You may banish one, then play it. If it is a unit, you may play it here. Recycle the rest.” The “you may play it here,” portion of this ability is not a mandatory requirement, because the player isn’t required to play the unit to that location.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-17-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-17-a" aria-label="Link to rule 355.17.a">355.17.a.</a> If there are multiple mandatory requirements that do not conflict, players must make a choice that will satisfy those mandatory requirements. Any individual group of such complementary mandatory requirements that contains as many non-contradictory mandatory requirements as possible forms a set. If there are two or more sets of mandatory requirements that are mutually contradictory, choose one of those sets and ignore the rest.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Here to Help is played from facedown at Targon’s Peak. Its controller chooses to play a Dune Drake from hand. The other battlefield is Dragon Roost. When finalizing Dune Drake, they choose to pay the [A][A] additional cost from Dragon Roost. There are two competing mandatory requirements here: playing Dune Drake to Targon’s Peak, or playing it to Dragon Roost. Dune Drake’s controller must choose one of those requirements to satisfy, and ignore the rest.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: If Here to Help were played from hand, and its controller controlled both battlefields, if they paid the [A][A] additional cost, they would have no choice but to play Dune Drake to Dragon Roost as it would satisfy both mandatory requirements.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-17-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-17-b" aria-label="Link to rule 355.17.b">355.17.b.</a> If there are one or more restrictions on choices being made during this process, those restrictions must be followed. Failure to do so means the card will fail to finalize.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Perched Grimwyrm is a unit that reads “Play me only to a battlefield you conquered this turn.” If a player plays Perched Grimwyrm via Here to Help and they haven’t conquered a battlefield this turn, Perched Grimwyrm will fail to finalize. Although Here to Help presents a mandatory requirement, Perched Grimwyrm’s restriction on being played overrides it.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Mageseeker Warden is a unit that reads in part “While I'm at a battlefield, opponents can only play units to their base.” If a player attempts to play a Perched Grimwyrm to a battlefield they’ve conquered this turn while their opponent controls a Mageseeker Warden at a battlefield, the Perched Grimwyrm will fail to finalize.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-355-18" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-355-18" aria-label="Link to rule 355.18">355.18.</a> If a spell or ability requires one or more players to make choices that are not outlined in this section, they are made on resolution.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-356" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356" aria-label="Link to rule 356">356.</a> 3. Determine Total Cost.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-1" aria-label="Link to rule 356.1">356.1.</a> Apply base cost modifications in any order.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 356.1.a">356.1.a.</a> If an ability or instruction allows you to play a card “for [Cost]”, replace the card’s Base Costs with [Cost].</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 356.1.b">356.1.b.</a> If an ability or instruction allows you to "ignore" one or more of a card's costs, set the appropriate Base Cost(s) of the card to zero.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-1-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-1-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 356.1.b.1">356.1.b.1.</a> If a card allows a player to play a card "ignoring its cost," its base Energy cost and base Power cost are set to zero.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-1-b-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-1-b-2" aria-label="Link to rule 356.1.b.2">356.1.b.2.</a> If a card instructs a player to play a card "ignoring its Energy cost" or "ignoring its Power cost," only the appropriate cost is set to zero, and the remaining cost still applies.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-1-b-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-1-b-3" aria-label="Link to rule 356.1.b.3">356.1.b.3.</a> Further additional costs and/or cost increases applied in subsequent steps may raise the card's Total Cost above zero.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Legion Rearguard is a Fury unit that costs 2 Energy and 0 Power and has Accelerate. A player plays Legion Rearguard and is instructed to ignore its costs, but chooses to pay the Accelerate cost. They ignore Legion Rearguard's Base Cost of 2 Energy, but the optional additional cost of 1 Energy and 1 Fury Power is added to its Total Cost and must be paid.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-1-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-1-c" aria-label="Link to rule 356.1.c">356.1.c.</a> Effects that refer to the Base Cost of the card refer to the Printed or Copied Base Cost of said card, and not the modified Base Cost that is used when determining the total cost of the card.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-2" aria-label="Link to rule 356.2">356.2.</a> Apply additional costs in any order.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 356.2.a">356.2.a.</a> Mandatory Additional Costs</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-2-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-2-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 356.2.a.1">356.2.a.1.</a> Some Additional Costs specified by Passive Abilities on the card being played or another card are Mandatory, and must be paid to complete playing the card. They use the phrase "as an additional cost" and don't include the word "may."</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A unit has the passive ability "As an additional cost to play me, kill a friendly unit." To play that unit, a player must kill a friendly unit.</p></blockquote>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-363">363</a>. Passive Abilities for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-2-a-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-2-a-2" aria-label="Link to rule 356.2.a.2">356.2.a.2.</a> The cost imposed by the Deflect keyword is a Mandatory Additional Cost.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-809">809</a>. Deflect for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 356.2.b">356.2.b.</a> Optional Additional Costs</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-2-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-2-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 356.2.b.1">356.2.b.1.</a> Some Optional Costs specified by Passive Abilities on the card being played or another card are Non-Mandatory, and must be paid only if the player made the choice to pay them in step 2. They use the phrase "as an additional cost" and the word "may."</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A unit has the ability "As you play me, you may discard 1 as an additional cost. If you do, reduce my cost by [2]." While playing the unit, its controller declares their intention to pay the additional cost in step 2, applies that additional cost in rule <a href="#rule-356-2">356.2</a>, applies the discount granted by paying that cost in rule <a href="#rule-356-4">356.4</a>, and discards a card to pay that additional cost in rule <a href="#rule-357-2">357.2</a>.</p></blockquote>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-363">363</a>. Passive Abilities for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-3" aria-label="Link to rule 356.3">356.3.</a> Apply cost increases.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-4" aria-label="Link to rule 356.4">356.4.</a> Apply discounts.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 356.4.a">356.4.a.</a> Discounts may be applied by the card being played or by any other card or effect.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-4-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-4-b" aria-label="Link to rule 356.4.b">356.4.b.</a> Discounts may say that cards "cost [amount] less" or that one or more of their costs are "reduced by [amount]."</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-4-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-4-c" aria-label="Link to rule 356.4.c">356.4.c.</a> Discounts that only apply to a component of the cost will be applied when that component is added to the cost of the spell and before any other discounts.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Ezreal, Prodigy reads “optional additional costs you pay cost [1] or [A] less.” When playing a Frigid Touch and choosing to pay the additional cost in step 2, as soon as the additional cost is added to the cost of the spell, Ezreal, Prodigy’s discount is applied to it.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-4-c-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-4-c-1" aria-label="Link to rule 356.4.c.1">356.4.c.1.</a> Discounts that apply to a given component of a spell’s cost may be applied in any order to that component, and apply only to that component.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-4-c-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-4-c-2" aria-label="Link to rule 356.4.c.2">356.4.c.2.</a> Discounts that apply to a given component of a spell’s cost cannot reduce that component to less than 0.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-4-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-4-d" aria-label="Link to rule 356.4.d">356.4.d.</a> Discounts that apply to the total cost of a spell and not any one component of the cost must be applied after any discount that applies only to a component of the cost.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-4-d-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-4-d-1" aria-label="Link to rule 356.4.d.1">356.4.d.1.</a> These discounts may be applied in any order as long as they are applied after component discounts.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-4-e" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-4-e" aria-label="Link to rule 356.4.e">356.4.e.</a> If a discount applies a minimum cost, that minimum applies only to that discount.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Eager Apprentice says "While I'm at a battlefield, the Energy costs for spells you play is reduced by [1], to a minimum of [1]." A player who controls Eager Apprentice and a unit with 7 Might plays Sky Splitter, a spell that costs 8 Energy and says "This spell's Energy cost is reduced by the highest Might among units you control." That player can choose to apply Eager Apprentice's discount first, reducing Sky Splitter's Energy cost to 7, then apply Sky Splitter's discount, reducing its Energy cost to 0. If they applied these discounts in the other order, Sky Splitter's Energy cost would be 1.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-4-f" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-4-f" aria-label="Link to rule 356.4.f">356.4.f.</a> Discounts can reduce additional costs, including to 0.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-4-f-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-4-f-1" aria-label="Link to rule 356.4.f.1">356.4.f.1.</a> An optional additional cost was "paid" if the player made the decision to pay it. It doesn't matter how much the player actually paid.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Clockwork Keeper is a unit that costs 2 Energy and 0 Power and says "As you play me, you may pay [C] as an additional cost. If you do, draw 1." A player controls a card that says "Units you play cost [A] less." That player plays Clockwork Keeper and chooses to pay the optional additional cost of [C]. They will draw a card, even though the optional additional cost was reduced to 0.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-4-g" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-4-g" aria-label="Link to rule 356.4.g">356.4.g.</a> If a discount applies a variable cost reduction to the card, that value is checked now.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Rhasa, the Sunderer reads “I cost [1] less for each card in your trash.” The value of this discount depends on the number of cards in the trash. That value is checked during this step when the discount is applied to Rhasa’s cost. If the number of cards in his controller’s trash changes at a later point in the finalization process, that will not affect the value checked during this step or the total cost calculated in this step.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-5" aria-label="Link to rule 356.5">356.5.</a> Apply modifications to the total cost in any order.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-5-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-5-a" aria-label="Link to rule 356.5.a">356.5.a.</a> If an effect allows a player to play a card ignoring “any and all costs,” set the total cost to [0], including any non-standard costs.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-6" aria-label="Link to rule 356.6">356.6.</a> Energy and Power costs can't be reduced below 0.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-356-7" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-356-7" aria-label="Link to rule 356.7">356.7.</a> Costs may be Energy costs, Power costs, or non-standard costs.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A card reads "As an additional cost to play me, kill a friendly unit." Killing a friendly unit is an additional cost to play that card.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-357" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-357" aria-label="Link to rule 357">357.</a> 4. Pay the card's costs.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-357-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-357-1" aria-label="Link to rule 357.1">357.1.</a> In total, pay the combined Energy cost (if any) and Power cost (if any).</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-357-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-357-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 357.1.a">357.1.a.</a> During this step, the card's controller can use activated abilities with the Reaction tag that Add resources to add Energy and Power to pay the card's costs.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-165">165</a>. Rune Pools and rule <a href="#rule-430">430</a>. Add for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-357-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-357-2" aria-label="Link to rule 357.2">357.2.</a> In addition, pay any non-standard Cost summed in step 3 in any order.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A card reads in part “As an additional cost to play me, kill a friendly unit and discard a unit.” When paying the costs for that card, its controller can pay those two costs in any order; they can kill a friendly unit first and then discard a unit, or they can discard a unit and then kill a friendly unit.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-357-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-357-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 357.2.a">357.2.a.</a> Costs that are replaced with other events by replacement effects are still considered paid.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player plays Cruel Patron, which says "As an additional cost to play me, kill a friendly unit." They also control Zhonya’s Hourglass, which says “If a friendly unit would die, kill this instead. Heal that unit, exhaust it, and recall it.” They choose to kill a friendly unit during step 3, but as they pay the cost in step 4, Zhonya’s Hourglass replaces that unit’s death. The cost is considered paid, and the player can continue playing Cruel Patron.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-357-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-357-3" aria-label="Link to rule 357.3">357.3.</a> A player may not pay costs during this step that will deterministically result in illegal choices or actions later in this process unless they have no choice.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player plays a card which reads “as an additional cost to play this, you may kill a friendly unit. Give a friendly unit +2 [M] this turn. If you paid the cost, give that unit +7 [M] this turn instead” If they chose to pay the cost, they must choose to kill a unit other than the targeted unit unless they have no choice.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-358" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-358" aria-label="Link to rule 358">358.</a> 5. Check legality.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-358-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-358-1" aria-label="Link to rule 358.1">358.1.</a> Check that the card made appropriate choices.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: If the card chose targets, ensure that the targets are legal.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: If the card made choices that had restrictions or requirements, ensure that restrictions were followed and that the requirements were met as appropriate.</p></blockquote>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-355">355</a>. Make Relevant Choices for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-358-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-358-2" aria-label="Link to rule 358.2">358.2.</a> Check that all costs were paid.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-358-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-358-3" aria-label="Link to rule 358.3">358.3.</a> Check that the outcome of the effect of this card being played would not create an illegal state.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Check that a spell's execution does not create a state where a Battlefield has Units controlled by 3 different players.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Check that a permanent is not played to a base other than that of its controller.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-358-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-358-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 358.3.a">358.3.a.</a> If a Game Effect prevents the performance of a game action, that effect doesn’t prevent cards and abilities that instruct a player to perform that game action from being played or finalized.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Flash is a spell that reads in part “Move up to 2 friendly units to base.” Vilemaw’s Lair is a battlefield that reads “Units can’t move from here to base.” Flash is legal to play choosing a unit at Vilemaw’s Lair; on resolution of the spell, the move instruction will be negated.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-358-3-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-358-3-b" aria-label="Link to rule 358.3.b">358.3.b.</a> This check only extends to the resolution of the current card. Any Reflexive Triggers, cards played, or abilities triggered as a result of the execution must be evaluated on their own merits as they finalize.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-358-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-358-4" aria-label="Link to rule 358.4">358.4.</a> Check that the card has the appropriate permissions to be played at this timing.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: If the state is Showdown Closed and the card was the one that Closed the state, ensure that it has [Action] or [Reaction].</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: If the state is Closed and the card wasn’t the one that Closed the state, ensure that it has [Reaction].</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-358-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-358-5" aria-label="Link to rule 358.5">358.5.</a> If any of the above checks fail, the actions taken in this process are undone, any effects that those actions caused to occur are treated as having never happened, and the action is cancelled.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-359" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359" aria-label="Link to rule 359">359.</a> 6. Finish finalizing this card and proceed with the card's category of Play.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-1" aria-label="Link to rule 359.1">359.1.</a> This card is no longer Pending.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-2" aria-label="Link to rule 359.2">359.2.</a> A Permanent leaves the Chain and becomes a Game Object.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 359.2.a">359.2.a.</a> Any passive abilities become active.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 359.2.b">359.2.b.</a> Execute all rules text on the card, from top to bottom.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-2-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-2-c" aria-label="Link to rule 359.2.c">359.2.c.</a> If it is a Unit, it enters the Board exhausted at the Location that was chosen.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-2-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-2-d" aria-label="Link to rule 359.2.d">359.2.d.</a> If it is a Non-Unit Gear, it enters the Board Ready at the player's Base.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3">359.3.</a> A Spell lingers on the Chain.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.a">359.3.a.</a> This card becomes a Finalized Chain Item.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-b" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.b">359.3.b.</a> If there are other Pending Items on the Chain, then the controller of those Pending Items completes Steps 2 through 5 of Playing Cards for those items before continuing.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-327">327</a>. Chains for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-c" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.c">359.3.c.</a> Other players have an opportunity to play Reactions before the resolution of spells.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-327">327</a>. Chains for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-d" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.d">359.3.d.</a> Otherwise, execute the game effect of the spell, from top to bottom of the rules text of the card and then place the card in the Trash of the owning player.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-e" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-e" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.e">359.3.e.</a> Handling illegal and impossible instructions</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-e-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-e-1" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.e.1">359.3.e.1.</a> The spell resolves even if some or all of its targets are illegal.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-e-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-e-2" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.e.2">359.3.e.2.</a> A target is illegal as the spell resolves if it no longer meets the targeting requirements of the spell, or if it has changed Zones to or from a Non-Board Zone.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-e-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-e-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.e.2.a">359.3.e.2.a.</a> This includes targeting restrictions that are applied to the spell by game effects other than the spell’s instructions themselves.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Temporal Breach is a spell that reads in part “Banish a unit, then its owner plays it to the same location, ignoring its cost.” By default, the only targeting restrictions on the spell are that the target is a unit, and that the unit is able to be chosen by the spell. However, if Temporal Breach is played from facedown, it gains the restriction that it must choose from among game objects at the battlefield it was played from. If the unit it chooses moves to a different location, that unit will be an illegal target on resolution of Temporal Breach.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-e-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-e-3" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.e.3">359.3.e.3.</a> If a target ceases to meet the targeting requirements while the spell is on the chain, then meets them again, it's a legal target.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A spell targets "a unit at a battlefield." A player reacts with a spell that moves the unit to base, then another player reacts with a spell that moves it back to that battlefield, then the original spell resolves. The unit is a legal target.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-e-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-e-4" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.e.4">359.3.e.4.</a> If a target changes Zones to or from a Non-Board Zone and then returns to its original zone, it is no longer a legal target, because it's not treated as the same object.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Examples: An enemy unit at a battlefield is no longer a legal target if it is no longer an enemy, no longer a unit, or no longer at a battlefield.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>A unit with 3 or less Might is no longer a legal target if it is no longer a unit or if its Might is greater than 3.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Something that's exhausted is no longer a legal target if it is no longer exhausted. (It can't stop being "something.")</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-e-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-e-5" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.e.5">359.3.e.5.</a> If any of the spell's targets are no longer legal, those game objects, players, or zones are unaffected by the spell as it resolves. Any instructions related to an illegal target can’t be followed.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player plays Void Seeker, a spell that says "Deal 4 to a unit at a battlefield. Draw 1." The unit's controller uses a Reaction to move the unit to their base. Since the unit is no longer a legal target, it is not dealt any damage. Void Seeker's controller still draws 1.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player plays Bellow’s Breath targeting a unit in combat that reads in part “I can’t be chosen by enemy spells and abilities unless I’m in combat.” In reaction, that unit’s controller plays Flash, moving the unit to their base. The unit is no longer in combat, so it is no longer a legal target for Bellow’s Breath. The unit will be unaffected by Bellow’s Breath as it resolves.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player plays Hidden Blade from the facedown zone at a battlefield, targeting an enemy unit. In reaction to Hidden Blade, their opponent plays Tideturner from facedown at another battlefield, choosing to swap locations with the unit Hidden Blade targeted. When Hidden Blade resolves, the chosen unit is no longer at the appropriate battlefield, so any instructions related to that unit are ignored.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-e-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-e-6" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.e.6">359.3.e.6.</a> Instructions that can't be followed because of illegal targets, or because they reference information that is unavailable, are considered Ignored. Instructions that can’t be followed for any other reason are Negated.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player plays Ride the Wind choosing to move their unit at Vilemaw’s Lair to base. Base is a legal move destination for Ride the Wind, but on resolution of Ride the Wind’s effect, the move instruction will be negated because Vilemaw’s restriction makes the instruction impossible.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player plays Hidden Blade targeting a unit at a battlefield. In reaction to Hidden Blade, the unit’s controller plays Flash, moving it to base. Hidden Blade’s instruction “Kill a unit.” will be ignored on resolution of the spell.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-e-7" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-e-7" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.e.7">359.3.e.7.</a> If all of an instruction's Targets become Invalid or Unavailable by the time the spell begins resolving, that instruction will not execute.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-e-8" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-e-8" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.e.8">359.3.e.8.</a> If an instruction has more than one Target and fewer than all of the Targets become Invalid or Unavailable by the time the spell begins resolving, the instruction will execute, with only the Targets available and valid being operated on.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Singularity reads “deal 6 to each of up to two units.” The instruction will execute even if one of those units is made unavailable before the spell begins.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-e-9" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-e-9" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.e.9">359.3.e.9.</a> The process for a card's choice becoming Invalid or Unavailable is referred to as Mistargeting.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A spell has the instruction "Deal 2 to a unit at a battlefield." Before that instruction can execute, the chosen unit is moved to its base. The instruction will not be executed, because it specifies that the unit it chooses must be at a Battlefield, and by the time it attempted to execute, the unit was no longer valid as a choice.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-e-9-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-e-9-a" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.e.9.a">359.3.e.9.a.</a> If another spell or ability attempts to reference the number of game objects, players, or zones that a Finalized Chain Item targets, it will include any mistargeted choices in the zone specified implicitly or explicitly by the instruction that targeted them, but not any targets that have changed to or from a non-board zone.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: When a player moves their Volibear, Furious to an occupied enemy battlefield and combat initiates, Volibear’s attack trigger goes on the chain targeting three of the units at that battlefield. In reaction, the defending player plays Flash moving two of the three units back. That player cannot then target the attack trigger with Repulse, which reads “Choose a friendly unit at a battlefield. Counter an enemy spell or ability that chooses it and no other friendly unit.” If the defending player instead played Heedless Resurrection twice, killing the two units, Repulse can legally target the attack trigger, because two of the targets have changed to a non-board zone.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-e-10" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-e-10" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.e.10">359.3.e.10.</a> It is possible for none of a spell's instructions to be executed as it resolves, due to all of them requiring targets to act on and all of those targets becoming Invalid or Unavailable. In this case, the spell has no effect but is still considered played.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player plays a spell that reads "Deal 2 to a unit at a battlefield" with no other instructions, and chooses an enemy unit at a battlefield. They also control a unit with the ability "When you play a spell, give me +1 [M] this turn." Before the spell resolves, the chosen unit is moved to its base. The spell resolves and its only instruction cannot be executed, but the unit's ability still triggers as the spell resolves and gives it +1 [M].</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-e-11" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-e-11" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.e.11">359.3.e.11.</a> Instructions that can be partially followed are followed as much as possible and negated otherwise.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player plays a spell that says "Discard 2, then draw 2." If their hand is empty, the instruction to discard 2 will be negated. They'll still draw 2. If they had 1 card in hand, they would discard it and draw 2.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-e-12" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-e-12" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.e.12">359.3.e.12.</a> If the spell checks information about a target that is no longer legal or a card or permanent whose location, zone, or status has changed such that that information is no longer available, that check returns "null" and all instructions based on it are Ignored.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Examples: A unit that is no longer on the board is treated as having null Might, null cost, etc.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>A unit that is no longer on the board has no location, is neither exhausted nor readied, etc.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Baited Hook says "[1][C], [E]: Kill a friendly unit. Look at the top 5 cards of your Main Deck. You may banish a unit from among them that has Might up to 1 more than the killed unit and play it, ignoring its cost. Then recycle the rest." While Baited Hook’s ability is on the chain, an opponent reacts with a spell that returns the friendly unit to its owner's hand. Because the friendly unit is no longer a legal target, it can't be killed and its Might is treated as null. Baited Hook’s controller looks at the top 5 cards of their Main Deck, but can’t choose any unit from among them.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Strike Down reads “Choose an equipped friendly unit. It deals damage equal to its Might to an enemy unit. Then detach an Equipment from it.” While Strike Down is on the chain targeting a unit with only one Equipment attached to it, that unit has its Equipment detached via Angle Shot. When Strike Down goes to resolve, the targeted unit is no longer legal, so the unit’s Might will return as “null” and the instructions related to it are ignored.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-e-12-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-e-12-a" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.e.12.a">359.3.e.12.a.</a> If the spell checks information about a target that is legal or a card or permanent whose location, zone, or status has not changed such that information is no longer available, that information is accessible.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-e-13" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-e-13" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.e.13">359.3.e.13.</a> A spell or ability that moves something to a different zone as a cost or effect can "look back" at its characteristics before it changes zones.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-e-14" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-e-14" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.e.14">359.3.e.14.</a> Some instructions may reference Game Objects affected by, or Game Actions performed in, other instructions in a card. The referenced and referencing instructions are called “linked instructions.”</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Hidden Blade reads “Kill a unit at a battlefield. Its controller draws 2.” The “its” in the second instruction references the unit in the first, so the two instructions of the spell are linked.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-e-14-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-e-14-a" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.e.14.a">359.3.e.14.a.</a> In order for a later linked instruction to execute, its earlier linked instruction must have executed or been negated. If the earlier linked instruction is Ignored, the later linked instruction will also be Ignored. If any individual target of an earlier linked instruction is ignored, it will also be ignored by any later linked instruction.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Hidden Blade reads “Kill a unit at a battlefield. Its controller draws 2.” If the chosen unit changes zones or moves to base in reaction to Hidden Blade, the spell will mistarget and the first instruction will be ignored. If the first instruction is ignored, the second instruction will not execute and the unit’s controller will not draw 2.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-e-14-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-e-14-b" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.e.14.b">359.3.e.14.b.</a> If the Game Action performed in an earlier linked instruction is Negated, this will not affect the later linked instruction, unless the later linked instruction directly references the Game Action being performed. An instruction is Negated if the event its execution generates is replaced or skipped, or if the game action can’t be performed because of a reason other than it being Ignored.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Hidden Blade reads “Kill a unit at a battlefield. Its controller draws 2.” This effect is split into two instructions: killing the unit and the targeted unit’s controller drawing 2. The later linked instruction doesn’t reference an action directly, so it will execute even if the kill action of the earlier linked action is negated.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Deathgrip reads “Kill a friendly unit. If you do, give +[M] equal to its Might to another friendly unit this turn.” If the friendly unit in question is not killed by Deathgrip because its death is replaced with another event, the later linked instruction will not execute because “if you do” directly references the game action.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Ride the Wind is a spell that reads in part “Move a friendly unit and ready it.” Vilemaw’s Lair is a battlefield that reads “Units can’t move from here to base.” If Ride the Wind is played on a unit at Vilemaw’s Lair choosing the base as its destination, the move instruction will be negated on resolution by the passive ability of Vilemaw’s Lair, but the ready instruction will still execute.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-e-14-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-e-14-c" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.e.14.c">359.3.e.14.c.</a> Referencing a game action in this way only refers to the game action being performed and the object or player it was performed on, not the circumstances of its performance.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A spell reads “Deal 3 to an enemy unit at a battlefield. If you do, heal units you control at the same location as that unit.” If some but not all of that damage is prevented, the later linked instruction will still execute.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A spell reads “Banish an enemy unit at a battlefield, then play it to any battlefield. Then if you do, do this: stun all enemy units there.” A player plays it targeting a unit that has a passive replacement effect that reads “I enter in base.” The replacement effect causes the unit to enter in base, but this does not prevent the reflexive trigger of the spell from being placed on the chain. All that matters is that the unit was played.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-e-15" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-e-15" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.e.15">359.3.e.15.</a> A spell or ability that leaves the chain during the process of its resolution will cease further execution of its instructions. This immediately causes the spell or ability to finish resolving.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-e-16" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-e-16" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.e.16">359.3.e.16.</a> If a Delayed Ability’s duration has ended before it was generated, the Delayed Ability is not generated and any instructions related to it are ignored.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Targon’s Peak is a battlefield that reads in part “When you conquer here, ready up to 2 runes at the end of this turn.” If Targon’s Peak is conquered during the Ending Phase after the beginning of the ending step, such as via an effect like Thrill of the Hunt, the delayed trigger will not be generated.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Hostile Takeover is a spell that reads “Take control of an enemy unit at a battlefield. Ready it.” and “Lose control of that unit and recall it at end of turn.” If Hostile Takeover is played during the Ending Phase, its delayed triggered ability will not trigger. “At end of turn” and “at the end of this turn” are synonymous, so it will not trigger again.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Akshan, Mischievous reads in part “When you play me, if you paid the additional cost, move an enemy gear to your base. You control it until I leave the board. If it's an Equipment, attach it to me.” If Akshan leaves the board in reaction to his play effect, the delayed passive ability “You control it until I leave the board,” will not be generated. You will not gain control of the targeted gear even for a moment.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-f" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-f" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.f">359.3.f.</a> Referents</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-f-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-f-1" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.f.1">359.3.f.1.</a> Some information used by abilities is referenced from the source of those abilities, or from one or more targets of a spell or ability. This can usually be recognized by the presence of words like “here,” “my,” or “its.”</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-f-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-f-2" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.f.2">359.3.f.2.</a> Information referenced in an instruction in this way will be checked on execution of the instruction, unless specified otherwise by the effect.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Examples: A player moves Yasuo, Remorseful to an occupied enemy battlefield and initiates combat there. In reaction to the Yasuo, Remorseful attack trigger, their opponent plays Fight or Flight from facedown targeting Yasuo, moving him back to base. When the attack trigger resolves, “here” is no longer the battlefield where combat is ongoing and the attack trigger mistargets.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>In reaction to a Yasuo, Remorseful attack trigger, an opponent plays Stupefy targeting Yasuo. When Yasuo’s attack trigger resolves, it will deal damage equal to his current Might of 5.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-f-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-f-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.f.2.a">359.3.f.2.a.</a> When a referent checks information on execution of the instruction related to a target, and that target isn’t legal, that referent will return “null” and all instructions related to it will be ignored.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-f-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-f-3" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.f.3">359.3.f.3.</a> Some information used by triggered abilities is referenced from the trigger condition of the ability. This information is checked when the trigger condition is fulfilled.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Lillia, Fae Fawn reads “when I move from a location, play a 3 [M] Sprite token with Temporary there.” If Lillia moves to a battlefield, her triggered ability will be placed on the chain and it will note the location she moved from when it does so. If she moves to a non-board zone in reaction to the triggered ability on the chain, it will not affect where the Sprite token will be played when the triggered ability resolves.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-f-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-f-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.f.3.a">359.3.f.3.a.</a> In the case of a delayed triggered ability, the information is referenced when the triggered ability is created unless specified otherwise.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-f-3-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-f-3-b" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.f.3.b">359.3.f.3.b.</a> In the case of a linked ability that references information from a linked triggered ability, that information may be referenced from the trigger condition of the linked triggered ability if specified.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Iascylla reads “When I hold, at the start of your next Main Phase, you may move an enemy unit to this battlefield.” The “this battlefield” in her delayed triggered ability refers to the battlefield she held, and so will be referenced from the trigger condition, when the triggered ability is generated.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-359-3-f-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-359-3-f-4" aria-label="Link to rule 359.3.f.4">359.3.f.4.</a> Some information used by the effect of a triggered ability is referenced from the triggered ability itself, such as “enemy” and “friendly” status. This information is checked on execution of the referencing instruction.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Yasuo, Remorseful reads “when I attack, deal damage equal to my Might to an enemy unit here.” Yasuo moves to an occupied enemy battlefield and his attack trigger goes on the chain. In reaction to the attack trigger, the defending player plays a hidden Hostile Takeover and gains control of Yasuo. The triggered ability is unaffected by Yasuo changing controllers, and “enemy” is in reference to the triggered ability itself, so it will resolve with no issue.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: In reaction to the same Yasuo, Remorseful trigger, say the defending player had instead played a spell that reads “[Reaction]. Gain control of a triggered ability. You may make new choices for it.” They chose the attack trigger. When Yasuo’s attack trigger resolves, if they didn’t make new choices for the trigger, the controller of the triggered ability will no longer be an enemy to the targeted unit, so the triggered ability will mistarget and do nothing. If they instead chose Yasuo with the attack trigger, he would be an enemy unit to the triggered ability and so it would deal damage equal to his Might to himself.</p></blockquote>
</div>

### 360. Abilities {#rule-360}


<div id="rule-361" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-361" aria-label="Link to rule 361">361.</a> An Ability is the structured rules and capabilities of Game Objects or Spells.</p>
</div>
