<div id="rule-361-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-361-1" aria-label="Link to rule 361.1">361.1.</a> An Ability has multiple structures. Passive Abilities Replacement Effects Activated Abilities Triggered Abilities Delayed Abilities</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-362" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-362" aria-label="Link to rule 362">362.</a> A card can have more than one Ability and more than one type of Ability.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-363" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-363" aria-label="Link to rule 363">363.</a> Passive Abilities</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-364" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-364" aria-label="Link to rule 364">364.</a> Conditions, rules, constraints, or statements that affect the course of regular play.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-364-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-364-1" aria-label="Link to rule 364.1">364.1.</a> These abilities have a wide variety of formats to recognize.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: "I get +1 [M] while you have 2 or more cards in your hand."</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: "Friendly Yordles at my battlefield have [Shield]."</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-364-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-364-2" aria-label="Link to rule 364.2">364.2.</a> They can be recognized by being statements of fact.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-364-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-364-3" aria-label="Link to rule 364.3">364.3.</a> Passive Abilities can be conditional.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-364-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-364-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 364.3.a">364.3.a.</a> Conditional Passive Abilities can be recognized by the occurrence of "if" or "while" as part of the statement of the ability.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: "While I'm attacking or defending alone, I have +2 [M]."</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: "If an opponent controls a battlefield, I enter ready."</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-365" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-365" aria-label="Link to rule 365">365.</a> Presence on Permanents</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-365-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-365-1" aria-label="Link to rule 365.1">365.1.</a> Passive Abilities of Permanents are typically only active while on the Board.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-366" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-366" aria-label="Link to rule 366">366.</a> Presence on Card outside of the Board</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-366-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-366-1" aria-label="Link to rule 366.1">366.1.</a> Passive Abilities of cards in zones that are outside of the Board will self-describe their context.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: The passive ability "Play me only during an opponent's turn." applies in any zone from which that card can be played.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Undying Legion has a passive ability that reads “[Legion][&gt;] You may play me from your trash for [3][C].” That passive ability only applies when Undying Legion is in the trash.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-366-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-366-2" aria-label="Link to rule 366.2">366.2.</a> Passive Abilities can alter the costs of cards as they are played.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-366-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-366-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 366.2.a">366.2.a.</a> These apply at all times in any zone from which the card with the ability can be played.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-367" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-367" aria-label="Link to rule 367">367.</a> Replacement Effects</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-368" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-368" aria-label="Link to rule 368">368.</a> An ability that alters the application of another game effect or game rule.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-368-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-368-1" aria-label="Link to rule 368.1">368.1.</a> Passive Abilities can be Replacement Effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-369" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-369" aria-label="Link to rule 369">369.</a> Replacement Effects intercede during the execution of a Game Effect and alter its execution.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-369-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-369-1" aria-label="Link to rule 369.1">369.1.</a> A Replacement Effect can usually be identified by the presence of the terms “as,” “would,” or "instead."</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Zhonya's Hourglass reads "The next time a friendly unit would die, kill this instead. Heal that unit, exhaust it, and recall it." This is a replacement effect that alters the execution of any Game Effect that would kill a friendly unit.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Undertitan is a unit that reads in part “As I’m revealed from your deck, [Add] [2].” This is a replacement effect that alters the execution of any Game Effect that reveals Undertitan from your deck.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-369-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-369-2" aria-label="Link to rule 369.2">369.2.</a> Some Game Actions are themselves Replacement Effects.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Burning Out is a replacement effect.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Preventing Damage is a replacement effect.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-369-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-369-3" aria-label="Link to rule 369.3">369.3.</a> Replacement Effects that apply to a unit as it enters the Board can be identified by describing how the unit enters, or by describing a game action that occurs “as” a unit enters.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Master Yi, Honed reads “I enter ready.” This applies a replacement effect to the way that units normally enter. The event of him entering exhausted is replaced by one where he enters ready.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Baron Nashor reads “As you play me, add the Baron Pit battlefield token to the board if it's not there already. If you do, I enter there.” The last sentence of his ability is a replacement effect that replaces the event of him entering at his original play location with him entering at the Baron Pit if it was created.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-370" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-370" aria-label="Link to rule 370">370.</a> A Replacement Effect can alter the typical flow of play, including other cards' executions.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-370-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-370-1" aria-label="Link to rule 370.1">370.1.</a> Replacement Effects apply to any event or instruction that qualifies for their application. A Replacement Effect will specify the circumstances by which an event or instruction will qualify to be replaced.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-370-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-370-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 370.1.a">370.1.a.</a> An event is the singular moment that results from a Game Action being performed or from a Game Object changing state.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: The moment that results from a unit being killed is an event that can be referenced by game effects, or even skipped entirely.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: The moment that results from a unit becoming Mighty is an event that can be referenced by game effects, or even skipped entirely.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-370-1-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-370-1-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 370.1.a.1">370.1.a.1.</a> Modifying or replacing an event is the same as modifying or replacing that Game Action or change in state that generated that event.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Zhonya’s Hourglass reads in part “If a friendly unit would die, kill this instead. Heal that unit, exhaust it, and recall it.” A unit’s death being replaced by Zhonya’s Hourglass is the same as the kill action that caused that death not occurring.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A card reads in part “The next time an enemy unit would become Mighty this turn, banish it instead. Its controller plays a 3 [M] Mech unit token to its location.” A unit becoming Mighty being replaced by this effect means the unit never became Mighty—no effects that trigger on units becoming Mighty will trigger.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-370-1-a-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-370-1-a-2" aria-label="Link to rule 370.1.a.2">370.1.a.2.</a> An event can occur simultaneously with other events only when those events are all the result of the same Game Action or change in state occurring.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A spell reads in part “Kill up to two units at battlefields.” When that spell resolves, the units targeted are killed simultaneously because their deaths result from the same game action.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A spell reads in part “Kill a friendly unit. If you do, kill an enemy unit with no more Might than it.” When that spell resolves, the units are not killed simultaneously. There are two kill game actions being performed in the instructions of the spell. The friendly unit is killed first, followed by the enemy unit.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-370-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-370-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 370.1.b">370.1.b.</a> When a Replacement Effect applies, it replaces the qualifying event with one or more Game Actions or events, or the qualifying instruction with another instruction.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-370-1-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-370-1-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 370.1.b.1">370.1.b.1.</a> In the case of Replacement Effects that describe a game action to occur “as” an event occurs, the described event is replaced by that same event plus the game action being performed.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Undertitan is a unit that reads in part “As I’m revealed from your deck, [Add] [2].” The event of Undertitan being revealed from your deck is replaced by Undertitan being revealed from your deck and adding [2] Energy to your Rune Pool.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-370-1-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-370-1-c" aria-label="Link to rule 370.1.c">370.1.c.</a> Replacement Effects are applied before any qualifying event has actually occurred.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-370-1-c-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-370-1-c-1" aria-label="Link to rule 370.1.c.1">370.1.c.1.</a> If the Replacement Effect of a Game Object applies to an event that involves that same Game Object while it is in a secret zone, it applies to and replaces such an event simultaneously with that event occurring instead. The replacing event still occurs in the order specified by the instruction that generated it, and the original event does not occur if it is replaced.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Nocturne, Horrifying reads in part “As you look at or reveal me from the top of your deck, you may banish me. If you do, you may play me for [A].” Because Nocturne’s replacement effect replaces an event that applies while he is in a secret zone, it applies to the event of him being looked at or revealed from the top of a player’s Main Deck simultaneously with that event occurring.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A card reads in part “If you would burn this, you may burn this and an additional card instead.” Because the replacement effect applies to the card while it is in a secret zone, it applies simultaneously with the event occurring. Another card reads “If a player would put a card into their trash from their Main Deck, banish it instead.” This replacement effect is active on the board, and thus applies before the qualifying event occurs. Because these replacement effects are applied at different timings, they are applied in order. The card will be banished first, and subsequently its replacement effect will have nothing to replace.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-370-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-370-2" aria-label="Link to rule 370.2">370.2.</a> A Replacement Effect can only be applied once to an event, or to any Game Actions or events that replace that event.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player plays a spell that reads “gear you control become 1 [M] gear units this turn.” They control two copies of Zhonya’s Hourglass when the spell resolves. If one of those copies is killed, both of their Replacement Effects will be applied. Whichever is applied first, that Replacement Effect can’t be applied again. When it is applied, it kills its source, which creates an event the other can apply its Replacement Effect to. Once they’ve both applied their Replacement Effect to the original death event and the event that replaced it, they cannot go any further. At that point, whichever Zhonya’s Hourglass applied its Replacement Effect last will die.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-370-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-370-3" aria-label="Link to rule 370.3">370.3.</a> If a Game Object has a Replacement Effect that is active in a specific zone, it is evaluated and subsequently applied if it enters that zone before an event occurs that it could replace.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A unit that reads “if a unit you control would die, you may banish me from your trash instead. If you do, heal that unit, exhaust it and recall it.” The first unit dies simultaneously with a 1 [M] Recruit token. It does not enter the trash before the Recruit dies, so it will not be able to replace its death.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-370-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-370-4" aria-label="Link to rule 370.4">370.4.</a> A Game Object can apply its Replacement Effects to any qualifying events that occur simultaneously with it leaving the zone that its Replacement Effect is active in.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Soraka, Wanderer has a Replacement Effect that reads, “If another unit you control here would die, if it has less Might than me, instead heal it, exhaust it, and recall it.” Soraka’s replacement can be applied to any qualifying event that occurs simultaneously with her leaving the board, including to units that die simultaneously with her.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-371" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-371" aria-label="Link to rule 371">371.</a> Some Replacement Effects will begin with “once each turn,” or “N times each turn.”</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-371-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-371-1" aria-label="Link to rule 371.1">371.1.</a> These Replacement Effects may only be applied to the specified number of events each turn. Once they have been applied to that many events, they cannot be applied to a later event in the same turn.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-371-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-371-2" aria-label="Link to rule 371.2">371.2.</a> If the Replacement Effect says a player “may” apply the Replacement Effect, the player has the choice of whether or not to apply it.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-371-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-371-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 371.2.a">371.2.a.</a> When an event the Replacement Effect could apply to occurs, the player who controls the Replacement Effect may choose to apply it to the event.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-371-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-371-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 371.2.b">371.2.b.</a> If they do not, it has not been applied this turn.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Zilean, Time mage reads “Once each turn, if you would play a token unit while I'm at a battlefield, you may play that token and an additional copy of it instead.” When his controller plays a token, they can choose not to apply the replacement effect to that event. If they do, they can choose to apply it to a later event of a token being played.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-372" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-372" aria-label="Link to rule 372">372.</a> If more than one Replacement Effect applies to the same event being executed, then the controller of the object being acted on determines the order the Replacement Effects will apply.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-372-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-372-1" aria-label="Link to rule 372.1">372.1.</a> If it is a player being acted on, that player decides the order the Replacement Effects will apply.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-372-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-372-2" aria-label="Link to rule 372.2">372.2.</a> If the affected object is an Uncontrolled Battlefield then the Current Turn Player decides the order the Replacement Effects will apply.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-373" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-373" aria-label="Link to rule 373">373.</a> If more than one event occurs simultaneously that Replacement Effects could apply to, each event is treated separately and individually for the purposes of Replacement Effects, and Replacement Effects with the same controller are applied in the order of their controller’s choosing.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Two units controlled by the same player die in the same cleanup. That player also controls Zhonya’s Hourglass. They must decide which event to apply Zhonya’s Hourglass to first.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-373-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-373-1" aria-label="Link to rule 373.1">373.1.</a> Although these events are simultaneous, the applied Replacement Effects are ordered. If multiple applied Replacement Effects with different controllers would execute simultaneously, they execute in turn order.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-373-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-373-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 373.1.a">373.1.a.</a> When executing Replacement Effects, the Game Actions that comprise their instructions are performed before any simultaneous unmodified events.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Two units die simultaneously. One of those units has their death replaced by being healed, exhausted, and recalled. The healing, exhausting, and recalling of that unit will be performed before the other dies.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-373-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-373-2" aria-label="Link to rule 373.2">373.2.</a> When applying Replacement Effects to events that occur simultaneously, each Replacement Effect may only be applied in one sequence, to any number of events that are qualified to be replaced.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Soraka, Wanderer reads “If another unit you control here would die, if it has less Might than me, instead heal it, exhaust it, and recall it.” Soraka dies simultaneously with two 1 [M] Recruit tokens at the same battlefield and two 1 [M] Recruit tokens in base. Soraka has a Guardian Angel attached to her when she dies, which appends “If I would die, kill Guardian Angel instead. Heal me, exhaust me, and recall me” to Soraka’s rules text. There are several possible ways to order the Replacement Effects being applied to the various events: If Soraka’s Replacement Effect is applied first, it saves the Recruits at the same battlefield as her but not the Recruits in base. If the Replacement Effect appended by Guardian Angel then saves Soraka, she cannot apply her Replacement Effect to the Recruits in base as her Replacement Effect has already been applied to an event simultaneous with it dying. If the Replacement Effect appended by Guardian Angel is applied first, it saves Soraka and recalls her - then when Soraka’s Replacement Effect is applied, it can only save the Recruits in base.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-373-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-373-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 373.2.a">373.2.a.</a> A sequence of Replacement Effects is an uninterrupted series of applications to a set of simultaneous events.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-373-2-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-373-2-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 373.2.a.1">373.2.a.1.</a> A Replacement Effect that replaces an event or Game Action that is part of another Replacement Effect will not interrupt the sequence of the replaced Replacement Effect’s application.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-374" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-374" aria-label="Link to rule 374">374.</a> A Replacement Effect’s controller is the player that controls the source of the Replacement Effect.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-375" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-375" aria-label="Link to rule 375">375.</a> If an event that a Replacement Effect applies to would be modified by the Game Effect that generated that event, or the results of that event would be modified by a Game Action from a linked ability that references the replaced event, the Replacement Effect will inherit those modifications.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Treasure Hunter reads “When I move, play a Gold gear token exhausted.” A Replacement Effect that says “if you would play a token gear, play that token and an additional copy instead” is applied to the event of the Gold gear token being played. The additional copy will also be exhausted, as it inherits the “exhausted” modification.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Another Replacement Effect says “if you would play a token, draw 1 instead.” The modification from Treasure Hunter’s ability cannot apply, so we ignore it.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A spell reads “play a ready 3 [M] Mech token. Then do this: Give it Temporary.” A Replacement Effect that says “if you would play a unit token, play that token and a 1 [M] Recruit token instead” is applied to the event of the Mech token being made. The Recruit token enters ready and is given Temporary.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-376" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-376" aria-label="Link to rule 376">376.</a> Activated Abilities</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-377" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-377" aria-label="Link to rule 377">377.</a> Activated Abilities are repeatable effects with a cost. They follow a process of going onto the chain and resolving, similar to Playing a Card.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-349">349</a>. Playing Cards for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-377-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-377-1" aria-label="Link to rule 377.1">377.1.</a> Activated Abilities are recognized by the presence of a ":" in the text of the card, preceded by a cost and succeeded by an effect.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: "[2]: Draw 1" is an activated ability. The cost is 2 energy. The effect is to draw 1 card.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-377-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-377-2" aria-label="Link to rule 377.2">377.2.</a> Card text will refer to activating Activated Abilities with the word “use” or “play.”</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-377-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-377-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 377.2.a">377.2.a.</a> If “using” or “playing” an Activated Ability is part of a trigger condition, that condition is fulfilled when the Activated Ability resolves.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-377-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-377-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 377.2.b">377.2.b.</a> If an Activated Ability has a condition on “using” or “playing” it, that condition must be true in order to activate the ability in question.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Ultrasoft Poro reads “[E]: Play two 1 [M] Bird unit tokens with [Deflect]. Use this ability only while I'm at a battlefield.” In order to activate the ability, Ultrasoft Poro must be located at a battlefield.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-377-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-377-3" aria-label="Link to rule 377.3">377.3.</a> Activated Abilities use the chain.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-377-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-377-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 377.3.a">377.3.a.</a> Declare activation of the Ability.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-377-3-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-377-3-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 377.3.a.1">377.3.a.1.</a> The ability goes on the chain but has no card to represent it, so players need to take note that it is now a Closed State.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-377-3-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-377-3-b" aria-label="Link to rule 377.3.b">377.3.b.</a> Proceed with executing the Chain.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-377-3-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-377-3-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 377.3.b.1">377.3.b.1.</a> Follow the steps of “Playing or Activating Abilities” in rule <a href="#rule-398">398</a>. This ability will become a Pending Chain Item.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-377-3-b-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-377-3-b-2" aria-label="Link to rule 377.3.b.2">377.3.b.2.</a> Opponents have an opportunity to respond, as appropriate, as if a card was played onto the chain.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-377-3-b-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-377-3-b-3" aria-label="Link to rule 377.3.b.3">377.3.b.3.</a> If no further action is taken, execute the Activated Ability.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-378" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-378" aria-label="Link to rule 378">378.</a> The player who controls the source of the Activated Ability decides when and whether to activate the ability.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-378-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-378-1" aria-label="Link to rule 378.1">378.1.</a> If the source of the Activated Ability is uncontrolled, and it isn’t a Battlefield, the owner of the source decides when and whether to activate the ability.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-378-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-378-2" aria-label="Link to rule 378.2">378.2.</a> Some activated abilities specify that one or more players other than the controller or owner of their source can activate them. Those players decide when and whether to activate the ability.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-379" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-379" aria-label="Link to rule 379">379.</a> Activated abilities are present on Game Objects and some Spells.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-380" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-380" aria-label="Link to rule 380">380.</a> Can primarily be activated while on the Board.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-381" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-381" aria-label="Link to rule 381">381.</a> All Activated Abilities can only be activated on the appropriate Player's Turn and during an Open State.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-381-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-381-1" aria-label="Link to rule 381.1">381.1.</a> If the source of the Activated Ability is controlled by a player, that is the controller’s Main Phase during an Open State.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-381-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-381-2" aria-label="Link to rule 381.2">381.2.</a> If the source of the Activated Ability is uncontrolled and it isn’t a Battlefield, that is the owner’s Main Phase during an Open State.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-381-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-381-3" aria-label="Link to rule 381.3">381.3.</a> If the Activated Ability specifies that one or more players other than the controller or owner of the source can activate it, that is during the activating player's Main Phase during an Open State.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-382" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-382" aria-label="Link to rule 382">382.</a> Triggered Abilities</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383" aria-label="Link to rule 383">383.</a> Triggered Abilities are repeatable effects that happen when a Condition is met.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-1" aria-label="Link to rule 383.1">383.1.</a> Triggered Abilities can usually be recognized by the word "when" followed by a game action or event; the word "at" followed by a point in time during the turn sequence; or the phrase “the [Nth] time” followed by a game action or event.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Examples: "When you conquer here, you may spend a buff to draw 1."</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>"At the end of your turn, ready 2 runes."</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>“The first time I move each turn, you may ready something else that's exhausted.”</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 383.1.a">383.1.a.</a> The phrases that identify triggered abilities do not always appear at the beginning of sentences or abilities.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 383.1.b">383.1.b.</a> If an ability triggers “the [Nth] time” something happens and that trigger condition is met multiple times simultaneously, the ability’s controller picks one of those instances to serve as the trigger condition. The ability triggers only once, due to the chosen condition.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Wraith of Echoes reads “The first time another friendly unit dies each turn, draw 1.” That ability hasn’t triggered yet this turn. Two other friendly units die simultaneously (say, due to combat damage). The Wraith’s controller chooses one of those deaths to trigger Wraith’s ability.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-2" aria-label="Link to rule 383.2">383.2.</a> Triggered Abilities have a Condition and an Effect.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 383.2.a">383.2.a.</a> The Condition is the clause with When, At, or the Nth Time.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-2-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-2-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 383.2.a.1">383.2.a.1.</a> Any additional conditional statement immediately after the Condition must be true in order for the Condition to be fulfilled. Such a conditional statement is part of the Trigger Condition and not the Effect.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Sona, Harmonious reads “At the end of your turn, if I'm at a battlefield, ready up to 4 friendly runes.” Her Trigger Ability’s Condition will be fulfilled in the Ending Step, but the Triggered Ability will only be placed on the chain if she is located at a battlefield when the Condition is fulfilled. If she is removed in reaction to the triggered ability, it will still resolve.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Loose Cannon reads “At the start of your Beginning Phase, draw 1 if you have one or fewer cards in your hand.” The “if you have one or fewer cards in your hand” conditional statement is not immediately after the trigger condition, so it is part of the effect and not the condition.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 383.2.b">383.2.b.</a> The Effect is the Instructions that are not part of the Condition.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-2-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-2-c" aria-label="Link to rule 383.2.c">383.2.c.</a> The Condition of a Trigger is evaluated after a potentially inciting event has been processed, unless the event the Condition checks is the source of the triggered ability leaving the board.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-2-c-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-2-c-1" aria-label="Link to rule 383.2.c.1">383.2.c.1.</a> If a Game Object has a Triggered Ability that is active in a specific zone, it is evaluated and subsequently triggered if it enters that zone at the same time that its Trigger’s condition is met.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Immortal Phoenix says “When you kill a unit with a spell, you may pay [1][C] to play me from your trash.” This ability triggers if Immortal Phoenix is in your trash immediately after you kill a unit with a spell, even if the unit you killed with a spell was that Immortal Phoenix.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-2-c-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-2-c-2" aria-label="Link to rule 383.2.c.2">383.2.c.2.</a> A Game Object will not be able to successfully evaluate its Trigger Condition, however, if it leaves the zone that its Trigger is active from at the same time that its Trigger is satisfied.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Viktor, Leader says “When another non-Recruit unit you control dies, play a 1 [M] Recruit unit token into your base.” This ability triggers if Viktor is on the board immediately after another non-Recruit unit you control dies. It does not trigger if Viktor and another non-Recruit unit you control die during the same game action (for instance, if they are both killed in the same Cleanup due to the damage dealt by Unchecked Power).</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-2-c-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-2-c-3" aria-label="Link to rule 383.2.c.3">383.2.c.3.</a> If a Game Object has a Triggered Ability whose condition is the Game Object leaving the board, such a Triggered Ability is processed immediately before the Game Object changes zones.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: If a game object has a deathknell ability, which triggers on the game object dying, it will be processed and placed on the chain just prior to the game object being placed in the trash, during the event of the game object dying.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Treasure Trove has a triggered ability that triggers as it “leaves the board.” Such a triggered ability is processed and placed on the chain just before Treasure Trove completes the action of leaving the board by being moved to another zone.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-2-c-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-2-c-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 383.2.c.3.a">383.2.c.3.a.</a> Triggered Abilities that trigger from Game Objects leaving the board trigger and are placed on the chain after any Replacement Effects are processed. If a Replacement Effect causes the Game Object to not leave the board in the appropriate way, the relevant Triggered Abilities will not trigger.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: If a unit’s death is replaced by an event of that unit being healed, exhausted, and recalled, that unit’s Deathknell abilities will not trigger, as the unit’s death event is replaced and does not occur.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: If Treasure Trove’s being killed is replaced with it being banished, it will still trigger just before the banish action is executed.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-2-c-3-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-2-c-3-b" aria-label="Link to rule 383.2.c.3.b">383.2.c.3.b.</a> When the Triggered Ability is being processed, note any information about the source of the Triggered Ability or the state of the game as needed in order to process the resulting Triggered Ability.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A unit reads in part “When I leave the board, deal 2 to a unit where I was.” Its controller plays Retreat on the unit, returning it to hand. Before the unit is returned to hand, the triggered ability is placed on the chain and the necessary information about that unit is noted. When the triggered ability is being finalized, we can identify the former location of the unit in question with the noted information.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-3" aria-label="Link to rule 383.3">383.3.</a> When a Condition is met, a Triggered Ability behaves like an Activated Ability and is placed on the Chain.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 383.3.a">383.3.a.</a> If a Triggered Ability says “you may”or “they may” as the first part of its Effect, the controller of its source will choose whether or not to perform the Triggered Ability during finalization.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Tideturner reads “When you play me, you may choose a unit you control at another location. Move me to its location and it to my original location.” This “you may” appears as the first part of its effect, so the choice represents whether or not to perform the triggered ability.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-3-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-3-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 383.3.a.1">383.3.a.1.</a> The decision of “may” when it appears in this way is solely whether or not to perform said triggered ability.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-3-a-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-3-a-2" aria-label="Link to rule 383.3.a.2">383.3.a.2.</a> If the controller of the Triggered Ability chooses not to perform that Triggered Ability during finalization, it is removed from the chain and considered to have not triggered.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-3-a-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-3-a-3" aria-label="Link to rule 383.3.a.3">383.3.a.3.</a> If “you may” or “they may” appears in any later part of the Effect of a triggered ability, it is decided on resolution.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Ornn, Blacksmith reads “When you play me or when I hold, look at the top 4 cards of your Main Deck. You may reveal a gear from among them and draw it. Then recycle the rest.” This “you may” does not appear as the first part of its effect, so the choice is made on resolution. The ability is always finalized to the chain.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-3-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-3-b" aria-label="Link to rule 383.3.b">383.3.b.</a> If a Triggered Ability contains a cost within instructions at the beginning of the effect or immediately following the “you may” or “they may” that appears as the first part of the effect, that cost is treated as the base cost of the Triggered Ability.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Ekko, Recurrent reads “[Deathknell][&gt;] Recycle me to ready your runes.” In this case, “recycle me to ready your runes” is a cost within instructions that appears at the beginning of the effect of the ability, and thus “recycle me” is taken as the base cost of the triggered ability.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Insightful Investigator reads “When you play me, choose an opponent. They reveal their hand. You may pay 2 XP to choose a card from their hand. If you do, they discard that card and draw 1.” The “pay 2 XP” is a cost within instructions, but because it does not appear in the first part of the effect, it is not taken as the base cost of the triggered ability. Paying 2 XP is performed on resolution.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-3-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-3-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 383.3.b.1">383.3.b.1.</a> The cost must be paid in order to finalize the Triggered Ability to the Chain.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-3-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-3-c" aria-label="Link to rule 383.3.c">383.3.c.</a> Triggered Abilities can be put on the Chain during Closed States or Open States on any player's turn.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-3-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-3-d" aria-label="Link to rule 383.3.d">383.3.d.</a> If more than one Triggered Ability is Triggered simultaneously, then the player that controls the Abilities selects the order to place them on the Chain.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-3-d-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-3-d-1" aria-label="Link to rule 383.3.d.1">383.3.d.1.</a> If multiple players separately control Triggered Abilities that are Triggered simultaneously, then starting with the Turn Player and proceeding in Turn Order, each player orders their Triggered Abilities on the Chain.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-3-e" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-3-e" aria-label="Link to rule 383.3.e">383.3.e.</a> Some Triggered Abilities will trigger “once each turn,” or “N times each turn.”</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-3-e-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-3-e-1" aria-label="Link to rule 383.3.e.1">383.3.e.1.</a> Such a Triggered Ability will only be performed the specified number of times each turn. If its trigger condition would be fulfilled and it has already been performed that many times, it does not trigger.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-3-e-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-3-e-2" aria-label="Link to rule 383.3.e.2">383.3.e.2.</a> If the Triggered Ability says “you may” or “they may” as the first part of its effect, its controller has the choice of whether or not it is performed.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-3-e-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-3-e-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 383.3.e.2.a">383.3.e.2.a.</a> During finalization of the Triggered Ability, the player who controls the Triggered Ability may choose to perform it.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-3-e-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-3-e-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 383.3.e.2.b">383.3.e.2.b.</a> If they do not, it is removed from the chain.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player controls a unit that reads in part “Once each turn, when an enemy unit dies, you may banish it.” When an enemy Recruit token dies, the triggered ability goes on the chain. If they choose not to perform the ability on finalization, it is removed from the chain. When a Stalwart Poro dies later in the turn, they can choose to trigger it then.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4">383.4.</a> Some Conditions are commonly used and structured in a way that explicitly defines their use and other properties of the Effect that is associated with it.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.a">383.4.a.</a> Play Effects are Triggered Abilities whose Condition includes the Permanent that has the Play Effect being played to the board.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.a.1">383.4.a.1.</a> These are commonly structured as “When you play me…” for Units and “When you play this…” for Gear.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-a-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-a-2" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.a.2">383.4.a.2.</a> These Triggered Abilities are put on the Chain as Pending Items after the Permanent these effects correspond to is finalized and enters the board.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-a-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-a-3" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.a.3">383.4.a.3.</a> These Triggered Abilities can be referred to as Play Effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-a-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-a-4" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.a.4">383.4.a.4.</a> Abilities that trigger when another object is played are not considered Play Effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-b" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.b">383.4.b.</a> Targeting Effects are Triggered Abilities whose Condition includes a Game Object becoming targeted.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.b.1">383.4.b.1.</a> These are commonly structured as “When you choose me …” or “When you choose a [Game Object] …”</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-b-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-b-2" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.b.2">383.4.b.2.</a> These Triggered Abilities are put on the Chain as Pending Items after a spell or ability targets an appropriate Game Object.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-b-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-b-3" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.b.3">383.4.b.3.</a> Although these abilities say “choose” in their Condition, they trigger specifically when an appropriate Game Object is Targeted.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-355-6">355.6</a>. Targeting for more information on what counts as Targeting.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-b-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-b-4" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.b.4">383.4.b.4.</a> These Triggered Abilities can be referred to as Targeting Effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-c" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.c">383.4.c.</a> Conquer Effects are Triggered Abilities whose Condition includes a Unit participating in, and successfully Conquering a Battlefield.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-c-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-c-1" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.c.1">383.4.c.1.</a> These are commonly structured as “When I conquer…” and “When you conquer…”</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-c-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-c-2" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.c.2">383.4.c.2.</a> This category of Triggered Abilities encompasses only those that are triggered from Units that were present during the Conquer action, or Abilities that reference the player that performed the Conquer action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-c-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-c-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.c.2.a">383.4.c.2.a.</a> The Conquer Abilities of Units are put on the Chain as Pending Items after the Unit(s) these effects correspond to are present at a Battlefield when a player gains control of it and gains 1 Victory Point from Conquering.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-c-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-c-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.c.2.b">383.4.c.2.b.</a> The Conquer Abilities of anything that references the player Conquering are put on the Chain as Pending Items when the Condition that the player that controls the triggering source has performed a Conquer.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-c-2-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-c-2-c" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.c.2.c">383.4.c.2.c.</a> If the act of gaining one point from Conquering is negated or replaced in any way, the Conquer Effect will still trigger.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-c-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-c-3" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.c.3">383.4.c.3.</a> These Triggered Abilities can be referred to as Conquer Effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-d" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.d">383.4.d.</a> Hold Effects are Triggered Abilities whose Condition includes a Unit being present at a Battlefield during the Beginning phase when a player scores Victory Points from Holding.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-d-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-d-1" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.d.1">383.4.d.1.</a> These are commonly structured as “When I hold…” or “When you hold…”</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-d-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-d-2" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.d.2">383.4.d.2.</a> This category of Triggered Abilities encompasses only those that are triggered from Units that were present during the Hold action, or Abilities that reference the player that performed the Hold action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-d-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-d-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.d.2.a">383.4.d.2.a.</a> The Hold Abilities of Units are put on the Chain as Pending Items after the Unit these effects correspond to are present at a Battlefield when a player maintains control of it and Gains 1 Victory Point during their Beginning Phase from Holding.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-d-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-d-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.d.2.b">383.4.d.2.b.</a> The Hold Abilities of anything that references the player Holding are put on the Chain as Pending Items when the Condition that the player that controls the triggering source has performed a Hold.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-d-2-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-d-2-c" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.d.2.c">383.4.d.2.c.</a> If the act of gaining one point from Holding is negated or replaced in any way, the Hold Effect will still trigger.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-d-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-d-3" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.d.3">383.4.d.3.</a> These Triggered Abilities can be referred to as Hold Effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-e" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-e" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.e">383.4.e.</a> Attack Triggers are Triggered Abilities that trigger when a Unit or Player gains the Attacker designation for the first time during a combat.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-e-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-e-1" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.e.1">383.4.e.1.</a> These are commonly structured as “When I attack…” or “When you attack…”</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-e-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-e-2" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.e.2">383.4.e.2.</a> These Triggered Abilities are put on the Chain as Pending Items after the Unit these effects correspond to gains the Attacker designation during Combat.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-e-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-e-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.e.2.a">383.4.e.2.a.</a> These triggers will only have their condition checked once per combat, despite a Unit being able to gain and lose the Attacker designation multiple times in the same combat.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-e-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-e-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.e.2.b">383.4.e.2.b.</a> If the trigger condition contains other requirements besides attacking and if those requirements are not fulfilled when the unit gains the Attacker designation, it will not trigger in that combat.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-e-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-e-3" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.e.3">383.4.e.3.</a> These Triggered Abilities can be referred to as Attack Triggers.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-f" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-f" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.f">383.4.f.</a> Defend Triggers are Triggered Abilities that trigger when a Unit or Player gains the Defender designation for the first time during a combat.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-f-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-f-1" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.f.1">383.4.f.1.</a> These are commonly structured as “When I defend…” or “When you defend…”</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-f-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-f-2" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.f.2">383.4.f.2.</a> These Triggered Abilities are put on the Chain as Pending Items after the Unit these effects correspond to gains the Defender designation during Combat.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-f-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-f-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.f.2.a">383.4.f.2.a.</a> These triggers will only have their condition checked once per combat, despite a Unit being able to gain and lose the Defender designation multiple times in the same combat.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-f-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-f-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.f.2.b">383.4.f.2.b.</a> If the trigger condition contains other requirements besides defending and if those requirements are not fulfilled when the unit gains the Defender designation, it will not trigger in that combat.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-f-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-f-3" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.f.3">383.4.f.3.</a> These Triggered Abilities can be referred to as Defend Triggers.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-g" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-g" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.g">383.4.g.</a> Some effects may instruct a player to “activate” one of these named triggered abilities.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-g-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-g-1" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.g.1">383.4.g.1.</a> To do so, that player checks the condition of all of the specified effects, as if they had fulfilled the named part of the condition.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Reckoner’s Arena reads “When you hold here, activate the conquer effects of units here.” For each unit at the battlefield, you will check the trigger condition of their conquer effects to see if the condition has been fulfilled, treating the conquer portion of the condition as having been fulfilled. If all of the conditions are fulfilled for a conquer effect, it is placed on the chain as if it had just triggered. If any of the non-conquer parts of the condition are not fulfilled, it will not be placed on the chain.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A spell reads “Activate the play effects of your gear.” For each gear you control, you will treat it as if you had just played the gear and check the other conditions of that gear. If all of the conditions are fulfilled for a play effect, it is placed on the chain as if it had just triggered.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-383-4-g-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-383-4-g-2" aria-label="Link to rule 383.4.g.2">383.4.g.2.</a> The named part of the condition is fulfilled, but no actual such event occurs.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Red Brambleback is a unit that reads in part “Your conquer effects for conquering here trigger an additional time.” Reckoner’s Arena is a battlefield that reads “When you hold here, activate the conquer effects of units here.” The conquer effects that were activated in this way were not the result of a conquer occurring, so they will not trigger an additional time from Red Brambleback’s passive ability if it is located there.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-384" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-384" aria-label="Link to rule 384">384.</a> Presence on Permanents</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-384-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-384-1" aria-label="Link to rule 384.1">384.1.</a> Typically active while on the Board.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-384-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-384-2" aria-label="Link to rule 384.2">384.2.</a> Triggered Abilities of Permanents are only able to have their Conditions evaluated while on the Board.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-385" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-385" aria-label="Link to rule 385">385.</a> Presence on Cards outside of the Board</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-385-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-385-1" aria-label="Link to rule 385.1">385.1.</a> Triggered Abilities on cards outside of the Board rely on the Information Level of the zone they are in.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-385-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-385-2" aria-label="Link to rule 385.2">385.2.</a> Triggered Abilities outside of the Board will self-describe their context.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: The triggered ability "When you conquer, you may discard 1 to return this from your trash to your hand." triggers while the card it's on is in the trash, and not anywhere else.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-386" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-386" aria-label="Link to rule 386">386.</a> Some Replacement Effects, Passive Abilities, and Triggered Abilities will begin with “once each turn,” “N times each turn,” “once on each of your turns,” or “N times on each of your turns.”</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-386-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-386-1" aria-label="Link to rule 386.1">386.1.</a> Such Replacement Effects and Triggered Abilities will only be applied to the specified number of events each turn, or performed the specified number of times each turn. Once they have been performed that many times or been applied to that many events, they cannot trigger or be applied to a later event in the same turn.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-386-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-386-2" aria-label="Link to rule 386.2">386.2.</a> If such a Triggered Ability says “you may,” or “they may,” as the first part of its effect, or such a Replacement Effect says a player “may” replace the qualifying event, the controller of the ability has the choice of whether or not to perform or apply it.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-386-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-386-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 386.2.a">386.2.a.</a> For Replacement Effects, when an event the Replacement Effect could apply to occurs, the player who controls the Replacement Effect may choose not to apply it to the event. For Triggered Abilities, during Finalization of the Triggered Ability, the player who controls the Triggered Ability may choose not to perform it.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-386-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-386-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 386.2.b">386.2.b.</a> If they do not apply the Replacement Effect, it has not been applied this turn. If they do not perform the Triggered Ability, it is removed from the chain.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Zilean, Time mage reads “Once each turn, if you would play a token unit while I'm at a battlefield, you may play that token and an additional copy of it instead.” When his controller plays a token, they can choose not to apply the replacement effect to that event. If they do, they can choose to apply it to a later event of a token being played.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player controls a unit that reads in part “Once each turn, when an enemy unit dies, you may banish it.” When an enemy Recruit token dies, the triggered ability goes on the chain. If they choose not to perform the ability on finalization, it is removed from the chain. When a Stalwart Poro dies later in the turn, they can choose to perform it then.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-386-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-386-3" aria-label="Link to rule 386.3">386.3.</a> For such Passive Abilities, they will be active until they have been applied to the specified number of events, after which point they will become inactive.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-386-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-386-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 386.3.a">386.3.a.</a> If such a Passive Ability says “you may” or “they may” as the first part of the ability after the “once each turn,” the controller of the source of the Passive Ability chooses whether or not to apply the ability to the event in question. If they do not, the Passive Ability is Ignored during that event.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-764">764</a>. Ignoring Effects for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-387" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-387" aria-label="Link to rule 387">387.</a> Reflexive Triggers</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-388" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-388" aria-label="Link to rule 388">388.</a> Reflexive Triggers are a type of Triggered Ability that create one or more Chain Items when their condition is met.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-388-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-388-1" aria-label="Link to rule 388.1">388.1.</a> Reflexive Triggers can be recognized by the phrase “Do this:” or “Do one of the following:”.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-388-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-388-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 388.1.a">388.1.a.</a> “Do this” can be followed by “N times.” The Reflexive Trigger will thus be added to the chain N times when its condition is met.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-388-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-388-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 388.1.b">388.1.b.</a> “Do one of the following” can instead be “Do X of the following” or “Do the following”. The effect will specify which of the related Reflexive Triggers will go on the chain when the condition is met.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-388-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-388-2" aria-label="Link to rule 388.2">388.2.</a> Reflexive Triggers will be preceded by their conditions, if any. If no condition is present in the ability then the Reflexive Trigger will always be added to the Chain.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-388-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-388-3" aria-label="Link to rule 388.3">388.3.</a> If present, the Condition of a Reflexive Trigger will follow the same format as a Triggered Ability.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-388-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-388-4" aria-label="Link to rule 388.4">388.4.</a> If a Reflexive Trigger says “you may” or “they may” as the first part of its Effect, the controller of its source will choose whether or not to perform the Reflexive Trigger during finalization.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Death From Below is a spell that reads in part “Then, if it had 3 might or less, do this: You may play this from your trash for [A].” The reflexive trigger is added to the chain when the spell is resolving, and then on finalization of the reflexive trigger its controller will decide if they want to perform the effect. If they don’t, it is removed from the chain.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-389" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-389" aria-label="Link to rule 389">389.</a> Reflexive Triggers use the Chain.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-389-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-389-1" aria-label="Link to rule 389.1">389.1.</a> A new ability is created and added to the chain as a Pending Item.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-399">399</a>. Playing or Activating Abilities for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-389-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-389-2" aria-label="Link to rule 389.2">389.2.</a> If a Reflexive Trigger creates more than one Pending Item it creates them all in order, but does not go beyond the first step of adding them to the Chain.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-399">399</a>. Playing or Activating Abilities for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-390" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-390" aria-label="Link to rule 390">390.</a> Delayed Abilities</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-391" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-391" aria-label="Link to rule 391">391.</a> Delayed Abilities are a type of Ability that specifies a window of applicability during which they are active.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-391-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-391-1" aria-label="Link to rule 391.1">391.1.</a> Delayed Abilities can be any other type of Ability, and contain all of the properties of that type in addition to the properties of Delayed Abilities.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-391-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-391-2" aria-label="Link to rule 391.2">391.2.</a> Delayed Triggers are Triggered Abilities that can be recognized by describing a specific time of the turn, or by structuring a Triggered Ability with a specific frame of time as a restriction.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-391-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-391-3" aria-label="Link to rule 391.3">391.3.</a> Delayed Replacements are Replacement Effects that can be recognized by specifying the effect they are replacing at a specific time, or “the [Nth] time” in the description of the effect as it resolves.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-391-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-391-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 391.3.a">391.3.a.</a> Some Delayed Replacements take the form “then recycle it,” or “then banish it,” in reference to chain items being played by linked abilities or instructions. These are short for “if it would leave the chain after becoming a finalized chain item, and leaving the chain wasn’t instructed by its own execution, perform the specified game action instead.”</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-391-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-391-4" aria-label="Link to rule 391.4">391.4.</a> Delayed Passive Abilities are Passive Abilities that are applicable only during a specified window of time. The time that the Delayed Passive Ability applies will be recognized in the effect that initiates it.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-391-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-391-5" aria-label="Link to rule 391.5">391.5.</a> Delayed Linked Abilities are Linked Abilities that are generated by another Ability and reference that Ability or Game Objects it affects instead of a window of time.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-391-5-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-391-5-a" aria-label="Link to rule 391.5.a">391.5.a.</a> If that Ability affects a Game Object, the Delayed Linked Ability’s window will be as long as that Game Object is in an appropriate zone.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-391-5-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-391-5-b" aria-label="Link to rule 391.5.b">391.5.b.</a> If the Delayed Linked Ability references the source of the abilities, its window will be as long as the source is in an appropriate zone.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-391-5-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-391-5-c" aria-label="Link to rule 391.5.c">391.5.c.</a> The appropriate zones for these Delayed Linked Abilities will be determined implicitly by the Linked Ability that generated them.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-391-5-c-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-391-5-c-1" aria-label="Link to rule 391.5.c.1">391.5.c.1.</a> If the Linked Ability causes the affected Game Objects to change zone to a non- board zone, the appropriate zone for the Delayed Linked Ability will be the zone the Game Object is moved to.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-391-5-c-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-391-5-c-2" aria-label="Link to rule 391.5.c.2">391.5.c.2.</a> Otherwise, the appropriate zone is whatever zone the Linked Ability triggered or was played from, or whatever zone the affected Game Object is located in when it is affected.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-392" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-392" aria-label="Link to rule 392">392.</a> Delayed Abilities will resolve or be active just like the ability they augment, but only during the specified time in the effect that created the Delayed Ability.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Ravenborn Tome reads “The next spell you play this turn deals 1 Bonus Damage.” This is a Delayed Passive Ability that passively adds 1 Bonus Damage to just the next spell played. The next spell is a specific time, and the 1 Bonus Damage is a passive ability.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Noxian Guillotine reads “Choose a unit. Kill it the next time it takes damage this turn.” When the chosen unit takes damage is the specified time, and killing it is the condition for a Delayed Triggered Ability.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-393" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-393" aria-label="Link to rule 393">393.</a> Delayed Abilities are not associated with Units or Gear; they are created by other Abilities or Spells. As such they are executed when their condition and/or specified time occurs regardless of whether the source of the Delayed Ability is still on the board or not.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-394" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-394" aria-label="Link to rule 394">394.</a> Linked Abilities</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-395" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-395" aria-label="Link to rule 395">395.</a> Linked Abilities are a set of Abilities with one or more of the component Abilities referencing the other Abilities in the set.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-395-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-395-1" aria-label="Link to rule 395.1">395.1.</a> Component Abilities can reference other Abilities in the set by means of referencing those Abilities directly or by referencing Game Objects affected by or mentioned in another Ability in the set.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-396" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-396" aria-label="Link to rule 396">396.</a> In order for a set of Abilities to be Linked, they must be present in the printed Effect or Rules Text of the same Game Object, or be granted by the same source to another Game Object.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: The Zero Drive is an Equipment gear whose rules text reads in part “[3][B], Banish this: Play all units banished with this, ignoring their costs.” The Zero Drive’s effect text reads “[Deathnkell][&gt;] Banish me.” The granted Deathknell ability is linked with the Zero Drive’s activated ability.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-397" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-397" aria-label="Link to rule 397">397.</a> Linked Abilities can contain component Abilities of any type.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-398" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-398" aria-label="Link to rule 398">398.</a> A component Linked Ability that references a Game Object affected by another Ability in the set may only interact with Game Objects affected by the Abilities it is Linked with.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: The Zero Drive is an Equipment gear whose rules text reads in part “[3][B], Banish this: Play all units banished with this, ignoring their costs.” Any units banished by effects other than component Linked Abilities in the same set as the activated ability cannot be played when resolving the activated ability.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-399" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-399" aria-label="Link to rule 399">399.</a> Playing or Activating Abilities</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-400" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-400" aria-label="Link to rule 400">400.</a> Playing or activating Abilities follows the same steps of playing cards.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-401" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-401" aria-label="Link to rule 401">401.</a> Abilities when added to the Chain become Pending Items until they complete the steps of Playing.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-401-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-401-1" aria-label="Link to rule 401.1">401.1.</a> When an Ability finishes the steps of playing it becomes a Finalized Chain Item just like a Spell.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-401-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-401-2" aria-label="Link to rule 401.2">401.2.</a> When an Ability with the [Add] action is finalized it resolves immediately, like a Unit or Gear.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-402" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-402" aria-label="Link to rule 402">402.</a> 1. Activate or trigger the Ability</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-402-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-402-1" aria-label="Link to rule 402.1">402.1.</a> Add a Pending Item to the chain representing the Ability that is either being Activated or Triggered. Notably, although this Chain Item will not have a card representing it, this will create a Closed State.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-349">349</a>. Playing Cards for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-402-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-402-2" aria-label="Link to rule 402.2">402.2.</a> If there is currently a game effect being resolved, continue resolving the game effect instead of continuing the following steps.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-403" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-403" aria-label="Link to rule 403">403.</a> 2. Make relevant choices</p>
</div>
