<div id="rule-403-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-403-1" aria-label="Link to rule 403.1">403.1.</a> If the first part of a Triggered Ability’s effect is “you may,” or “they may,” its controller decides whether or not to perform the Triggered Ability now.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-403-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-403-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 403.1.a">403.1.a.</a> If they choose not to perform the Triggered Ability now, it is removed from the chain.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-403-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-403-2" aria-label="Link to rule 403.2">403.2.</a> Make all choices required for this ability, such as targets, modes, or other relevant decisions.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-349">349</a>. Playing Cards for more information</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-403-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-403-3" aria-label="Link to rule 403.3">403.3.</a> If legal options are not available for an Activated Ability, it is not legal to activate it.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-403-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-403-4" aria-label="Link to rule 403.4">403.4.</a> If there are not enough options to make legal choices for a Triggered Ability that has been put on the chain, remove it from the Chain now. It ceases to be a Pending Item but never becomes a Finalized Chain Item.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-403-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-403-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 403.4.a">403.4.a.</a> This is not an Ability being countered.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-403-4-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-403-4-b" aria-label="Link to rule 403.4.b">403.4.b.</a> If there are legal options to choose, the ability’s controller must choose them. They may not decline this stage of playing a Trigger.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-404" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-404" aria-label="Link to rule 404">404.</a> 3. Determine Total Cost</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-404-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-404-1" aria-label="Link to rule 404.1">404.1.</a> Determine the base cost of the Ability.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-404-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-404-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 404.1.a">404.1.a.</a> Activated Abilities will have a cost listed before the “:” in their text.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-404-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-404-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 404.1.b">404.1.b.</a> Triggered Abilities will typically not have a base cost associated with them when placed on the chain due to their conditions.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-404-1-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-404-1-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 404.1.b.1">404.1.b.1.</a> If a Triggered Ability has a cost within instructions (e.g. “[do X] to [do Y]”), the cost is taken as the base cost.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-204-3">204.3</a>. for more information on costs within instructions.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-404-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-404-2" aria-label="Link to rule 404.2">404.2.</a> Apply cost increases and decreases as a result of choices made in the prior step.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-349">349</a>. Playing Cards for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-404-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-404-3" aria-label="Link to rule 404.3">404.3.</a> Apply any other cost increases or decreases as necessary.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-349">349</a>. Playing Cards for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-405" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-405" aria-label="Link to rule 405">405.</a> 4. Pay Costs</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-405-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-405-1" aria-label="Link to rule 405.1">405.1.</a> Pay costs as determined in the prior step.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-349">349</a>. Playing Cards for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-405-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-405-2" aria-label="Link to rule 405.2">405.2.</a> At this stage, players may decline to pay for Triggered Abilities that have incurred a cost. If they do, the ability will cease being a Pending Item and be removed from the Chain. It never becomes a Finalized Chain Item.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-405-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-405-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 405.2.a">405.2.a.</a> This is not an Ability being countered.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-406" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-406" aria-label="Link to rule 406">406.</a> 5. Check Legality</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-406-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-406-1" aria-label="Link to rule 406.1">406.1.</a> Ensure that the Ability’s targets are still legal.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-406-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-406-2" aria-label="Link to rule 406.2">406.2.</a> Ensure that the Ability’s effect would not create an illegal state. If it would, resolve in the same way you would resolve a Card that creates an illegal state.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-349">349</a>. Playing Cards for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-407" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-407" aria-label="Link to rule 407">407.</a> 6. Proceed with Play</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-407-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-407-1" aria-label="Link to rule 407.1">407.1.</a> This Ability is no longer Pending.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-407-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-407-2" aria-label="Link to rule 407.2">407.2.</a> This Ability becomes a Finalized Chain Item.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-407-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-407-3" aria-label="Link to rule 407.3">407.3.</a> If there are other Pending Chain Items on the Chain, their controllers perform the remaining steps of playing now.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-407-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-407-4" aria-label="Link to rule 407.4">407.4.</a> Other players have an opportunity to play Reactions before the resolution of spells and abilities.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-327">327</a>. Chains for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-407-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-407-5" aria-label="Link to rule 407.5">407.5.</a> Otherwise, execute the Ability just like a Spell, then clear the Chain Item from the Chain.</p>
</div>

### 408. Game Actions {#rule-408}


<div id="rule-409" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-409" aria-label="Link to rule 409">409.</a> Game Actions are actions players may perform at any given time during the game.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-410" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-410" aria-label="Link to rule 410">410.</a> A player, unless otherwise specified or prompted, may only perform actions on their turn.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-411" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-411" aria-label="Link to rule 411">411.</a> There are two types of Game Actions:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-411-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-411-1" aria-label="Link to rule 411.1">411.1.</a> Discretionary Actions</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-411-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-411-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 411.1.a">411.1.a.</a> A Discretionary Action is a Game Action that may be performed at any time during a player's turn during a Neutral Open State.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-307">307</a>. States of the Turn for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-411-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-411-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 411.1.b">411.1.b.</a> A player may take any number of Discretionary Actions available to them during their turn.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-411-1-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-411-1-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 411.1.b.1">411.1.b.1.</a> As long as any conditions, if applicable, are met.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-411-1-b-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-411-1-b-2" aria-label="Link to rule 411.1.b.2">411.1.b.2.</a> As long as any costs, if applicable, are paid.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-411-1-b-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-411-1-b-3" aria-label="Link to rule 411.1.b.3">411.1.b.3.</a> As long as it does not result in any forbidden action being taken or game state being created.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player can't use the Standard Move discretionary action to move a unit to a Battlefield that's already occupied by Units controlled by two other players.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-411-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-411-2" aria-label="Link to rule 411.2">411.2.</a> Limited Actions</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-411-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-411-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 411.2.a">411.2.a.</a> A Limited Action is a game action that a spell, ability, or circumstance of the turn's progression causes the player to perform. A player cannot perform these actions at-will.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-411-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-411-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 411.2.b">411.2.b.</a> These actions can only be taken when a player is instructed to do so as the execution of an effect or during their specified occasion during the turn.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player may Draw during the Draw Phase on their turn or when instructed to do so by a spell or ability. They can't choose to Draw at any other time.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-412" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-412" aria-label="Link to rule 412">412.</a> Responsibility</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-412-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-412-1" aria-label="Link to rule 412.1">412.1.</a> Game Actions may be the responsibility of up to one player. The player that performs the Game Action is responsible for it.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Cull the Weak is a spell that reads “Each player kills one of their units.” When the spell resolves, each player picks a unit they control and kills that unit. They are responsible for the death of their own unit.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-412-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-412-2" aria-label="Link to rule 412.2">412.2.</a> In the case where a Game Action is performed by procedures of the game and not by any player, that Game Action is not the responsibility of any player.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-412-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-412-3" aria-label="Link to rule 412.3">412.3.</a> Certain rules assign responsibility to players for Game Actions that they are not normally responsible for.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-412-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-412-4" aria-label="Link to rule 412.4">412.4.</a> If an ability triggers when “you” do something, it triggers when a Game Action that you are responsible for occurs.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A gear reads “When you move an enemy unit, you may exhaust this to [Stun] it.” It will trigger whenever a move game action that you are responsible for causes an enemy unit to move. If an opponent plays a spell that reads “choose a Battlefield. Each player may move a unit they don’t control to that battlefield,” and a player that controls the gear chooses to move an enemy unit, their gear will trigger.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-412-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-412-5" aria-label="Link to rule 412.5">412.5.</a> Note that certain Game Actions, specifically Kill, can be attributed to spells and abilities. This attribution is not the same as the responsibility a given player has for the kill game action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-412-5-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-412-5-a" aria-label="Link to rule 412.5.a">412.5.a.</a> Spells and abilities have attribution for game actions, while players have responsibility.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-412-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-412-6" aria-label="Link to rule 412.6">412.6.</a> Some Game Effects may check if a player has performed a Game Action “with” a Game Object. In order to do so, the Game Action must be attributed to the spell or ability of that Game Object if necessary, the player must control that spell or the ability of that Game Object, and the player must be responsible for the Game Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-412-6-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-412-6-a" aria-label="Link to rule 412.6.a">412.6.a.</a> Abilities granted via the effect text of a Game Object to another Game Object count for both Game Objects for this purpose.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-413" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-413" aria-label="Link to rule 413">413.</a> Types of Actions (A Non-Exhaustive List)</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-414" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-414" aria-label="Link to rule 414">414.</a> Draw</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-414-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-414-1" aria-label="Link to rule 414.1">414.1.</a> Drawing a card takes a single card from a zone and adds it to the player's Hand.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-414-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-414-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 414.1.a">414.1.a.</a> Unless specified otherwise, drawing takes cards from the top of the Main Deck.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-414-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-414-2" aria-label="Link to rule 414.2">414.2.</a> This is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-414-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-414-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 414.2.a">414.2.a.</a> Each player draws 1 during the Draw Phase on their turn.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-414-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-414-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 414.2.b">414.2.b.</a> The player may draw cards when instructed to do so by other game effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-414-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-414-3" aria-label="Link to rule 414.3">414.3.</a> This action, when instructed, is formatted as "Draw X."</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-414-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-414-4" aria-label="Link to rule 414.4">414.4.</a> If a player attempts to draw more cards than are available in their Main Deck, they do the following:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-414-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-414-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 414.4.a">414.4.a.</a> Draw as many as possible.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-414-4-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-414-4-b" aria-label="Link to rule 414.4.b">414.4.b.</a> Perform a Burn Out.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-432">432</a>. Burn Out for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-414-4-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-414-4-c" aria-label="Link to rule 414.4.c">414.4.c.</a> Draw the remaining cards needed to complete the Draw action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-415" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-415" aria-label="Link to rule 415">415.</a> Exhaust</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-415-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-415-1" aria-label="Link to rule 415.1">415.1.</a> Exhausting is an action that marks a non-spell Game Object on the board as "spent."</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-415-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-415-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 415.1.a">415.1.a.</a> To mark it, rotate the card 90 degrees opposite of Readying, so that the orientation of the card is lengthwise in front of you.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-415-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-415-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 415.1.b">415.1.b.</a> A Game Object that is already Exhausted cannot be Exhausted again.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-415-1-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-415-1-c" aria-label="Link to rule 415.1.c">415.1.c.</a> If a Game Object is instructed to be Exhausted while it is already Exhausted, nothing additional happens.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-415-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-415-2" aria-label="Link to rule 415.2">415.2.</a> "Exhausted" is a state for Game Objects on the board that other game effects and rules can reference.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-415-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-415-3" aria-label="Link to rule 415.3">415.3.</a> This is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-415-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-415-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 415.3.a">415.3.a.</a> Most Exhaust actions will be costs for Activated Abilities or Discretionary Actions.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A unit's Standard Move exhausts the unit as a cost.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-415-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-415-4" aria-label="Link to rule 415.4">415.4.</a> When Exhausting is listed as a Cost, then the Action must be able to be completed for the cost to be paid.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A spell says "As an additional cost to play this, you may exhaust a friendly unit." An exhausted friendly unit may not be exhausted again as the additional cost for the spell, and the additional cost has not been paid.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-415-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-415-5" aria-label="Link to rule 415.5">415.5.</a> In abilities, the Exhaust symbol represents the cost "Exhaust this" or "Exhaust me." It resembles a card turning sideways.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-416" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-416" aria-label="Link to rule 416">416.</a> Ready</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-416-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-416-1" aria-label="Link to rule 416.1">416.1.</a> Readying is an action that marks a non-spell Game Object on the board as available for action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-416-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-416-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 416.1.a">416.1.a.</a> To mark it, rotate the card 90 degrees opposite of Exhausting, so that it is vertically oriented in front of you.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-416-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-416-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 416.1.b">416.1.b.</a> A Unit that is already Ready cannot be Readied again.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-416-1-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-416-1-c" aria-label="Link to rule 416.1.c">416.1.c.</a> If a Unit is instructed to be Readied while it is already Ready, nothing additional happens.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-416-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-416-2" aria-label="Link to rule 416.2">416.2.</a> "Ready" is a state for Game Objects on the board that other game effects and rules can reference.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-416-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-416-3" aria-label="Link to rule 416.3">416.3.</a> This is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-416-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-416-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 416.3.a">416.3.a.</a> A player Readies all non-spell Game Objects they Control during the Awakening Phase on their turn.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-416-3-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-416-3-b" aria-label="Link to rule 416.3.b">416.3.b.</a> Players may also Ready Game Objects on the board when effects or spells instruct them to do so.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-417" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-417" aria-label="Link to rule 417">417.</a> Recycle</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-417-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-417-1" aria-label="Link to rule 417.1">417.1.</a> Recycling cards is the action in which a player takes one or more cards from a specific zone and then puts it on the bottom of the corresponding deck.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-417-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-417-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 417.1.a">417.1.a.</a> Main Deck cards are Recycled to the Main Deck.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-417-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-417-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 417.1.b">417.1.b.</a> Runes are Recycled to the Rune Deck.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-417-1-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-417-1-c" aria-label="Link to rule 417.1.c">417.1.c.</a> Each player Recycles cards to their own Main Deck and Rune Deck, regardless of which player is instructed to perform the Recycle action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-417-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-417-2" aria-label="Link to rule 417.2">417.2.</a> This is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-417-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-417-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 417.2.a">417.2.a.</a> The player must Recycle cards from the specified zones when instructed to do so by game effects or costs.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-417-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-417-3" aria-label="Link to rule 417.3">417.3.</a> When Recycling is listed as a Cost, the action must be able to be completed for the cost to be paid.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Vi, Destructive has the ability "Recycle 1 from your trash: Give me +1 [M] this turn." Each time a player activates the ability, they must recycle 1 card from their trash to pay its cost. If they have no cards in their trash, they can't activate the ability, because they can't pay its cost.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-417-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-417-4" aria-label="Link to rule 417.4">417.4.</a> When Recycling is part of an effect, a player must Recycle as many cards as possible from the specified zone or zones.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-417-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-417-5" aria-label="Link to rule 417.5">417.5.</a> If 2 or more cards are Recycled to the Main Deck simultaneously, they are placed on the bottom of that deck in a random order.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Garbage Grabber has the ability "Recycle 3 from your trash, [1], [E]: Draw " To pay the cost of activating this ability, its controller chooses 3 cards from their trash and places them on the bottom of their Main Deck in a random order.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-417-5-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-417-5-a" aria-label="Link to rule 417.5.a">417.5.a.</a> If 2 or more cards are Recycled to the Rune Deck simultaneously, they are placed on the bottom of that deck in the order of their owner's choosing.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-417-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-417-6" aria-label="Link to rule 417.6">417.6.</a> This action, when instructed, is sometimes formatted as "Recycle X from [Zone]." That means to take X cards of the instructed player's choice from the relevant zone and recycle them. Such an instruction does not target the recycled cards.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Dr. Mundo, Expert has the ability "At the start of your Beginning Phase, recycle 3 from your trash." As that ability resolves, its controller recycles 3 cards from their trash. If there are fewer than 3 cards in that player's trash, they recycle as many as they can.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-418" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-418" aria-label="Link to rule 418">418.</a> Deal</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-418-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-418-1" aria-label="Link to rule 418.1">418.1.</a> Spells, Units, Abilities, and other game effects may Deal Damage to units.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-418-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-418-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 418.1.a">418.1.a.</a> Assigning Damage during the Combat Damage Step is not Dealing Damage, but will cause Damage to be Dealt when assignment is complete.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-418-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-418-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 418.1.b">418.1.b.</a> To Deal Damage to Units, mark the specified amount of Damage on the Unit.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-418-1-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-418-1-c" aria-label="Link to rule 418.1.c">418.1.c.</a> Damage is marked on each unit separately.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-418-1-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-418-1-d" aria-label="Link to rule 418.1.d">418.1.d.</a> Damage can be Dealt to more than one Unit at the same time.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-418-1-e" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-418-1-e" aria-label="Link to rule 418.1.e">418.1.e.</a> Valid Damage is a positive integer amount, greater than or equal to 1 Damage.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-418-1-e-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-418-1-e-1" aria-label="Link to rule 418.1.e.1">418.1.e.1.</a> Only Valid Damage is Dealt.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A unit reads “when I take damage, give me +2 [M] this turn.” A spell is played that Prevents the next 3 damage the unit would take. If a player plays Hextech Ray targeting the unit, it will take no damage and its triggered ability will not trigger. If that player had played Void Seeker instead, it would be Dealt 1 and trigger its ability.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-418-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-418-2" aria-label="Link to rule 418.2">418.2.</a> Only Damage can be Dealt.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-418-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-418-3" aria-label="Link to rule 418.3">418.3.</a> Dealing Damage is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-418-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-418-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 418.3.a">418.3.a.</a> Assigning Damage causes Damage to be dealt outside of being directed to Deal Damage.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-461">461</a>. Combat for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-418-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-418-4" aria-label="Link to rule 418.4">418.4.</a> Dealing can have the intrinsic property of Bonus Damage.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-418-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-418-5" aria-label="Link to rule 418.5">418.5.</a> Bonus Damage is a property granted to the action of Dealing and alters the amount of Damage distributed by this action.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-712">712</a>. Bonus Damage for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-418-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-418-6" aria-label="Link to rule 418.6">418.6.</a> Deal actions can originate from one or more sources.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-418-6-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-418-6-a" aria-label="Link to rule 418.6.a">418.6.a.</a> If a game effect does not specify a source, the game effect describing the Deal action is the source.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Void Seeker is a spell that reads “Deal 4 to a unit at a battlefield. Draw 1.” The damage that Void Seeker instructs you to deal is dealt by Void Seeker.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-418-6-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-418-6-b" aria-label="Link to rule 418.6.b">418.6.b.</a> If a game effect does specify a source, then that source is what is considered the origin of the Damage for this Deal action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-418-6-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-418-6-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 418.6.b.1">418.6.b.1.</a> Units and Spells can be the source of Damage for Deal actions.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-418-6-b-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-418-6-b-2" aria-label="Link to rule 418.6.b.2">418.6.b.2.</a> Abilities can be the source of Damage for Deal actions.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-418-6-b-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-418-6-b-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 418.6.b.2.a">418.6.b.2.a.</a> When an Ability is the source of Damage for a Deal action, it is in addition to the Spell or Unit that created that Ability.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Iron Ballista is a gear that says “[E]: Deal 2 to a unit at a battlefield.” This damage is dealt both by a gear and by an ability.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-418-6-b-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-418-6-b-3" aria-label="Link to rule 418.6.b.3">418.6.b.3.</a> When a spell or ability specifies a Unit as the source of the Damage for the Deal action, it is not in addition to the spell or ability that instructed it.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Challenge is a spell that reads “Choose a friendly unit and an enemy unit. They deal damage equal to their Mights to each other.” The damage that Challenge causes to be dealt is dealt by the chosen units, not by Challenge.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-418-6-b-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-418-6-b-4" aria-label="Link to rule 418.6.b.4">418.6.b.4.</a> The controller of the source of a Deal action is responsible for that Deal action unless the player performing the Deal action is otherwise specified.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: If a player plays Challenge targeting a friendly unit and an enemy unit, the controller of the enemy unit is responsible for the damage dealt by their unit. Any effects that trigger “when you deal damage” that that player controls will trigger.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-418-6-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-418-6-c" aria-label="Link to rule 418.6.c">418.6.c.</a> Damage Dealt as a result of being assigned during Combat has the Units as its source.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-418-6-c-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-418-6-c-1" aria-label="Link to rule 418.6.c.1">418.6.c.1.</a> The Damage assigned, and subsequently Dealt, to attackers has the defenders as the source and vice versa.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-418-7" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-418-7" aria-label="Link to rule 418.7">418.7.</a> Deal actions can distribute Damage as part of combat actions or non-combat actions.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-418-8" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-418-8" aria-label="Link to rule 418.8">418.8.</a> Some Deal actions may be written as “Deal X … for each …” Such actions Deal one instance of Damage.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Riven, Shattered reads in part “When I attack, choose an enemy unit here. Deal 2 to it for each Equipment attached to me.” If Riven has three Equipment attached to her, she will deal one instance of 6 damage, not three instances of 2 damage.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-419" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-419" aria-label="Link to rule 419">419.</a> Heal</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-419-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-419-1" aria-label="Link to rule 419.1">419.1.</a> Damage being cleared from Units is Healing.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-419-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-419-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 419.1.a">419.1.a.</a> If Damage is cleared for any reason it is considered Healing.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-419-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-419-2" aria-label="Link to rule 419.2">419.2.</a> More than one Unit can be Healed at the same time.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-419-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-419-3" aria-label="Link to rule 419.3">419.3.</a> Healing is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-419-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-419-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 419.3.a">419.3.a.</a> Players may only Heal units when game effects instruct them to, or at specific points in the turn.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-420" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-420" aria-label="Link to rule 420">420.</a> Play</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-420-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-420-1" aria-label="Link to rule 420.1">420.1.</a> A player Plays cards by placing them on the chain and queuing them to be finalized.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-349">349</a>. Playing Cards for more information on playing cards.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-337">337</a>. Finalize for more information on finalizing cards.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-420-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-420-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 420.1.a">420.1.a.</a> By default, a player can only Play cards from their hand or their Chosen Champion zone.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-420-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-420-2" aria-label="Link to rule 420.2">420.2.</a> This is a Discretionary Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-420-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-420-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 420.2.a">420.2.a.</a> As long as a player has the resources to pay the costs associated with the card and legal choices to make for their cards, they may Play cards.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-420-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-420-3" aria-label="Link to rule 420.3">420.3.</a> Game effects may result in cards being Played as part of their resolution.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-420-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-420-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 420.3.a">420.3.a.</a> This treats Play as a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-420-3-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-420-3-b" aria-label="Link to rule 420.3.b">420.3.b.</a> Treat all steps of Play as normal, except as noted by the game effect instructing this Limited Play Effect.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-420-3-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-420-3-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 420.3.b.1">420.3.b.1.</a> Such game effects may specify choices or effects such as alternate costs, ignored costs, play locations, targets, or other choices or effects normally made or processed during Finalization.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-420-3-b-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-420-3-b-2" aria-label="Link to rule 420.3.b.2">420.3.b.2.</a> Such game effects may specify those choices or effects based on the condition of the board. If they do so, that information is checked on finalization of the resulting Game Object, unless it is specified as being checked earlier.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Bone Skewer is a spell that reads in part “Choose a battlefield. An opponent reveals their hand. You may choose a unit from it. They play that unit to that battlefield, ignoring any and all costs.” The chosen battlefield isn’t checked on finalization of the resulting game object but when the spell itself is resolving.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A spell reads in part “Play a unit from your trash to an enemy occupied battlefield, ignoring its Energy cost.” On finalization, they can choose any enemy occupied battlefield to play the unit to. If no such battlefield exists as the unit is being finalized, it will fail to finalize.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Rek’Sai, Swarm Queen is a unit that reads “When I attack, you may reveal the top 2 cards of your Main Deck. You may banish one, then play it. If it is a unit, you may play it here. Recycle the rest.” The attack trigger adds a permission to the normal valid locations for the played unit, that being “here.” The “here” in Rek’Sai’s ability is a referent, whose information is checked when the ability resolves. As such, it will not check on finalization of the unit being played.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-420-3-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-420-3-c" aria-label="Link to rule 420.3.c">420.3.c.</a> If there are no eligible cards to Play when instructed to Play in this manner, then nothing happens and resolution continues.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-420-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-420-4" aria-label="Link to rule 420.4">420.4.</a> Some Abilities trigger when cards are played or otherwise check whether cards have been played.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-420-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-420-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 420.4.a">420.4.a.</a> Any such triggered abilities trigger when the act of playing the card has been completed by the resolution of the card.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-420-4-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-420-4-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 420.4.a.1">420.4.a.1.</a> If a game effect prevents the resolution of the card—for example, because the card was countered—abilities that trigger on playing cards will not trigger.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-426">426</a>. Counter for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-420-4-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-420-4-b" aria-label="Link to rule 420.4.b">420.4.b.</a> Non-triggered abilities and conditional clauses that check cards being played do so by means of referencing whether said cards have been Finalized.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player plays a spell, which is countered by Defy. Any Legion abilities of game objects controlled by that same player will be active.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player plays a spell, which is countered by Defy. If that player plays Battering Ram and has played no other cards that turn, it will cost [4] Energy.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-420-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-420-5" aria-label="Link to rule 420.5">420.5.</a> If an effect refers to a card being played and doesn’t do so by means of checking whether cards were played, it refers to the card being added to the chain and queued for Finalization, that is, the Play Game Action.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Astral Heron is a unit that reads “When you play your first card each turn, if I'm at a battlefield, the next card you play this turn costs [2][A][A] less.” This effect applies a discount to the next card the play game action, discretionary or limited, is performed on.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-421" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-421" aria-label="Link to rule 421">421.</a> Move</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-421-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-421-1" aria-label="Link to rule 421.1">421.1.</a> Moving is the act of a Game Object moving between two Locations on The Board.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-447">447</a>. Movement for more information on movement.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-421-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-421-2" aria-label="Link to rule 421.2">421.2.</a> Moving is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-421-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-421-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 421.2.a">421.2.a.</a> Players may only move Game Objects when instructed to do so by Game Effects or costs.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-421-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-421-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 421.2.b">421.2.b.</a> The Standard Move inherent to Units may also cause Movement.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-144">144</a>. for more information on the Standard Move.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-421-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-421-3" aria-label="Link to rule 421.3">421.3.</a> The Standard Move inherent to Units is a Discretionary Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-421-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-421-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 421.3.a">421.3.a.</a> The Cost is Exhausting one or more Units.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-421-3-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-421-3-b" aria-label="Link to rule 421.3.b">421.3.b.</a> The Effect is Moving those Units.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-422" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-422" aria-label="Link to rule 422">422.</a> Hide</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-422-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-422-1" aria-label="Link to rule 422.1">422.1.</a> Hiding a card is the act of placing a card facedown at a Battlefield you control.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-422-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-422-2" aria-label="Link to rule 422.2">422.2.</a> Hiding is a Discretionary Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-422-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-422-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 422.2.a">422.2.a.</a> The Hidden keyword allows a player to pay a cost to Hide a card any time they have a Hidden card in their hand or Champion Zone and meet the prerequisites.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-811">811</a>. Hidden for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-422-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-422-3" aria-label="Link to rule 422.3">422.3.</a> Cards that are facedown at Battlefields have their gameplay properties and permissions defined by the effect that put them there.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Cards that players Hide with the Hidden keyword can be Played for [0] on any subsequent turn as a property of the Hidden keyword.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-422-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-422-4" aria-label="Link to rule 422.4">422.4.</a> If a facedown card would change zones or if the game ends, its owner reveals it to all players.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-128">128</a>. Privacy for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-423" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-423" aria-label="Link to rule 423">423.</a> Discard</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-423-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-423-1" aria-label="Link to rule 423.1">423.1.</a> Discarding a card is moving it from a player's hand directly into their trash without activating or executing its normal rules text.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-423-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-423-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 423.1.a">423.1.a.</a> The player who is performing the action chooses which cards to send to their Trash, and may use Private Information to do so.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-423-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-423-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 423.1.b">423.1.b.</a> “When I am discarded” abilities or other Triggered Abilities that trigger on discarding are executed after discarding has occurred.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-423-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-423-2" aria-label="Link to rule 423.2">423.2.</a> Discarding is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-423-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-423-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 423.2.a">423.2.a.</a> A player must Discard cards when instructed to do so by game effects or costs.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-423-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-423-3" aria-label="Link to rule 423.3">423.3.</a> When Discarding is listed as a Cost, then the Action must be able to be completed for the cost to be paid.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A card has the ability "Discard 2: Deal 2 damage to a unit at a battlefield." To activate the ability, the card's controller must have at least 2 cards in hand and must be able to discard them.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-423-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-423-4" aria-label="Link to rule 423.4">423.4.</a> When Discarding is part of an effect, then a player must Discard as many cards as possible from their hand. If instructed to discard more cards than they have in their hand, further discard instructions are negated.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Undercover Agent has the ability "[Deathknell][&gt;] Discard 2, then draw 2. (When I die, get the effect.)" If Undercover Agent's controller has 2 or more cards in hand, they must discard 2. If they have 1 card in hand, they discard 1, and the rest of the discard instruction is negated. If they have no cards in hand, the entire discard instruction is negated. Regardless of how many cards they discard, they then draw 2.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-423-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-423-5" aria-label="Link to rule 423.5">423.5.</a> This action is formatted as "Discard X."</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-424" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-424" aria-label="Link to rule 424">424.</a> Stun</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-424-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-424-1" aria-label="Link to rule 424.1">424.1.</a> Stunning is the act of selecting one or more Units on the Board and rendering them Stunned.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-424-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-424-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 424.1.a">424.1.a.</a> Stunned is a binary state. A Unit is Stunned or it isn't.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-424-1-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-424-1-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 424.1.a.1">424.1.a.1.</a> A Stunned Unit can not be Stunned again.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Eclipse Herald has the ability "When you stun an enemy unit, ready me and give me +1 [M] this turn." Its controller plays a spell that reads "Stun a unit." They may choose a unit that's already stunned, but if they do, Eclipse Herald will not trigger.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-424-1-a-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-424-1-a-2" aria-label="Link to rule 424.1.a.2">424.1.a.2.</a> Stunned Units lose the Stunned status during step 3d of the end of turn cleanup.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-424-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-424-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 424.1.b">424.1.b.</a> A Stunned Unit does not contribute its might to damage in the combat damage step.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-467">467</a>. The Combat Damage Step for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-424-1-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-424-1-c" aria-label="Link to rule 424.1.c">424.1.c.</a> A Stunned Unit must still have damage applied to it equal to, or greater than, its full might value to be killed.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-424-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-424-2" aria-label="Link to rule 424.2">424.2.</a> Stunning is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-424-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-424-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 424.2.a">424.2.a.</a> Players may only Stun Units when directed to by Game Effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-425" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-425" aria-label="Link to rule 425">425.</a> Reveal</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-425-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-425-1" aria-label="Link to rule 425.1">425.1.</a> Revealing is the act of presenting a card to all players from a zone that one or more players do not have access to the information of.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-425-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-425-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 425.1.a">425.1.a.</a> Revealed is a temporary state and is not a zone.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-425-1-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-425-1-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 425.1.a.1">425.1.a.1.</a> Other cards, including the card being revealed, can reference the act of being Revealed.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-425-1-a-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-425-1-a-2" aria-label="Link to rule 425.1.a.2">425.1.a.2.</a> Cards remain in the zone they are being Revealed from.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: If a card is being Revealed from the top of a player's Main Deck, it is still the top card of that player's Main Deck.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-425-1-a-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-425-1-a-3" aria-label="Link to rule 425.1.a.3">425.1.a.3.</a> The state of being Revealed lasts for the duration specified by the game effect that applied the state; if there is no duration specified and the state was caused by a Reveal action as instructed by a spell or ability, it lasts until the resolution of that spell or ability finishes.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-425-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-425-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 425.1.b">425.1.b.</a> Unless otherwise described, Revealed cards do nothing else beyond become temporarily known information to all players.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-425-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-425-2" aria-label="Link to rule 425.2">425.2.</a> Revealing is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-425-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-425-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 425.2.a">425.2.a.</a> Players may only Reveal cards from Private or Secret zones when instructed to do so by Game Effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-425-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-425-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 425.2.b">425.2.b.</a> During the course of a game of Riftbound, a player may choose to show Private information to one or more other players. This does not count as revealing and does not trigger any effects that trigger when cards are revealed.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-425-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-425-3" aria-label="Link to rule 425.3">425.3.</a> This action is formatted as "Reveal cards from [zone]." or “Reveal [Zone].”</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: "Reveal 2 cards from the top of your Main Deck" would be executed by taking the two cards from the top of your Main Deck, and then presenting them to all players to clearly read and understand at the table. Then, when all players have had a chance to understand the revealed information, return them to the top of the Main Deck in the same order.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-425-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-425-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 425.3.a">425.3.a.</a> When the zone is instructed to be Revealed without indicating a number of cards, that refers to “All cards currently in the specified zone.”</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A card reads “Choose an opponent and reveal your hand. That player picks a card in your hand. You may play the rest this turn, ignoring their costs.” When instructed to reveal their hand, that player reveals the cards currently in their hand.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-425-3-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-425-3-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 425.3.a.1">425.3.a.1.</a> The Revealed state is applied to those cards. Any further cards added to that zone after the instruction has been executed do not become Revealed.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-425-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-425-4" aria-label="Link to rule 425.4">425.4.</a> Game Effects can manipulate or modify the cards Revealed while they are Revealed</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-425-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-425-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 425.4.a">425.4.a.</a> While cards are Revealed, those cards can be further manipulated, or accessed. They can even have their destination modified for when the Reveal ends.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: "Reveal 2 cards from the top of your Main Deck. Put one into your hand and Recycle the other" would be executed by taking the top two cards from your Main Deck, presenting them to the table, and once every player present has had a chance to understand them you would make the selection of one of them to add to your hand. The one you did not select would then be Recycled to the bottom of the Main Deck.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-426" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-426" aria-label="Link to rule 426">426.</a> Counter</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-426-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-426-1" aria-label="Link to rule 426.1">426.1.</a> Countering is the act of negating the execution, activation, or otherwise playing of a card or ability by a player.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-426-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-426-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 426.1.a">426.1.a.</a> A card or ability that is Countered does nothing and is cleared from the chain.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-426-1-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-426-1-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 426.1.a.1">426.1.a.1.</a> Cards that are cleared from the chain in this way are placed in the trash.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-426-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-426-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 426.1.b">426.1.b.</a> A card that is Countered is not considered to have been played for abilities that trigger on cards being played.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-426-1-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-426-1-c" aria-label="Link to rule 426.1.c">426.1.c.</a> Countering does not refund any costs paid to play a card, activate an ability, or trigger an ability.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-426-1-c-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-426-1-c-1" aria-label="Link to rule 426.1.c.1">426.1.c.1.</a> This includes additional costs.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-426-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-426-2" aria-label="Link to rule 426.2">426.2.</a> Countering is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-426-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-426-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 426.2.a">426.2.a.</a> Players may only Counter cards and abilities when directed to by Game Effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-426-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-426-3" aria-label="Link to rule 426.3">426.3.</a> This action is formatted as "Counter [a card or ability on the chain]."</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-427" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-427" aria-label="Link to rule 427">427.</a> Buff</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-427-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-427-1" aria-label="Link to rule 427.1">427.1.</a> Buffing is the action of placing a Buff counter on a Unit.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-701">701</a>. Buffs for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-427-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-427-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 427.1.a">427.1.a.</a> A Buff is also an object.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-427-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-427-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 427.1.b">427.1.b.</a> To Buff a unit, place a Buff Counter on it if it does not have one already</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-427-1-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-427-1-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 427.1.b.1">427.1.b.1.</a> If the unit already has a Buff Counter on it, it does not get another one.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-427-1-b-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-427-1-b-2" aria-label="Link to rule 427.1.b.2">427.1.b.2.</a> Some effects may grant a Game Object permission to be Buffed multiple times. Such an effect ignores this restriction.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-427-1-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-427-1-c" aria-label="Link to rule 427.1.c">427.1.c.</a> Units with Buff Counters can still be chosen for actions that Buff units, but will not be Buffed as part of the execution.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A spell reads "Buff a unit. Then, if it was buffed this way, draw a card." It will place a buff counter on a unit that has no buff counter, and then its controller will draw a card. However, if the player chooses a unit with a buff counter on it already, then the card will not be drawn.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A unit reads “When you buff me, ready me.” If that unit is chosen for a game effect that would buff it and it is already buffed, its ability will not trigger.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-427-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-427-2" aria-label="Link to rule 427.2">427.2.</a> Buffing is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-427-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-427-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 427.2.a">427.2.a.</a> Players may only Buff units when Game Effects direct them to do so.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-427-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-427-3" aria-label="Link to rule 427.3">427.3.</a> This action is formatted as "Buff [one or more units]." e.g., "Buff a unit." e.g., "Buff a friendly unit." e.g., "Buff two friendly units at the same battlefield."</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-428" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-428" aria-label="Link to rule 428">428.</a> Banish</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-428-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-428-1" aria-label="Link to rule 428.1">428.1.</a> Banishing is the action of placing a card from any other zone into Banishment. See 108.6. Banishment for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-428-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-428-2" aria-label="Link to rule 428.2">428.2.</a> When a card is Banished it is placed directly into the Banishment zone from its origin.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-428-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-428-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 428.2.a">428.2.a.</a> Banish is not a subset of Kill.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-428-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-428-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 428.2.b">428.2.b.</a> Banish is not a subset of Discard.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-428-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-428-3" aria-label="Link to rule 428.3">428.3.</a> Cards and effects can refer to cards that were banished by the same object.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-428-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-428-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 428.3.a">428.3.a.</a> Separate instances of an object or effect banishing cards do not reference other banished cards by objects of the same name or effects originating from cards of the same name.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A spell reads "Banish the top card of your Main Deck. When you conquer this turn, draw it." This card effect allows you to draw the banished card from banishment if a condition is met. If more than one of these effects is played, each one triggers separately. Each card will be drawn separately. If the turn player passes the turn without playing the card, the card remains in banishment indefinitely. If the same player plays another copy of the same spell, banishes another card, and satisfies its condition, they could play the new banished card, but not the original banished card.</p></blockquote>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-394">394</a>. Linked Abilities for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-428-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-428-4" aria-label="Link to rule 428.4">428.4.</a> Banishing is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-428-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-428-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 428.4.a">428.4.a.</a> Players may only Banish cards or permanents when Game Effects direct them to do so.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-428-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-428-5" aria-label="Link to rule 428.5">428.5.</a> This action is formatted as "Banish [one or more permanents or cards]." e.g., "Banish a card from your hand." e.g., "Banish 2 cards from your trash." e.g., "Look at the top 2 cards of your Main Deck. Draw one of them and banish the other."</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-429" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-429" aria-label="Link to rule 429">429.</a> Kill</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-429-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-429-1" aria-label="Link to rule 429.1">429.1.</a> Killing is the action of a Permanent going to the trash from the board.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-429-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-429-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 429.1.a">429.1.a.</a> This can be Active or Passive.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-429-1-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-429-1-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 429.1.a.1">429.1.a.1.</a> Active Kill is when the action is taken when instructed by a game effect or as a cost for a card or ability.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-429-1-a-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-429-1-a-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 429.1.a.1.a">429.1.a.1.a.</a> This is referred to as a Kill Instruction.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-429-1-a-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-429-1-a-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 429.1.a.1.b">429.1.a.1.b.</a> When a unit with a Deathknell or other ability that triggers on its own death is to be put in the Trash due to a Kill Instruction, it first has any such ability added to the chain as a Pending Item. Note the unit’s location, attributes, and other relevant information to process those abilities when finalized before completing this Kill Instruction.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Draven, Audacious reads in part “When I die in combat, choose an opponent. They gain 1 point.” The ability triggers when Draven himself dies, so it will go on the chain first when a kill instruction is performed on Draven, before he is put in the trash.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-429-1-a-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-429-1-a-2" aria-label="Link to rule 429.1.a.2">429.1.a.2.</a> Passive Kill is when the action is taken as a result of Lethal Damage or as a consequence for any other state.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-429-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-429-2" aria-label="Link to rule 429.2">429.2.</a> When a permanent is killed it is placed directly in the trash from its place of origin.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-429-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-429-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 429.2.a">429.2.a.</a> It is only considered Killed if its origin was any zone on the board.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-429-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-429-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 429.2.b">429.2.b.</a> This is not a subset of Move.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-429-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-429-3" aria-label="Link to rule 429.3">429.3.</a> Killing is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-429-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-429-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 429.3.a">429.3.a.</a> Players may only Kill units when Game Effects direct them to do so.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-429-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-429-4" aria-label="Link to rule 429.4">429.4.</a> Killing can also be the result of resolving a Cleanup.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-429-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-429-5" aria-label="Link to rule 429.5">429.5.</a> Killing can be attributed to one or more Game Objects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-429-5-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-429-5-a" aria-label="Link to rule 429.5.a">429.5.a.</a> The Killed Unit or Gear is said to be Killed by that Game Object.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-429-5-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-429-5-b" aria-label="Link to rule 429.5.b">429.5.b.</a> A spell or ability that contains a Kill instruction is responsible for Killing the Unit or Gear.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-429-5-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-429-5-c" aria-label="Link to rule 429.5.c">429.5.c.</a> When one or more Units is killed due to a Cleanup, that kill action is attributed to the spell or ability that resolved immediately prior to that Cleanup that dealt damage to the Unit or Units.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-429-5-c-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-429-5-c-1" aria-label="Link to rule 429.5.c.1">429.5.c.1.</a> The player responsible for the deal action is responsible for the kill action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-429-5-c-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-429-5-c-2" aria-label="Link to rule 429.5.c.2">429.5.c.2.</a> If the Cleanup that caused the units to be killed was the Combat Cleanup, the sources of the Combat Damage are attributed the kill action, and their controller is responsible for the kill action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-429-5-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-429-5-d" aria-label="Link to rule 429.5.d">429.5.d.</a> Abilities originating from Game Objects that are attributed Kill Actions are attributed in addition to the Game Object that created them.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: There is a spell that says “Do this twice: Deal 3 to a unit.” Immortal Phoenix is a unit that says “When you kill a unit with a spell, you may pay [1][C] to play me from your trash.” A player plays the spell while Immortal Phoenix is in their trash. The “do this” phrasing means that it has a reflexive triggered ability, which places two triggered abilities on the chain. As each of those triggered abilities resolve, it deals damage to the unit chosen for that ability. If one of these abilities deals lethal damage to a unit, both the spell and its ability are considered sources of the damage, and so both the spell and its ability receive attribution for killing the unit. This means that the spell’s controller killed a unit with a spell, so Immortal Phoenix’s ability will trigger.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-429-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-429-6" aria-label="Link to rule 429.6">429.6.</a> This action is formatted as "Kill [one or more permanents]." e.g., "Kill an enemy unit." e.g., "Kill this, [2]: Draw 1." e.g., "Kill all gear."</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-430" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-430" aria-label="Link to rule 430">430.</a> Add</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-430-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-430-1" aria-label="Link to rule 430.1">430.1.</a> Adding is the action of putting resources into a player's Rune Pool.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-430-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-430-2" aria-label="Link to rule 430.2">430.2.</a> Triggered and activated abilities that Add resources finalize immediately when they are played, even when handling tasks or during the resolution of spells and abilities, and resolve as soon as they are finalized.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-430-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-430-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 430.2.a">430.2.a.</a> Priority and Focus will not pass from Add abilities being finalized or resolving. Any ongoing Task or step in the FEPR process is paused while they are being finalized and while they are resolving.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-430-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-430-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 430.2.b">430.2.b.</a> Spells that Add resources will be finalized and will resolve at the normal timing for spells.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-430-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-430-3" aria-label="Link to rule 430.3">430.3.</a> Activated abilities that Add resources and have the Reaction tag can be activated at any time that spells or abilities require resources be paid.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player can add Energy and Power through any means before initiating the process of playing a spell. After initiating that process, in the Pay Costs step, they may activate Add Reactions to add Energy or Power to pay costs.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player moves two units to a battlefield where their opponent controls a Mageseeker Investigator. Although they have no window of priority, they may activate Add Reactions to pay for the applied cost from Mageseeker Investigator, and those abilities finalize and resolve immediately.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-430-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-430-4" aria-label="Link to rule 430.4">430.4.</a> Adding is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-430-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-430-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 430.4.a">430.4.a.</a> Players may only Add resources when Game Effects direct them to do so.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-430-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-430-5" aria-label="Link to rule 430.5">430.5.</a> This action is formatted as "Add [one or more resources]." e.g., "Add [2]." means "Add 2 Energy." e.g., "[E]: Add [Y]." means "Add 1 Power of the Order domain." e.g., "Add [1][G]." means "Add 1 Energy and 1 Power of the Calm domain."</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-431" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-431" aria-label="Link to rule 431">431.</a> Channel</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-431-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-431-1" aria-label="Link to rule 431.1">431.1.</a> Channeling is the action of taking one or more Runes from the top of a player's Rune Deck and putting them on the board.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-431-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-431-2" aria-label="Link to rule 431.2">431.2.</a> The Game Effect that instructs a player to channel 1 or more runes may specify the conditions or circumstances under which those runes enter the board.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A spell reads "Channel 1 rune exhausted." As that spell resolves, its controller puts the top rune of their rune deck onto the board and that rune enters the board exhausted rather than ready.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-431-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-431-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 431.2.a">431.2.a.</a> By default, runes are channeled readied.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-431-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-431-3" aria-label="Link to rule 431.3">431.3.</a> If there aren’t sufficient runes in the Rune Deck, channel as many as possible.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-431-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-431-4" aria-label="Link to rule 431.4">431.4.</a> Channeling is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-431-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-431-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 431.4.a">431.4.a.</a> A player Channels two Runes during the Channel Phase on their turn.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-431-4-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-431-4-b" aria-label="Link to rule 431.4.b">431.4.b.</a> Players may also Channel runes when Game Effects direct them to do so.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-431-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-431-5" aria-label="Link to rule 431.5">431.5.</a> This action is formatted as "Channel X rune(s)," optionally followed by conditions or stipulations. e.g., "Channel 1 rune." e.g., "When you play me, channel 1 rune exhausted." e.g., "Channel 2 runes exhausted. If you couldn't channel 2 runes this way, draw 1."</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-432" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-432" aria-label="Link to rule 432">432.</a> Burn Out</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-432-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
