<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-432-1" aria-label="Link to rule 432.1">432.1.</a> Burning Out is an action a player must perform if they attempt to move one or more cards from their Main Deck to any other zone in excess of the number of cards remaining in their Main Deck:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-432-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-432-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 432.1.a">432.1.a.</a> If a player must Draw cards in excess to the number of cards in their Main Deck, they will Draw as many as possible, perform this action, then Draw the remaining amount instructed.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-432-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-432-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 432.1.b">432.1.b.</a> If a player must put one or more cards from their Main Deck in any other zone, such as the Trash, in excess of the number of cards in their deck they will do so as much as possible, perform this action, and then complete the remaining number required by the instruction.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-432-1-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-432-1-c" aria-label="Link to rule 432.1.c">432.1.c.</a> If an instruction directs a player to look at or reveal cards in excess to the number of cards in a player’s Main Deck, that player looks at or Reveals as many as possible, but does not Burn Out, then proceeds with the rest of the instruction.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-432-1-c-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-432-1-c-1" aria-label="Link to rule 432.1.c.1">432.1.c.1.</a> If there are insufficient cards among the looked at or revealed cards to perform subsequent actions to the revealed or looked at cards, any further instructions are negated. This does not cause a Burn Out, even if those instructions would cause those cards to change zones. Reminder: Cards are considered in the zone of origin while being looked at or revealed, in this case the Main Deck.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-432-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-432-2" aria-label="Link to rule 432.2">432.2.</a> To Burn Out, a player does the following in sequence:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-432-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-432-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 432.2.a">432.2.a.</a> Performs as much of the prescribed action as possible.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-432-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-432-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 432.2.b">432.2.b.</a> Recycles their trash into their Main Deck. Reminder: When multiple cards are Recycled to the Main Deck at the same time, those cards must be randomized</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-432-2-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-432-2-c" aria-label="Link to rule 432.2.c">432.2.c.</a> Chooses an opponent to gain 1 point.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-432-2-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-432-2-d" aria-label="Link to rule 432.2.d">432.2.d.</a> Completes the remainder of the action that caused them to burn out.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player attempts to draw 1 during their Draw Phase while their Main Deck is empty. That player instead recycles their trash into their Main Deck, randomizing it as normal, then chooses an opponent to gain 1 point, and then draws 1.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-432-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-432-3" aria-label="Link to rule 432.3">432.3.</a> A player's Main Deck may remain empty as they Burn Out, usually because their trash is also empty. When they attempt to perform the original action again, it will cause another Burn Out.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-432-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-432-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 432.3.a">432.3.a.</a> Unless some effect intervenes, this will result in them burning out repeatedly, giving 1 point to an opponent each time, until an opponent passes the Victory Score and wins the game.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-432-3-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-432-3-b" aria-label="Link to rule 432.3.b">432.3.b.</a> Points gained after the first Burn Out being processed in sequence cannot be replaced or prevented by any means.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-432-3-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-432-3-c" aria-label="Link to rule 432.3.c">432.3.c.</a> Points gained after the first Burn Out being processed in sequence that cause a player to reach or surpass the Victory Score for their game mode will cause that player to win the game if they also have more points than every opponent.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: If a player has no cards in their deck or their trash and they go to draw 1, they will begin to burn out repeatedly. After the first burn out is processed, any subsequent burnout that brings a player to a number of points greater than the Victory Score and more than any opponent will cause them to win the game.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-432-3-c-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-432-3-c-1" aria-label="Link to rule 432.3.c.1">432.3.c.1.</a> The player wins immediately, without needing to wait for a cleanup to occur.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-432-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-432-4" aria-label="Link to rule 432.4">432.4.</a> Burning Out is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-432-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-432-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 432.4.a">432.4.a.</a> Players may only burn out when Game Effects direct them to do so.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-432-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-432-5" aria-label="Link to rule 432.5">432.5.</a> Burning Out is a Replacement Effect.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-367">367</a>. Replacement Effects for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-433" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-433" aria-label="Link to rule 433">433.</a> Double</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-433-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-433-1" aria-label="Link to rule 433.1">433.1.</a> Doubling is the act of increasing a numeric attribute by an amount equal to that attribute’s current value.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-433-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-433-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 433.1.a">433.1.a.</a> This creates an effect that modulates that attribute by that specific amount for the duration specified by the Game Effect that instructed the player to perform this action.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A unit with 3 base Might and Shield 2 is in combat as a Defender. Since Shield applies, its current Might is 5. A player chooses it as the target for Last Stand, a spell that reads in part “Double a friendly unit's Might this turn.” Its current Might is 5, so it gets +5 Might this turn, for a current Might of 10. After combat, Shield no longer applies, but the +5 Might from Last Stand does, so the unit’s Might is 8.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-433-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-433-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 433.1.b">433.1.b.</a> Some Game Effects may also cause Damage to be Doubled; in this case, those effects are the equivalent to a replacement effect that reads “If I would take damage from a source, I take double that damage from that source instead.” or “If [I/this] would deal damage, [I/this] deal double that damage instead.” The source of the Damage in these cases remains the same after Doubling.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-433-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-433-2" aria-label="Link to rule 433.2">433.2.</a> Doubling is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-433-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-433-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 433.2.a">433.2.a.</a> Players may only Double when Game Effects direct them to do so.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-434" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-434" aria-label="Link to rule 434">434.</a> Swap</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-434-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-434-1" aria-label="Link to rule 434.1">434.1.</a> Swapping is the act of increasing one numeric value and decreasing another numeric value on some number of Game Objects such that their values are reversed.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-434-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-434-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 434.1.a">434.1.a.</a> Swapping creates two different effects that apply to each attribute. One that Increases one value and one that Decreases the other. These effects last for the duration specified by the effect that instructed the Swap.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-434-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-434-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 434.1.b">434.1.b.</a> To accomplish this, determine the difference between these values and then apply an Increase for that amount to the lower value of the two attributes, and a Decrease of that amount to the higher value of the two attributes.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-434-1-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-434-1-c" aria-label="Link to rule 434.1.c">434.1.c.</a> If both attributes are the same numeric value, Swapping has no effect.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-434-1-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-434-1-d" aria-label="Link to rule 434.1.d">434.1.d.</a> If the attributes being swapped are Units’ Mights, this effect is the equivalent to giving those units a Might increasing effect and a Might decreasing effect respectively.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-434-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-434-2" aria-label="Link to rule 434.2">434.2.</a> Swapping is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-434-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-434-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 434.2.a">434.2.a.</a> Players may only swap when Game Effects direct them to do so.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-435" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-435" aria-label="Link to rule 435">435.</a> Attach</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-435-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-435-1" aria-label="Link to rule 435.1">435.1.</a> Attaching is the act of linking two cards on the board together to combine their effects in some way. This causes one or more cards to become Attached and at least one card to become a Top-Most Card.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-716">716</a>. Attachment for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-435-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-435-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 435.1.a">435.1.a.</a> Attaching affects two cards at once.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-435-1-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-435-1-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 435.1.a.1">435.1.a.1.</a> One card will be designated as the one being Attached, and thus become Attached. The other will become the Top-Most Card.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-435-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-435-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 435.1.b">435.1.b.</a> This is represented by physically laying the Top-Most card on top of the other or others such that all Effect Texts and Might Bonuses are showing, but nothing else from the card or cards Attached.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-435-1-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-435-1-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 435.1.b.1">435.1.b.1.</a> In the situation where there is more than one card attached to the Top-Most card, they should be stacked in such a way that all Effect Text boxes and Might Bonuses are readable. The order of the Attached cards has no bearing on the application of effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-435-1-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-435-1-c" aria-label="Link to rule 435.1.c">435.1.c.</a> The Top-Most card has all Effect Text of all cards Attached to it appended to its Rules Text.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-435-1-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-435-1-d" aria-label="Link to rule 435.1.d">435.1.d.</a> The Top-Most Card has its Might modulated by the Might Bonus of all cards Attached to it.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-435-1-e" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-435-1-e" aria-label="Link to rule 435.1.e">435.1.e.</a> Attaching one or more cards will cause those cards’ printed Rules Text to become Inactive for as long as they remain Attached.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-716">716</a>. Attachment for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-435-1-f" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-435-1-f" aria-label="Link to rule 435.1.f">435.1.f.</a> Attaching a card to a new Top-Most Card will cause it to Detach from the card to which it is currently Attached.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-435-1-g" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-435-1-g" aria-label="Link to rule 435.1.g">435.1.g.</a> Attaching a card to its current Top-Most Card will not have any effect.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-435-1-h" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-435-1-h" aria-label="Link to rule 435.1.h">435.1.h.</a> Units cannot be Attached to other cards.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-140">140</a>. Units for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-435-1-h-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-435-1-h-1" aria-label="Link to rule 435.1.h.1">435.1.h.1.</a> If a Unit would be Attached, nothing happens.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-435-1-h-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-435-1-h-2" aria-label="Link to rule 435.1.h.2">435.1.h.2.</a> If a non-Unit Game Object that is attached becomes a Unit through any means, it detaches as it becomes a Unit.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-435-1-i" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-435-1-i" aria-label="Link to rule 435.1.i">435.1.i.</a> If a Game Effect instructs a player to Attach a card to its current Top-Most Card, nothing additional happens.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-435-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-435-2" aria-label="Link to rule 435.2">435.2.</a> Attaching is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-435-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-435-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 435.2.a">435.2.a.</a> Players may only Attach cards when directed to by Game Effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-435-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-435-3" aria-label="Link to rule 435.3">435.3.</a> Attaching cards does not inherently choose or specify a target. However, Game Effects that Attach cards may do so.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-435-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-435-4" aria-label="Link to rule 435.4">435.4.</a> When a card Attaches to a card, its location becomes the same as the new Top-Most Card.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-435-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-435-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 435.4.a">435.4.a.</a> This is not a Move.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-435-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-435-5" aria-label="Link to rule 435.5">435.5.</a> Attaching a card to another card does not modify either card’s state except in making those cards Attached and the Top-Most Card, and causing the Attached card to change locations.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: An exhausted equipment being attached to a unit does not ready the equipment.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-436" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-436" aria-label="Link to rule 436">436.</a> Detach</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-436-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-436-1" aria-label="Link to rule 436.1">436.1.</a> Detaching is the act of unlinking two cards that are currently linked through the act of Attaching. This causes one to cease being Attached, and potentially causes the other to cease being a Top- Most card.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-436-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-436-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 436.1.a">436.1.a.</a> Detaching affects only cards that are currently Attached to another card.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-436-1-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-436-1-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 436.1.a.1">436.1.a.1.</a> Game effects that instruct a player to Detach a card that is currently not Attached to anything will do nothing.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-436-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-436-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 436.1.b">436.1.b.</a> When one or more cards become Detached, they cease to be in the Attached state.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-436-1-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-436-1-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 436.1.b.1">436.1.b.1.</a> To represent this, these cards should no longer be placed under the card they were previously placed under as Top-Most Card.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-436-1-b-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-436-1-b-2" aria-label="Link to rule 436.1.b.2">436.1.b.2.</a> If the Top-Most Card no longer has any cards Attached to it after this, it is no longer a Top-Most Card.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-436-1-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-436-1-c" aria-label="Link to rule 436.1.c">436.1.c.</a> The card being Detached has its Effect Text become Inactive and its Rules Text cease being Inactive.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-436-1-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-436-1-d" aria-label="Link to rule 436.1.d">436.1.d.</a> The Top-Most Card ceases to have the Effect Text of the card being Detached appended to its Rules Text.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-436-1-e" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-436-1-e" aria-label="Link to rule 436.1.e">436.1.e.</a> The Top-Most Card ceases to have its Might modulated by the Might Bonus of the card being Detached.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-436-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-436-2" aria-label="Link to rule 436.2">436.2.</a> Detaching is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-436-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-436-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 436.2.a">436.2.a.</a> Players may only Detach cards when directed to by Game Effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-436-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-436-3" aria-label="Link to rule 436.3">436.3.</a> Detaching cards does not inherently choose, or specify a target. However, Game Effects that Detach cards may.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-436-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-436-4" aria-label="Link to rule 436.4">436.4.</a> When a card Detaches from a Top-Most Card, its location is the same as the Top-Most Card from which it Detached.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-436-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-436-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 436.4.a">436.4.a.</a> If the Detached card was a Gear and this causes it to become present at a Battlefield, it will be Recalled during the next Cleanup.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-318">318</a>. Cleanups for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-436-4-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-436-4-b" aria-label="Link to rule 436.4.b">436.4.b.</a> If the Attached card was Detached because the Top-Most Card changed zones from a board zone to a non-board zone, then the location that the Attached Card will Detach to is the last location the Top-Most Card was at before changing from a board zone to a non- board zone.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-437" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-437" aria-label="Link to rule 437">437.</a> Predict</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-437-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-437-1" aria-label="Link to rule 437.1">437.1.</a> Predicting a card is the act of looking at a single card from the top of the Main Deck and choosing whether or not to Recycle it.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-437-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-437-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 437.1.a">437.1.a.</a> When more than one card is Predicted, the Predicting player looks at that many cards and Recycles any number of them before putting the rest back on top of their Main Deck in any order.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-437-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-437-2" aria-label="Link to rule 437.2">437.2.</a> Predicting is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-437-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-437-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 437.2.a">437.2.a.</a> Players may only Predict cards when instructed to do so by game effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-437-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-437-3" aria-label="Link to rule 437.3">437.3.</a> This action, when instructed, is formatted as "Predict X."</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-437-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-437-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 437.3.a">437.3.a.</a> If omitted, X is presumed to be 1.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-437-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-437-4" aria-label="Link to rule 437.4">437.4.</a> If a player attempts to Predict more cards than are available in their Main Deck, they will Predict as many as possible instead.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-437-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-437-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 437.4.a">437.4.a.</a> The Player will not perform a Burn Out as a result of Predicting with too few cards in their deck.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-438" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-438" aria-label="Link to rule 438">438.</a> Prevent</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-438-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-438-1" aria-label="Link to rule 438.1">438.1.</a> Preventing damage is the act of reducing the Damage a set of game objects would take.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-438-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-438-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 438.1.a">438.1.a.</a> Prevent is an action that interacts with Damage.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-438-1-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-438-1-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 438.1.a.1">438.1.a.1.</a> Prevent includes any applied Bonus Damage when determining the total damage being prevented.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-438-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-438-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 438.1.b">438.1.b.</a> Prevent appears in statements that define an amount of damage and the source of the damage it will affect, as well as the timespan it will be relevant for.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-438-1-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-438-1-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 438.1.b.1">438.1.b.1.</a> Prevent actions are usually formatted as “Prevent the next X [source] damage that would be dealt to a [unit] this turn.”</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-438-1-b-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-438-1-b-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 438.1.b.1.a">438.1.b.1.a.</a> The X is referred to as the Prevent Value.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-438-1-b-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-438-1-b-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 438.1.b.1.b">438.1.b.1.b.</a> X can be “All,” which specifies an infinite amount of damage.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-438-1-b-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-438-1-b-2" aria-label="Link to rule 438.1.b.2">438.1.b.2.</a> Prevent will always apply to the next damage that would be dealt to a unit affected by the Prevent action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-438-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-438-2" aria-label="Link to rule 438.2">438.2.</a> When damage is Prevented, it is replaced with an event where the source of the damage deals that much damage reduced by the Prevent Value tracked on the Unit specifically.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-438-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-438-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 438.2.a">438.2.a.</a> The damage being dealt as a result of Preventing can never be less than 0, but can be 0. This is equivalent to not dealing damage.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-438-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-438-3" aria-label="Link to rule 438.3">438.3.</a> When damage is dealt this way, reduce the Prevent Value being tracked on the Unit affected by the Prevent action by the prevented amount.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-438-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-438-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 438.3.a">438.3.a.</a> If the Prevent Value is 0 or less, then Prevent is no longer being tracked on the Unit in question, and the effect expires.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-438-3-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-438-3-b" aria-label="Link to rule 438.3.b">438.3.b.</a> The reduced value is the newly tracked Prevent Value.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-438-3-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-438-3-c" aria-label="Link to rule 438.3.c">438.3.c.</a> If the Prevent Value is “All” then it remains “All.”</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-438-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-438-4" aria-label="Link to rule 438.4">438.4.</a> Damage dealt to a Unit that has that all of that damage Prevented is not considered to have been dealt to it at all.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-438-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-438-5" aria-label="Link to rule 438.5">438.5.</a> Damage can still be assigned to Units in combat that are affected by Prevent. The damage dealt as a result of that assignment will be affected by the Prevent action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-438-5-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-438-5-a" aria-label="Link to rule 438.5.a">438.5.a.</a> Damage can be assigned to a Unit up to a value that would be Lethal considering the Prevent Value of all Prevent Actions on a Unit, following the normal rules for damage assignment.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A unit with 2 [M] is being assigned damage in the combat damage step. The unit has “prevent the first 3 damage I would take each combat.” The unit would need to be assigned 5 damage in order to have lethal damage assigned to it.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-438-5-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-438-5-b" aria-label="Link to rule 438.5.b">438.5.b.</a> No amount of damage is ever considered lethal if the Prevent Value is “All.”</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-438-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-438-6" aria-label="Link to rule 438.6">438.6.</a> Prevent is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-438-6-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-438-6-a" aria-label="Link to rule 438.6.a">438.6.a.</a> Players may only Prevent Damage when directed to by Game Effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-438-7" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-438-7" aria-label="Link to rule 438.7">438.7.</a> Prevent is a Delayed Replacement Effect.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-367">367</a>. Replacement Effects for more information.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-390">390</a>. Delayed Abilities for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-439" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-439" aria-label="Link to rule 439">439.</a> Replace</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-439-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-439-1" aria-label="Link to rule 439.1">439.1.</a> Replacing is the act of Creating a token in the place of another card or token without playing it while inheriting all effects or statuses of the game object it replaced.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-439-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-439-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 439.1.a">439.1.a.</a> The replacing token is treated as the same Game Object as the card or token it replaced for the purposes of Game Effects that target or reference that game object.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player with Green Father as their legend conquers Navori Fighting Pit. They choose to place the Green Father conquer effect on the chain after the Navori Fighting Pit conquer effect. When the Green Father trigger resolves, Navori Fighting Pit is replaced with Brush. Although the Navori Fighting Pit has been replaced, the “here” in its triggered ability still can have its information referenced, because the Brush inherited all statuses and conditions. The unit Navori Fighting Pit’s triggered ability has targeted will still be a legal target on resolution.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-439-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-439-2" aria-label="Link to rule 439.2">439.2.</a> Replacing is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-439-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-439-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 439.2.a">439.2.a.</a> The player may Replace cards and tokens when instructed to do so by other game effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-439-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-439-3" aria-label="Link to rule 439.3">439.3.</a> This action, when instructed, is formatted as "Replace [X] with [Y]."</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-439-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-439-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 439.3.a">439.3.a.</a> The [X] is the target to be Replaced.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-439-3-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-439-3-b" aria-label="Link to rule 439.3.b">439.3.b.</a> The [Y] is the object that will Replace the target.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-439-3-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-439-3-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 439.3.b.1">439.3.b.1.</a> This will always specify a Token to create.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-439-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-439-4" aria-label="Link to rule 439.4">439.4.</a> Replacing is not a subset of Banishing.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-439-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-439-5" aria-label="Link to rule 439.5">439.5.</a> The card or token that is Replaced is placed in Banishment.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-439-5-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-439-5-a" aria-label="Link to rule 439.5.a">439.5.a.</a> While it resides in Banishment, it is considered to have been Replaced and not Banished.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-439-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-439-6" aria-label="Link to rule 439.6">439.6.</a> If a token is Replaced it will stop existing once it begins its occupancy in Banishment.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-439-6-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-439-6-a" aria-label="Link to rule 439.6.a">439.6.a.</a> This does not invalidate the token created, or the act of Replacement.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-439-7" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-439-7" aria-label="Link to rule 439.7">439.7.</a> Tokens that have been Created through a Replace action can be instructed to be “Swapped back.” This may also appear as “replace [the token] with the [Game Object] it replaced.”</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-439-7-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-439-7-a" aria-label="Link to rule 439.7.a">439.7.a.</a> Swapping Back is an extension of the Replace action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-439-7-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-439-7-b" aria-label="Link to rule 439.7.b">439.7.b.</a> To Swap Back, the token stops existing and the original card is returned to the space that the token just occupied, inheriting all current effects and statuses.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-439-7-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-439-7-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 439.7.b.1">439.7.b.1.</a> Any card that has been Replaced by that token or any tokens it Replaced is eligible to swap back in this way.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-439-7-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-439-7-c" aria-label="Link to rule 439.7.c">439.7.c.</a> If there is nothing in Banishment to swap back to then this object can never swap back.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-440" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-440" aria-label="Link to rule 440">440.</a> Create</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-440-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-440-1" aria-label="Link to rule 440.1">440.1.</a> Creating is the act of producing a Game Object that previously did not exist in the game.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-440-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-440-2" aria-label="Link to rule 440.2">440.2.</a> Effects that Create one or more Game Objects will direct where those Game Objects must go - the Game Objects are Created directly to the zones in question.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-440-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-440-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 440.2.a">440.2.a.</a> Prior to being Created, these Game Objects did not exist outside of the zone they were Created to. After being Created, they may change zones as appropriate for Game Objects of their type.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-440-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-440-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 440.2.b">440.2.b.</a> If a zone is not specified by the effect, the Game Object will be created to the appropriate zone for its type.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-440-2-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-440-2-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 440.2.b.1">440.2.b.1.</a> Permanents will be Created at any location on the Board that they can be played to.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-440-2-b-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-440-2-b-2" aria-label="Link to rule 440.2.b.2">440.2.b.2.</a> Spells will be Created on the Chain.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-440-2-b-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-440-2-b-3" aria-label="Link to rule 440.2.b.3">440.2.b.3.</a> Runes will be Created at base.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-440-2-b-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-440-2-b-4" aria-label="Link to rule 440.2.b.4">440.2.b.4.</a> Legends will be Created in the Legend Zone.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-440-2-b-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-440-2-b-5" aria-label="Link to rule 440.2.b.5">440.2.b.5.</a> Battlefields will be Created in the Battlefield Zone.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-440-2-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-440-2-c" aria-label="Link to rule 440.2.c">440.2.c.</a> The zone a Game Object is created to can be specified implicitly by the Game Action being performed.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: “Play a 1 [M] Recruit token” specifies a zone where the token is Created to implicitly, by specifying the token is played and thus created on the chain.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: “Create a 1 [M] Recruit token” does not specify a zone, so the Recruit token will be created at any location on the board that it can be played to.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-440-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-440-3" aria-label="Link to rule 440.3">440.3.</a> An effect that Creates a Game Object will specify the state and nature of the Game Object created.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-440-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-440-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 440.3.a">440.3.a.</a> If the Game Object is a token, it will follow the normal rules for tokens.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-179">179</a>. Tokens for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-440-3-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-440-3-b" aria-label="Link to rule 440.3.b">440.3.b.</a> If the effect specifies a Game Object is Created for a specific duration, it exists while that duration is ongoing, and ceases to exist once that duration ends.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-440-3-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-440-3-c" aria-label="Link to rule 440.3.c">440.3.c.</a> If a Non-Token Game Object is Created from a finite set of choices made by a Linked Ability during deck building, only one of those Game Objects may exist in play at a time. If a player would Create a Game Object that already exists on the board or on the chain, that action is Negated.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-440-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-440-4" aria-label="Link to rule 440.4">440.4.</a> Unless specified otherwise by the Game Effect that Creates a Game Object, any such Game Object is owned by the player who Created it. Control is established as usual for Game Objects of the appropriate type.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-440-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-440-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 440.4.a">440.4.a.</a> A Created permanent, rune, legend, or spell is controlled by its owner as it is Created.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-440-4-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-440-4-b" aria-label="Link to rule 440.4.b">440.4.b.</a> A Created battlefield is uncontrolled as it is Created.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-440-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-440-5" aria-label="Link to rule 440.5">440.5.</a> This action, when instructed, is usually formatted as "Create [X] at [Y]" or “Add [X] to [Y].”</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-440-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-440-6" aria-label="Link to rule 440.6">440.6.</a> Creating is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-440-6-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-440-6-a" aria-label="Link to rule 440.6.a">440.6.a.</a> Players may only Create Game Objects when directed to by Game Effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-441" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-441" aria-label="Link to rule 441">441.</a> Burn</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-441-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-441-1" aria-label="Link to rule 441.1">441.1.</a> Burning is the act of moving cards from the top of a player’s Main Deck to their trash.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-441-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-441-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 441.1.a">441.1.a.</a> “When you burn” abilities or other Triggered Abilities that trigger on burning are executed after burning has occurred.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-441-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-441-2" aria-label="Link to rule 441.2">441.2.</a> This action is formatted as "Burn X."</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-441-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-441-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 441.2.a">441.2.a.</a> X is the number of cards to be Burned.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-441-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-441-3" aria-label="Link to rule 441.3">441.3.</a> Burning is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-441-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-441-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 441.3.a">441.3.a.</a> A player must Burn cards when instructed to do so by game effects or costs.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-441-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-441-4" aria-label="Link to rule 441.4">441.4.</a> When Burning is part of an effect, then a player must Burn as many cards as possible. If instructed to burn more cards than they have in their main deck, they burn that many cards, burn out and then burn the rest.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-432">432</a>. Burn Out for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-442" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-442" aria-label="Link to rule 442">442.</a> Empower</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-442-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-442-1" aria-label="Link to rule 442.1">442.1.</a> Empowering is the act of rendering one or more Game Objects Empowered.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-442-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-442-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 442.1.a">442.1.a.</a> Empowered is a binary state. A Game Object is Empowered or it isn’t.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-442-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-442-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 442.1.b">442.1.b.</a> An Empowered Game Object can not be Empowered.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-442-1-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-442-1-c" aria-label="Link to rule 442.1.c">442.1.c.</a> If a Game Object is instructed to be Empowered when it is already Empowered, nothing additional happens.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-442-1-c-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-442-1-c-1" aria-label="Link to rule 442.1.c.1">442.1.c.1.</a> Some effects may grant a Game Object permission to be Empowered multiple times. Such an effect ignores this restriction. For these Game Objects, Empowered is not a binary state but a stacking status.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-442-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-442-2" aria-label="Link to rule 442.2">442.2.</a> “Empowered” is a state for Game Objects on the board that other game effects and abilities can reference.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-442-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-442-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 442.2.a">442.2.a.</a> When a Game Object becomes Empowered as a result of the Empower game action, that is an event that can similarly be referenced by game effects and abilities.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-442-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-442-3" aria-label="Link to rule 442.3">442.3.</a> Empowering is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-442-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-442-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 442.3.a">442.3.a.</a> Players may only Empower Game Objects when directed to by Game Effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-443" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-443" aria-label="Link to rule 443">443.</a> Disempower</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-443-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-443-1" aria-label="Link to rule 443.1">443.1.</a> Disempowering is the act of removing the Empowered status from one or more Game Objects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-443-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-443-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 443.1.a">443.1.a.</a> Disempowering affects only cards that are currently Empowered.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-443-1-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-443-1-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 443.1.a.1">443.1.a.1.</a> Game effects that instruct a player to Disempower a card that is not currently Empowered will do nothing.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-443-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-443-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 443.1.b">443.1.b.</a> Disempowering a Game Object that has multiple Empowered statuses will remove one of those statuses.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-443-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-443-2" aria-label="Link to rule 443.2">443.2.</a> Disempowering is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-443-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-443-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 443.2.a">443.2.a.</a> Players may only Disempower Game Objects when directed to by Game Effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-444" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-444" aria-label="Link to rule 444">444.</a> Skip</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-444-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-444-1" aria-label="Link to rule 444.1">444.1.</a> Skipping an event is the act of replacing that event with nothing.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-444-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-444-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 444.1.a">444.1.a.</a> Skip is an action that interacts with events and procedures of the turn.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A card reads in part “Choose an enemy unit. Skip the next time that unit would move this turn.” This is a replacement effect that replaces the event of a unit moving with nothing.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A card reads in part “Skip your Draw phase.” This is a replacement effect that replaces a player’s Draw Phase with nothing.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A card reads in part “Choose a player. They skip their next point from conquering this turn.” This is a replacement effect that replaces the 1 point gained when conquering with nothing.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-444-1-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-444-1-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 444.1.a.1">444.1.a.1.</a> An event is the singular moment that results from a Game Action being performed or from a Game Object changing state.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-367">367</a>. Replacement Effects for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-444-1-a-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-444-1-a-2" aria-label="Link to rule 444.1.a.2">444.1.a.2.</a> Procedures of the turn include turns, phases, steps, and substeps.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-444-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-444-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 444.1.b">444.1.b.</a> Skip actions are usually formatted as “Skip [event or procedure of the turn].”</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-444-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-444-2" aria-label="Link to rule 444.2">444.2.</a> When an event or procedure of the turn is Skipped, it is replaced with nothing. Play proceeds accordingly.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-444-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-444-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 444.2.a">444.2.a.</a> Anything that triggers on the occurrence of the event or procedure of the turn does not trigger.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-444-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-444-3" aria-label="Link to rule 444.3">444.3.</a> Skip is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-444-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-444-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 444.3.a">444.3.a.</a> Players may only Skip events or procedures of the turn when directed to by Game Effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-444-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-444-4" aria-label="Link to rule 444.4">444.4.</a> Skip is a Replacement Effect.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-367">367</a>. Replacement Effects for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-445" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-445" aria-label="Link to rule 445">445.</a> Pay</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-445-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-445-1" aria-label="Link to rule 445.1">445.1.</a> Paying a resource is the act of removing that resource from your Rune Pool.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-445-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-445-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 445.1.a">445.1.a.</a> Pay is an action that interacts with Energy and Power.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-445-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-445-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 445.1.b">445.1.b.</a> In order to qualify as a resource to be removed from the Rune Pool, the resource must be [1] or greater, or [C] or [A] or greater.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: [0] is not a resource that can be removed from the Rune Pool. As a cost, it is paid without performing the pay game action. This cannot be declined.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-445-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-445-2" aria-label="Link to rule 445.2">445.2.</a> When a player is instructed to Pay a resource, that player may remove that resource from their Rune Pool if it exists there. If they choose not to, the instruction is negated.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-445-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-445-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 445.2.a">445.2.a.</a> If the instruction to Pay resources is part of paying costs for a card or ability, choosing not to pay those resources will cause the act of playing the card or ability to be undone.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-358">358</a>. Check Legality for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-445-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-445-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 445.2.b">445.2.b.</a> If the instruction to Pay resources is part of paying any other cost, choosing not to pay those resources will prevent the execution of the associated effect.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-201">201</a>. Costs for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-445-2-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-445-2-c" aria-label="Link to rule 445.2.c">445.2.c.</a> Players may activate abilities that Add resources with the Reaction keyword at any time that they are instructed to Pay resources. Those abilities finalize and resolve immediately, ignoring normal restrictions.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-430">430</a>. Add for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-445-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-445-3" aria-label="Link to rule 445.3">445.3.</a> Pay is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-445-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-445-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 445.3.a">445.3.a.</a> Players may only Pay resources when directed to by Game Effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-446" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-446" aria-label="Link to rule 446">446.</a> Copy</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-446-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-446-1" aria-label="Link to rule 446.1">446.1.</a> Copying a Game Object is the act of adding or replacing some or all of the copyable traits of another Game Object with those of the Copied Game Object.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-446-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-446-2" aria-label="Link to rule 446.2">446.2.</a> Copy Effects are rendered in the Copy Effects layer.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-475">475</a>. Layers for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-446-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-446-3" aria-label="Link to rule 446.3">446.3.</a> Some Game Objects may be created as a Copy of another Game Object. This is not a Copy Effect. The Copy in such a case is rendered with the same copyable traits as those of the Game Object it is a Copy of. It has no printed traits of its own that are subsequently replaced.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Zilean, Time Mage is a unit that reads “Once each turn, if you would play a token unit while I'm at a battlefield, you may play that token and an additional copy of it instead.” The additional copy played by Zilean’s replacement effect has no printed traits of its own. Its printed traits are those of the token it is a copy of.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-446-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-446-4" aria-label="Link to rule 446.4">446.4.</a> Copy is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-446-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-446-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 446.4.a">446.4.a.</a> Players may only Copy Game Objects when directed to by Game Effects.</p>
</div>

### 447. Movement {#rule-447}


<div id="rule-448" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-448" aria-label="Link to rule 448">448.</a> Moving is a Limited Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-448-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-448-1" aria-label="Link to rule 448.1">448.1.</a> A Permanent changing its position from any space on the Board to another space on the Board is a Move, unless it is caused by a corrective Recall or an Attached Permanent changing locations to or with its Top-Most Card.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-421">421</a>. Move for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-448-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-448-2" aria-label="Link to rule 448.2">448.2.</a> A card changing game zones does not in itself constitute a Move.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-448-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-448-3" aria-label="Link to rule 448.3">448.3.</a> Moving is instantaneous.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-448-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-448-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 448.3.a">448.3.a.</a> There is no state for Permanents between locations.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-448-3-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-448-3-b" aria-label="Link to rule 448.3.b">448.3.b.</a> Permanents are either at their Origin before Moving or their Destination after Moving.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-448-3-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-448-3-c" aria-label="Link to rule 448.3.c">448.3.c.</a> Moving does not use the Chain, nor is it able to be Reacted to.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-449" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-449" aria-label="Link to rule 449">449.</a> Moving is defined by the Origin and Destination of the Permanent that is changing locations.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-449-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-449-1" aria-label="Link to rule 449.1">449.1.</a> The Origin is where the Permanent is starting from.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-449-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-449-2" aria-label="Link to rule 449.2">449.2.</a> The Destination is where the Permanent is going to.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-449-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-449-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 449.2.a">449.2.a.</a> In Modes of Play with more than two players, Battlefields with Staged Combats or Combats in Progress are Invalid Destinations for Moves of all kinds (Standard Moves or otherwise) by Units controlled by Players not involved in those Combats or who don't already have units at that Battlefield.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-449-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-449-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 449.2.b">449.2.b.</a> In Modes of Play with teammates, Battlefields occupied by a player’s teammates are Invalid Destinations for Moves of all kinds (Standard Moves or otherwise) by Units that player controls.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-449-2-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-449-2-c" aria-label="Link to rule 449.2.c">449.2.c.</a> If an action would require a Move that would cause a Unit to become present in a Location where it cannot move for any reason, such as a Battlefield with two players that are not the controller of the Unit performing this Move action are in a Combat or such a Combat is Staged, it instead Recalls.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-456">456</a>. Recalls for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-450" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-450" aria-label="Link to rule 450">450.</a> Players may choose to move their Units with the Standard Move.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-144">144</a>. for more information on the Standard Move.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-451" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-451" aria-label="Link to rule 451">451.</a> Spells, Abilities, or other effects may cause a Move to occur.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-451-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-451-1" aria-label="Link to rule 451.1">451.1.</a> The source of the Move will provide details on any restrictions on legality for Destination.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-451-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-451-2" aria-label="Link to rule 451.2">451.2.</a> Units cannot Move to a Battlefield that already has units from 2 other players present by any means.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-452" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-452" aria-label="Link to rule 452">452.</a> The Destination becomes Contested if it is an Uncontested Battlefield not controlled by the controller of the Unit or Units that moved.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-453" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-453" aria-label="Link to rule 453">453.</a> Units may cause a Non-Combat Showdown when they Move.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-341">341</a>. Showdowns for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-454" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-454" aria-label="Link to rule 454">454.</a> Units may cause Combat when they Move.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-461">461</a>. Combat for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-455" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-455" aria-label="Link to rule 455">455.</a> When a Move action is complete, perform a Cleanup.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-456" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-456" aria-label="Link to rule 456">456.</a> Recalls</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-457" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-457" aria-label="Link to rule 457">457.</a> A Recall is when a Permanent is relocated from anywhere to its Base without it being a Move.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-458" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-458" aria-label="Link to rule 458">458.</a> Recalls are not Moves.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-458-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-458-1" aria-label="Link to rule 458.1">458.1.</a> They do not cause Triggered Abilities to trigger that are triggered by Move actions.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-458-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-458-2" aria-label="Link to rule 458.2">458.2.</a> A Recall causes a Permanent to change locations.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-458-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-458-3" aria-label="Link to rule 458.3">458.3.</a> A Recall cannot be prevented by actions and Game Effects that restrict or block Movement.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-459" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-459" aria-label="Link to rule 459">459.</a> Gear can be Recalled.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-459-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-459-1" aria-label="Link to rule 459.1">459.1.</a> When an un-attached non-Unit Gear without deploy is created or played at a battlefield, or is at a battlefield for any other reason, it is Recalled to its controller's base during the next Cleanup.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: An Equipment is attached to a unit at a battlefield, so the Equipment is present at that battlefield. If the unit dies, the Equipment will be recalled during the next cleanup.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-460" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-460" aria-label="Link to rule 460">460.</a> Recalls do not affect the state of the Permanent being recalled.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-460-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-460-1" aria-label="Link to rule 460.1">460.1.</a> Unless otherwise stated by the source of the Recall, Damage and statuses of a permanent will all remain unaffected by a Recall.</p>
</div>

### 461. Combat {#rule-461}


<div id="rule-462" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-462" aria-label="Link to rule 462">462.</a> A Combat occurs when a Cleanup occurs, there are no items on the Chain, there is a staged Combat at a Battlefield, and no Showdown or Combat is ongoing at any other Battlefield.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-318">318</a>. Cleanups for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-462-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-462-1" aria-label="Link to rule 462.1">462.1.</a> If there is an ongoing Showdown at the Battlefield where the Combat is staged, that Showdown will become a Combat Showdown and a Combat will be initiated there.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-463" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-463" aria-label="Link to rule 463">463.</a> Combat is considered Staged if there are units controlled by two opposing players at a Battlefield but the Steps of Combat have not been initiated.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-463-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-463-1" aria-label="Link to rule 463.1">463.1.</a> If more than one Battlefield has Units controlled by opposing players at it at the same time, the Turn Player decides which Combat to resolve first.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-463-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-463-2" aria-label="Link to rule 463.2">463.2.</a> If Staged Combats stop being Staged before the Steps of Combat are initiated, they are not resolved or executed.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-463-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-463-3" aria-label="Link to rule 463.3">463.3.</a> If a Combat and Showdown are staged at the same Battlefield and the turn player initiates the Showdown, it will open as a Combat Showdown.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-464" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-464" aria-label="Link to rule 464">464.</a> Combat can only occur between Units controlled by exactly two players.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-464-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-464-1" aria-label="Link to rule 464.1">464.1.</a> In Modes of Play with more than two players, Battlefields with Staged Combats or Combats in Progress are Invalid Destinations for Moves of all kinds (Standard Moves or otherwise) by Units controlled by Players not involved in those Combats or who don't already have Units at that Battlefield.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-449-2-a">449.2.a</a>. for more information on Invalid Destinations.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-464-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-464-2" aria-label="Link to rule 464.2">464.2.</a> In Modes of Play with more than two players, Battlefields with Staged Combats or Combats in Progress are Invalid to be chosen as a location to play one or more Units by a player not involved in that Combat by any means.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-464-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-464-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 464.2.a">464.2.a.</a> If an effect would require a Unit be played to a Battlefield with a Staged Combat or a Combat in Progress, where the controller of the played unit is not a participant, instead the Unit is played to its controller's Base.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-464-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-464-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 464.2.b">464.2.b.</a> Any subsequent reference to "here" in the corresponding effect is reassigned to the Controller's Base, where the Unit was played. Any further effects that may be invalidated are invalidated as if the effect was mistargeted.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-355-6">355.6</a>. Targeting for more information on Mistargeting.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-464-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-464-3" aria-label="Link to rule 464.3">464.3.</a> All choices that would result in a Combat occurring between more than two players simultaneously are invalid and ineligible to be completed.</p>
</div>

### 465. The Steps of Combat {#rule-465}


<div id="rule-466" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-466" aria-label="Link to rule 466">466.</a> Step 1: The Combat Showdown Step</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-466-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-466-1" aria-label="Link to rule 466.1">466.1.</a> Combat will open in one of two ways: when a Combat and Showdown are staged at the same Battlefield and the turn player initiates the Showdown; or when the turn is in a Showdown Open State and a Combat is staged at the Battlefield where the current Showdown is ongoing.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-318">318</a>. Cleanups for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-466-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-466-2" aria-label="Link to rule 466.2">466.2.</a> When Combat opens, it either opens with a Combat Showdown, or the current Showdown becomes a Combat Showdown.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-466-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-466-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 466.2.a">466.2.a.</a> The following Tasks become Outstanding, in the order described:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-466-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-466-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 466.2.b">466.2.b.</a> 1. Start of combat effects happen now. If the Combat opened with a Combat Showdown, start of showdown effects happen simultaneously.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-466-2-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-466-2-c" aria-label="Link to rule 466.2.c">466.2.c.</a> 2. Establish who is Attacker and who is Defender.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-466-2-c-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-466-2-c-1" aria-label="Link to rule 466.2.c.1">466.2.c.1.</a> The Attacker is the player whose unit(s) applied the Contested status to the Battlefield.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-466-2-c-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-466-2-c-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 466.2.c.1.a">466.2.c.1.a.</a> If a showdown opens as part of combat, this player gains Focus as the showdown begins.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-466-2-c-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-466-2-c-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 466.2.c.1.b">466.2.c.1.b.</a> If a showdown was already ongoing when the combat opens, the player who has Focus maintains their Focus.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-466-2-c-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-466-2-c-2" aria-label="Link to rule 466.2.c.2">466.2.c.2.</a> The Defender is the player who did not apply the Contested status to the Battlefield.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-466-2-c-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-466-2-c-3" aria-label="Link to rule 466.2.c.3">466.2.c.3.</a> The attacker and defender gain the Attacker or Defender designation now, as appropriate. Units at the Contested Battlefield controlled by the Attacker or Defender gain the Attacker or Defender designation now, as appropriate.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-466-2-c-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-466-2-c-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 466.2.c.3.a">466.2.c.3.a.</a> If a Unit controlled by the Attacker or Defender becomes present at this Battlefield after this moment, it will gain the Attacker or Defender designation during the Cleanup phase following the action that caused it to become present, as appropriate for its controller.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-466-2-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-466-2-d" aria-label="Link to rule 466.2.d">466.2.d.</a> 3. Add items to the Combat Chain if establishing Attacker and Defender has caused Triggered Abilities to become Pending.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-466-2-d-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-466-2-d-1" aria-label="Link to rule 466.2.d.1">466.2.d.1.</a> The Attacking player, who has Focus, places Triggered Abilities on the Chain first, followed by all non-Defender players in Turn Order, followed by the Defending Player.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-466-2-e" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-466-2-e" aria-label="Link to rule 466.2.e">466.2.e.</a> The State Closes if a Combat Chain was created.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-466-2-e-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-466-2-e-1" aria-label="Link to rule 466.2.e.1">466.2.e.1.</a> Otherwise the Combat Showdown continues, with the State Open as normal.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-466-2-f" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-466-2-f" aria-label="Link to rule 466.2.f">466.2.f.</a> Players proceed with any play on the Chain as normal.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-467" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-467" aria-label="Link to rule 467">467.</a> Step 2: The Combat Damage Step</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-467-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-467-1" aria-label="Link to rule 467.1">467.1.</a> If both Attacking and Defending units remain at this battlefield, the following Tasks become Outstanding, in the specified order:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-467-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-467-2" aria-label="Link to rule 467.2">467.2.</a> 1. When the Showdown closes, Attackers and Defenders resolve Combat Damage at the Battlefield that was attacked, using their current Might.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-467-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-467-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 467.2.a">467.2.a.</a> Sum the Might of all Attacking Units.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-467-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-467-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 467.2.b">467.2.b.</a> Sum the Might of all Defending Units.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-467-2-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-467-2-c" aria-label="Link to rule 467.2.c">467.2.c.</a> Starting with the Attacker, each player assigns an amount of damage equal to their summed Might among the other's Units.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-467-2-c-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-467-2-c-1" aria-label="Link to rule 467.2.c.1">467.2.c.1.</a> Assigning Damage is not Dealing Damage.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-467-2-c-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-467-2-c-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 467.2.c.1.a">467.2.c.1.a.</a> When all Damage is assigned, it will be Dealt simultaneously. These actions are not synonymous.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-467-2-c-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-467-2-c-2" aria-label="Link to rule 467.2.c.2">467.2.c.2.</a> Abilities or effects may influence the order in which damage is assigned. Reminder: Lethal Damage is non-zero damage equaling or exceeding the Might of a Unit.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-467-2-c-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-467-2-c-3" aria-label="Link to rule 467.2.c.3">467.2.c.3.</a> Units must have lethal damage assigned to them in full before damage is assigned to a different Unit.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: If a player has 5 damage to distribute among four 3 Might units, they may not choose to assign 2 damage to one of the units and 1 damage to each of the remaining 3. They must assign 3 damage to one, and the remaining 2 to another.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-467-2-c-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-467-2-c-4" aria-label="Link to rule 467.2.c.4">467.2.c.4.</a> Units cannot have more damage assigned to them than the minimum required to constitute lethal damage unless no further units remain to have damage assigned to them.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: If a player has 5 damage to distribute among four 3 Might units and those units each have 1 damage already marked on them, that player may not assign more than 2 damage to any of those units.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-467-2-c-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-467-2-c-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 467.2.c.4.a">467.2.c.4.a.</a> If the damage assigned to a Unit is modified to or replaced by an amount of damage larger than the initial value, the assigning player must choose the minimum applied value such that the unit would take lethal damage.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A unit with 3 [M] is being assigned damage in the combat damage step. There are other units without damage assigned to them with the same controller. The unit has a delayed replacement effect applied to it that reads “Double all damage that would be dealt to it this turn.” When assigning damage, the assigning player can only choose to assign 1 or 2 damage to this unit—when doing so, the assigned damage is doubled to 2 or 4 damage respectively. The minimum applied value such that the unit would take lethal damage in this way is 4 damage.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-467-2-c-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
