<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-467-2-c-5" aria-label="Link to rule 467.2.c.5">467.2.c.5.</a> When assigning damage in this way, passive abilities and replacement effects that would apply to the resulting damage are considered to apply to the assignment instead.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A unit with 2 [M] is being assigned damage in the combat damage step. The unit has “prevent the first 3 damage I would take each combat.” The unit would need to be assigned 5 damage in order to have lethal damage assigned to it.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: The attacking player is assigning their 3 [M] worth of damage to two defending units with 2 [M] each. One of the units has a delayed replacement effect applied to it that reads “Double all damage that would be dealt to it this turn.” The attacking player assigns two damage to the other defending unit, then when assigning damage to the unit with the delayed replacement effect they assign 2 damage to it; 1 damage that doubles to 2 damage as it is assigned to the unit. When that damage is dealt, it doesn’t get doubled again—the doubling is considered to have already happened during damage assignment.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: The attacking player is assigning their 3 [M] worth of damage to a 2 [M] unit. That unit has a prevent value of two being applied to it, as well as the effect of Lotus Trap, doubling the damage dealt to them. Both of these replacement effects apply to the assignment of damage, in the order of the controller of the 2 [M] unit’s choice. If they choose to order the replacement effects so that the prevent value is applied first, the unit will prevent 2 of the assigned damage, then the last 1 point of damage will be doubled to 2. The unit will have 4 damage assigned to it. When damage is dealt, the unit will take 2 damage. If they choose the other order, the unit will have 6 damage assigned to it, 2 of which will be prevented. When damage is dealt, the unit will take 4 damage.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-467-2-c-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-467-2-c-6" aria-label="Link to rule 467.2.c.6">467.2.c.6.</a> A player must obey all requirements and restrictions on damage assignment if able.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player is assigning damage to the following units: a unit with Tank ("I must be assigned combat damage first."); a unit with Backline ("I must be assigned combat damage last."); and another unit without any abilities. That player must assign combat damage first to the unit with Tank, then to the unit with no abilities, then to the unit with Backline.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-467-2-c-7" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-467-2-c-7" aria-label="Link to rule 467.2.c.7">467.2.c.7.</a> If multiple Units have abilities or effects that require a player to assign them damage with the same priority, that player may assign damage to those units in any order.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player is assigning damage to the following units: two units with Tank ("I must be assigned combat damage first.") and one unit with no abilities. That player chooses one of the units with Tank and assigns combat damage to it. Then they must assign any remaining damage first to the other unit with Tank, then to the unit with no abilities.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-467-2-c-8" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-467-2-c-8" aria-label="Link to rule 467.2.c.8">467.2.c.8.</a> If a Unit has one or more Abilities or effects applying to it that demand it be assigned damage in a specific way that is exclusionary, then the assigning player chooses only one of those abilities to apply when assigning damage.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Caitlyn, Patrolling with the Backline ability ("I must be assigned combat damage last.") has been given the Tank ability ("I must be assigned combat damage first."). A player is assigning damage to this Caitlyn with Tank and two units with no abilities. That player can’t fulfill both of Caitlyn’s damage requirements, so they may choose to assign damage to Caitlyn first, fulfilling the Tank requirement, or last, fulfilling the Backline requirement. They can’t choose to apply damage to Caitlyn in between the other two units, because that wouldn’t fulfill either requirement.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-467-2-c-9" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-467-2-c-9" aria-label="Link to rule 467.2.c.9">467.2.c.9.</a> If there is more than one unit in which this situation applies to, each unit is dealt with individually. The assigning player chooses which ability or effect applies, and then resolves the assignment. If this creates a situation where now more than one unit must be assigned with the same priority, those units may be assigned damage in any order as normal within that priority.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Two copies of Caitlyn, Patrolling with the Backline ability ("I must be assigned combat damage last.") have been given the Tank ability ("I must be assigned combat damage first."). A player assigning damage to these two Caitlyns and one unit with no abilities could choose to fulfill both Caitlyns’ Tank requirements by assigning them both damage before the other unit.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-467-2-c-10" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-467-2-c-10" aria-label="Link to rule 467.2.c.10">467.2.c.10.</a> If a unit cannot be dealt damage, then no amount of damage can be considered lethal. Such a unit is exempt from any considerations of mandatory assignment.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Kayn, Unleashed says “If I have moved twice this turn, I don't take damage.” While Kayn can’t take damage, it is ignored for the purposes of assigning lethal damage in combat.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Counter Strike says “Choose a unit. The next time that unit would be dealt damage this turn, prevent it.” The unit in question has a replacement effect applied to it, but it still can be dealt damage. Thus, it is not exempt from considerations of mandatory assignment.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-467-2-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-467-2-d" aria-label="Link to rule 467.2.d">467.2.d.</a> Deal Damage to each unit equal to the amount assigned to it.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-467-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-467-3" aria-label="Link to rule 467.3">467.3.</a> 2. Skip the FEPR process and cancel any outstanding tasks. Proceed to the Resolution Step.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-468" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-468" aria-label="Link to rule 468">468.</a> Step 3: The Resolution Step</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-468-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-468-1" aria-label="Link to rule 468.1">468.1.</a> 1. Perform a Combat Cleanup.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-468-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-468-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 468.1.a">468.1.a.</a> Invoke a Combat Special Cleanup.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-468-1-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-468-1-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 468.1.a.1">468.1.a.1.</a> Insert “3c. Heal all Units.”</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-468-1-a-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-468-1-a-2" aria-label="Link to rule 468.1.a.2">468.1.a.2.</a> Insert “3d. Recall Attackers present at the Battlefield if Defenders are still present.”</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-456">456</a>. Recalls for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-468-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-468-2" aria-label="Link to rule 468.2">468.2.</a> The following Task becomes Outstanding: Reminder: Resolve any items on the chain from dealing combat damage and the Combat Cleanup and associated FEPR before performing this step.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-468-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-468-3" aria-label="Link to rule 468.3">468.3.</a> 1. Determine Combat Result</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-468-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-468-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 468.3.a">468.3.a.</a> A Player has won a combat if they received either the attacker or defender designation and are the only Player that has units remaining at this battlefield during this step.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-468-3-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-468-3-b" aria-label="Link to rule 468.3.b">468.3.b.</a> A Player has lost a combat if they received either the attacker or defender designation and are the only Player that does not have any units remaining at this battlefield during this step.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-468-3-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-468-3-c" aria-label="Link to rule 468.3.c">468.3.c.</a> Units at this battlefield inherit the same combat result as their controllers.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-468-3-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-468-3-d" aria-label="Link to rule 468.3.d">468.3.d.</a> There is “No Result” if units were recalled during step 3d of the Combat Cleanup, if both Players have units present during this task, or if neither player has units present during this task.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-468-3-d-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-468-3-d-1" aria-label="Link to rule 468.3.d.1">468.3.d.1.</a> If “No Result” was reached, and both players have units remaining, stage a Showdown and a Combat at this battlefield.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-468-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-468-4" aria-label="Link to rule 468.4">468.4.</a> The following Task becomes Outstanding: Reminder: Resolve any items on the chain from determining combat result and associated FEPR before performing this step.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-468-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-468-5" aria-label="Link to rule 468.5">468.5.</a> 1. If no Showdown or Combat is staged at this location, the player with Units remaining here Establishes Control if they didn’t already control this Battlefield.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-468-5-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-468-5-a" aria-label="Link to rule 468.5.a">468.5.a.</a> Clear the Contested Status.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-468-5-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-468-5-b" aria-label="Link to rule 468.5.b">468.5.b.</a> If there are no Units remaining here controlled by any player, the Battlefield becomes Uncontrolled.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-468-5-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-468-5-c" aria-label="Link to rule 468.5.c">468.5.c.</a> Remove all Hidden cards from this Battlefield that do not share a controller with the Battlefield.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-468-5-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-468-5-d" aria-label="Link to rule 468.5.d">468.5.d.</a> Establishing Control results in a Conquer if that player has not yet scored this Battlefield this turn.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-188">188</a>. Control for more information on Control.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-471-1">471.1</a>. for more information on Conquering.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-468-5-e" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-468-5-e" aria-label="Link to rule 468.5.e">468.5.e.</a> This does not have to be the player that applied Contested to the Battlefield.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-468-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-468-6" aria-label="Link to rule 468.6">468.6.</a> The following Task becomes Outstanding: Reminder: Resolve any items on the chain from establishing control and associated FEPR before performing this step.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-468-7" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-468-7" aria-label="Link to rule 468.7">468.7.</a> 1. Combat ends.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-468-7-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-468-7-a" aria-label="Link to rule 468.7.a">468.7.a.</a> Remove Attacker and Defender Designation from all Units and Players.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-468-7-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-468-7-b" aria-label="Link to rule 468.7.b">468.7.b.</a> At the end of Combat or Combat ends effects take place.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-468-7-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-468-7-c" aria-label="Link to rule 468.7.c">468.7.c.</a> All “this combat” effects expire simultaneously.</p>
</div>

### 469. Scoring {#rule-469}


<div id="rule-470" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-470" aria-label="Link to rule 470">470.</a> Scoring is the act of a Player seizing or maintaining control over Battlefields they have not yet Scored this turn.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-470-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-470-1" aria-label="Link to rule 470.1">470.1.</a> Scoring is a distinct process from Gaining Points. Scoring may result in a player Gaining Points, but the two are not intrinsically linked.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-470-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-470-2" aria-label="Link to rule 470.2">470.2.</a> Card text will refer to the process of Gaining Points in the following way: “Score X points.” The “score” in this text does not refer to the process of Scoring.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-470-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-470-3" aria-label="Link to rule 470.3">470.3.</a> Card text will refer to the process of Scoring using the word “score” without referring to some number of points. This does refer to the process of Scoring.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-471" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-471" aria-label="Link to rule 471">471.</a> A player Scores in one of two ways:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-471-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-471-1" aria-label="Link to rule 471.1">471.1.</a> Conquer: A player gains Control of a Battlefield they did not yet Score this turn.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-471-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-471-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 471.1.a">471.1.a.</a> In Modes of Play with teammates, Battlefields under the Control of a teammate during the scoring step of the Beginning Phase of a player’s turn are also disqualified from being Scored through Conquer by any means by that player’s team.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-471-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-471-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 471.1.b">471.1.b.</a> A player will gain control of a Battlefield after establishing Control.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-471-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-471-2" aria-label="Link to rule 471.2">471.2.</a> Hold: A player maintains Control of a Battlefield they did not yet Score this turn during their Beginning Phase.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-472" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-472" aria-label="Link to rule 472">472.</a> A player may only Score, from either method, once per Battlefield per turn.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-473" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-473" aria-label="Link to rule 473">473.</a> When a player Scores, two things occur:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-473-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-473-1" aria-label="Link to rule 473.1">473.1.</a> The player Gains up to one Point, depending on their current score.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-473-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-473-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 473.1.a">473.1.a.</a> The Final Point has additional restrictions.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-473-1-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-473-1-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 473.1.a.1">473.1.a.1.</a> Notably, points Gained from sources that are not Conquer are not beholden to these restrictions.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-473-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-473-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 473.1.b">473.1.b.</a> When a player tries to Gain a Point through a Conquer, and their current Point Total is 1 point from the Victory Score of the Mode of Play or higher, the following occurs:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-473-1-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-473-1-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 473.1.b.1">473.1.b.1.</a> If the player has Scored every Battlefield this turn, that player Gains the Final Point. If the player has not Scored every Battlefield this turn, that player draws a card instead.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-473-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-473-2" aria-label="Link to rule 473.2">473.2.</a> Trigger Score abilities at the Battlefield that Scored.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-473-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-473-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 473.2.a">473.2.a.</a> Conquer abilities trigger at a Battlefield that was Conquered.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-473-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-473-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 473.2.b">473.2.b.</a> Hold abilities trigger at a Battlefield that was Held.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-473-2-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-473-2-c" aria-label="Link to rule 473.2.c">473.2.c.</a> These will only trigger when the Battlefield is Scored; I.E. These cannot be triggered more than once per turn for a player.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-474" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-474" aria-label="Link to rule 474">474.</a> When a cleanup occurs and a player has accrued Points greater than or equal to the Victory Score for their Mode of Play, and if they have more points than every opponent, they Win the Game.</p>
</div>

### 475. Layers {#rule-475}


<div id="rule-476" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-476" aria-label="Link to rule 476">476.</a> Layers are the mechanism in which Game Effects alter the Traits, Intrinsic Abilities, or other properties of Game Objects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-477" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-477" aria-label="Link to rule 477">477.</a> Layers are an organizational structure.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-477-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-477-1" aria-label="Link to rule 477.1">477.1.</a> Layers only serve to structure the application and order that Game Effects apply to Game Objects to maintain consistency.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-478" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-478" aria-label="Link to rule 478">478.</a> The layers are applied repeatedly until all effects operating on an object have been applied once and no changes have been processed.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-478-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-478-1" aria-label="Link to rule 478.1">478.1.</a> Layers are applied and evaluated continuously. All Game Objects have their layers updated in the same instant, but each Game Object is treated separately and individually when evaluating layers.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-478-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-478-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 478.1.a">478.1.a.</a> If evaluating a Game Effect’s applicability depends on information from one or more other Game Objects, that Game Effect has an object level Dependency. Pause evaluating the layers of the dependent Game Object and evaluate the layer effects of those Game Objects whose information is referenced. Continue once the applicability of that Game Effect has been established or disqualified.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A 4 [M] unit reads in part “While I’m [Mighty], I’m a Yeti.” Another unit reads in part “I have all tags of units you control.” When the 4 [M] unit is buffed, the layers for all game objects are evaluated. While evaluating the layers for the second unit, it requires us to pause and evaluate the layers for all other units. When we’re evaluating the layers for the 4 [M] unit, after all is said and done, the unit has the Yeti tag. Once we’ve evaluated all units, we go back to evaluating the second unit, and at that time it has the Yeti tag as well.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-478-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-478-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 478.1.b">478.1.b.</a> If a Game Object has an object level dependency with a Game Object that itself has an object level dependency with a third Game Object and so on, determine the final point of the dependency chain and evaluate the layer effects of that Game Object first, continuing up the dependency chain until all Game Objects have had their layer effects evaluated.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-478-1-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-478-1-c" aria-label="Link to rule 478.1.c">478.1.c.</a> If a dependency chain loops back around on itself, identify the minimum set of Game Objects in the loop and evaluate their layer effects simultaneously, layer by layer. If any of them have Game Effects that reference Game Objects from outside the set, pause and evaluate those Game Objects first before continuing with the set.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A 4 [M] unit reads “If there is a [Mighty] enemy unit here, I have +5 [M].” Both players control one of these units at a battlefield during combat. Each one is buffed. A third player plays Siphon Power, which reads “Choose a battlefield. Give friendly units there +1 [M] this turn, and enemy units there -1 [M] this turn, to a minimum of 1 [M].” Both of the units there are given -1 [M]. Because they have effects that reference each other, they have a dependency chain. After evaluating their layers simultaneously, layer by layer, both units will be 4 [M].</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-478-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-478-2" aria-label="Link to rule 478.2">478.2.</a> Layers are applied in sequence. Each effect in them is applied as soon as able, and only a single time across all sequences.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-478-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-478-3" aria-label="Link to rule 478.3">478.3.</a> When a sequence of applications completes, recur the process, and evaluate each layer again applying any effects that may now be applicable.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-478-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-478-4" aria-label="Link to rule 478.4">478.4.</a> The removal or disqualification of an effect is separate from the application of the effect, but still can only be applied once.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Fiora, Victorious has printed Might 4 and says “While I'm Mighty, I have Deflect, Ganking, and Shield.” If a player places a buff on Fiora, her Might is increased in the Arithmetic layer, after the layer for Ability-Altering Effects. The Ability-Altering Effect layer is then re-checked and the abilities Deflect, Ganking, and Shield applied. Since each effect has been applied once and there are no other effects to apply, Fiora’s characteristics are finalized as 5 Might with Deflect, Ganking, and Shield. While a buffed Fiora, Victorious is in combat as a defender, an additional +1 Might will be applied in the Arithmetic layer, giving her 6 Might and the 3 keywords.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A buffed Fiora, Victorious is in combat as a defender when her buff is removed. Reevaluating the layers in sequence, she no longer gains Deflect, Ganking, and Shield during the Ability-Altering Effect layer, so when the Arithmetic layer is evaluated, neither the buff (which is gone) nor Shield (which she no longer has) apply. She goes directly from 6 Might with three keywords to 4 Might with no keywords.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-479" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-479" aria-label="Link to rule 479">479.</a> Layers are applied in the following order:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-479-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-479-1" aria-label="Link to rule 479.1">479.1.</a> 1. Copy Effects</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-479-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-479-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 479.1.a">479.1.a.</a> This layer deals with all copy effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-479-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-479-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 479.1.b">479.1.b.</a> When a Copy effect is applied to a Game Object, the traits specified by the Copy Effect replace or are added to the Copyable Traits of the Game Object being Copied as specified by the effect. If no traits are specified by a Copy Effect, all traits of the Game Object are replaced with those of the Copied Game Object.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player triggers Leblanc, Deceiver’s hold effect and plays a Reflection token, making it a copy of Honest Broker. That player then plays Mirror Image, targeting the Reflection token. When the Mirror Image Reflection token is played, it copies all of the copyable traits of the original Reflection token - which are currently those of Honest Broker which it is a copy of. That player will have three units named Honest Broker in play, two of which are token Copies with Temporary.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-479-1-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-479-1-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 479.1.b.1">479.1.b.1.</a> The copyable traits of a Game Object are all of the printed—or if a copy effect is applied, copied—traits of that Game Object. In the case of a Token, they are the traits the token had when it was Created.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: The copyable traits include the printed versions of the following traits: Name, Supertype, Type, Tags, Cost, Domain, Rules Text, Effect Text, Might, Might Bonus, Flavor Text, Art</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-479-1-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-479-1-c" aria-label="Link to rule 479.1.c">479.1.c.</a> Copyable traits of a Game Object are distinct from the actual traits of that Game Object, which may be modified by subsequent applied Layer effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-479-1-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-479-1-d" aria-label="Link to rule 479.1.d">479.1.d.</a> Effects for this layer can be identified by the phrase "copy" in the text.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Svellsongur is an Equipment that reads in part “As this is attached to a unit, copy that unit's text to this Equipment's effect text for as long as this is attached to it.” This is a copy effect that is applied in this layer.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-479-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-479-2" aria-label="Link to rule 479.2">479.2.</a> 2. Trait-Altering Effects</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-479-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-479-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 479.2.a">479.2.a.</a> This layer deals with effects that grant, remove, or replace inherent traits of Game Objects. Name Supertype Type Tags Controller Cost Domain Base Might Base Might Bonus</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-479-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-479-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 479.2.b">479.2.b.</a> Effects for this layer can be identified by the phrase "become(s)", "give," "is," or "are" in the text.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A permanent has the ability "Other friendly units are Yordles." Other friendly units gain the Yordle tag in this layer.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-479-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-479-3" aria-label="Link to rule 479.3">479.3.</a> 3. Ability-Altering Effects</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-479-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-479-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 479.3.a">479.3.a.</a> This layer deals with non-Copy effects that grant, remove, or replace the abilities or rules text of Game Objects. Keywords Passive Abilities Appending rules text Removing rules text Effect Text</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-479-3-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-479-3-b" aria-label="Link to rule 479.3.b">479.3.b.</a> Effects for this layer can be identified by the phrase "become(s)," "give," "lose(s)," "have," "has," "is," or "are" in the text.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A permanent has the ability "Other friendly units have [Vision]." Other friendly units gain the Vision keyword in this layer.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-479-3-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-479-3-c" aria-label="Link to rule 479.3.c">479.3.c.</a> Abilities of Effect Text of Attached cards are appended in this layer.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-479-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-479-4" aria-label="Link to rule 479.4">479.4.</a> 4. Arithmetic Effects</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-479-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-479-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 479.4.a">479.4.a.</a> This layer deals with the mathematics of increasing and decreasing the numeric values of the traits of Game Objects. Might Energy Cost Power Cost</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-479-4-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-479-4-b" aria-label="Link to rule 479.4.b">479.4.b.</a> When an arithmetic effect from a source that is not a passive ability has a limitation that applies, it is limited at the time of its application, and is “remembered” at that limited level for the duration of its effect. This process is called “snapshotting.”</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: If an effect gives a unit “-4 [M] to a min of 1 this turn” choosing a unit with 2 [M], then the effect will generate -1 [M] this turn.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A unit reads “Units you control here have their Might increased to 5 [M].” This is a passive ability, so it will not snapshot.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A spell reads “Increase a friendly unit’s Might to 5 [M].” This effect is applied once, with an unlimited duration. Because it isn’t from a passive ability, it will snapshot.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-479-4-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-479-4-c" aria-label="Link to rule 479.4.c">479.4.c.</a> Players cannot increase a numeric attribute by a negative amount. If an effect would instruct a player to do so, they increase it by 0 instead.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player plays Last Stand, which reads “Double a friendly unit's Might this turn. Give it Temporary.” The player declares a 2 [M] unit as the target during finalization. In reaction to Last Stand, an opponent plays Eclipse targeting the 2 [M] unit. When Last Stand resolves, the unit is -2 [M]. Last Stand instructs its controller to increase the unit’s Might by its current amount, -2, when the double action is performed. This is not possible, so the unit’s Might is increased by 0 instead.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-479-4-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-479-4-d" aria-label="Link to rule 479.4.d">479.4.d.</a> Might Bonuses of Attached cards are applied in this layer.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-479-4-e" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-479-4-e" aria-label="Link to rule 479.4.e">479.4.e.</a> This layer applies arithmetic in the following way.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-479-4-e-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-479-4-e-1" aria-label="Link to rule 479.4.e.1">479.4.e.1.</a> 1. Increases</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-479-4-e-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-479-4-e-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 479.4.e.1.a">479.4.e.1.a.</a> Positive values, or increases, to Might are applied first.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-479-4-e-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-479-4-e-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 479.4.e.1.b">479.4.e.1.b.</a> If there is a restriction or limitation to this increase and it isn’t from a passive ability, the limitation is “snapshotted” for the duration of the effect.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-479-4-e-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-479-4-e-2" aria-label="Link to rule 479.4.e.2">479.4.e.2.</a> 2. Decreases</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-479-4-e-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-479-4-e-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 479.4.e.2.a">479.4.e.2.a.</a> Negative values, or decreases, to Might are applied last.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-479-4-e-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-479-4-e-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 479.4.e.2.b">479.4.e.2.b.</a> If there is a restriction or limitation to this decrease and it isn’t from a passive ability, the limitation is “snapshotted” for the duration of the effect.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-479-4-f" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-479-4-f" aria-label="Link to rule 479.4.f">479.4.f.</a> Effects for this layer can be identified by the phrase "give,” “swap,” "increase," "decrease," "cost … less," "cost … more," "-X,” or “+X,” in the text.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: An effect says “I cost [1] less for each card you've played this turn, to a minimum of [1].” The cost is modified in this layer.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-480" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-480" aria-label="Link to rule 480">480.</a> If more than one effect applies to the same Game Object in the Same Layer, or to each other in the same layer, then both effects will apply but their order may be determined by Dependency.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-480-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-480-1" aria-label="Link to rule 480.1">480.1.</a> A Dependency is established if:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-480-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-480-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 480.1.a">480.1.a.</a> Applying one of the effects alters the existence of the other; or</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-480-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-480-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 480.1.b">480.1.b.</a> Applying one of the effects alters the number of objects the other effect can influence; or</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-480-1-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-480-1-c" aria-label="Link to rule 480.1.c">480.1.c.</a> Applying one of the effects alters the outcome when applying the other.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-481" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-481" aria-label="Link to rule 481">481.</a> To determine which effect Depends on another, determine which of the prior criteria applies, and then also which effect’s evaluation is altered by the sequence of applications. That effect is said to Depend on the other.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A unit with 4 [M] is under the effects of a passive ability that reads “Units you control here have their Might increased to 5 [M].” Its controller plays Discipline on the unit, giving it +2 [M]. When applying Layer alterations, both effects are applied in the same layer. If we apply the passive ability first, the passive ability will give +1 [M] while the Discipline effect will give +2 [M]. If we apply them in the other order, the Discipline effect will give +2 [M], and the passive ability will give +0 [M]. The passive ability is altered by the sequence of applications, so it depends on the Discipline effect.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-481-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-481-1" aria-label="Link to rule 481.1">481.1.</a> If both effects are altered by the application of the other, no Dependency can be established.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A unit with 4 [M] is under the effects of a passive ability that reads “Units you control here have their Might increased to 5 [M].” Its controller plays a spell that reads “Give a unit +2 [M], to a maximum of 5 [M].” If we apply the passive ability first, the passive ability will apply +1 [M] and the spell effect will apply +0 [M]. If we apply the spell effect first, it will apply +1 [M] while the passive ability applies +0 [M]. Both effects are altered by the sequence of applications, so we can’t establish a dependency.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-481-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-481-2" aria-label="Link to rule 481.2">481.2.</a> To resolve a dependency, the effects within the same layer that created the dependency must be applied such that: 1. Identify which effect Depends on the other within the Layer. 2. Apply the effect that is depended on first. 3. Immediately apply the effect that Depends on the first effect next.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A unit with 4[M] is under the effects of a passive ability that reads “Units you control here have their Might increased to 5 [M].” Its controller plays Discipline on the unit. As previously established, the passive ability depends on the Discipline effect. We apply the Discipline effect first, giving the unit +2 [M], and then immediately apply the passive ability that depends on it. The unit’s final Might is 6 [M].</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-482" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-482" aria-label="Link to rule 482">482.</a> If more than one effect applies to the same Game Object in the same layer but no dependency is established, then Timestamp order is applied to the effects within that layer and sublayer</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-482-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-482-1" aria-label="Link to rule 482.1">482.1.</a> When an effect begins applying, it establishes a time for which it is compared against other Game Effects for purposes of resolving Layered effects as its Timestamp.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-482-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-482-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 482.1.a">482.1.a.</a> Timestamps are not rote values.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-482-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-482-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 482.1.b">482.1.b.</a> Timestamps are relative comparisons between effects and when they began applying to the game.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-482-1-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-482-1-c" aria-label="Link to rule 482.1.c">482.1.c.</a> Timestamps are not referenced by Game Effects in any way. They are only used to finalize layered effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-482-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-482-2" aria-label="Link to rule 482.2">482.2.</a> When Rules Text becomes Inactive for any reason, it loses its Timestamp. When it ceases to be Inactive, a new Timestamp is established.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-482-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-482-3" aria-label="Link to rule 482.3">482.3.</a> Effects are applied such that the earliest Timestamp within each Layer and Sublayer applies first, followed by other Effects in that Layer and Sublayer in chronological order.</p>
</div>

### 483. Modes of Play {#rule-483}


<div id="rule-484" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-484" aria-label="Link to rule 484">484.</a> There are multiple methods of playing Riftbound.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-485" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-485" aria-label="Link to rule 485">485.</a> A Mode of Play must define several variables for the game.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-485-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-485-1" aria-label="Link to rule 485.1">485.1.</a> Number of Players: How many people are playing the game.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-485-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-485-2" aria-label="Link to rule 485.2">485.2.</a> Formation of Players: How the players are organized while playing.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-485-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-485-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 485.2.a">485.2.a.</a> This defines if a player is alone or on a team.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-485-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-485-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 485.2.b">485.2.b.</a> This defines the number of opponents.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-485-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-485-3" aria-label="Link to rule 485.3">485.3.</a> Victory Score: The point total a player or team must reach to win.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-485-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-485-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 485.3.a">485.3.a.</a> Victory Scores can be any positive number.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-485-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-485-4" aria-label="Link to rule 485.4">485.4.</a> Battlefield Count: Determines how many Battlefields are in play, contributed by players, during play.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-485-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-485-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 485.4.a">485.4.a.</a> This may influence deck building requirements in competitive settings.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-485-4-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-485-4-b" aria-label="Link to rule 485.4.b">485.4.b.</a> This may involve utilizing less Battlefields than there are players.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-485-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-485-5" aria-label="Link to rule 485.5">485.5.</a> Setup: Any changes to initial setup required for this mode.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-485-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-485-6" aria-label="Link to rule 485.6">485.6.</a> Format: Conditions to win or additional rules added over play.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-485-7" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-485-7" aria-label="Link to rule 485.7">485.7.</a> First Turn Process: Adjustments to each player's first turn.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-485-8" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-485-8" aria-label="Link to rule 485.8">485.8.</a> Unique Rules</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-485-8-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-485-8-a" aria-label="Link to rule 485.8.a">485.8.a.</a> If any, they will be appended here.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-486" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-486" aria-label="Link to rule 486">486.</a> Sanctioned Modes</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-487" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-487" aria-label="Link to rule 487">487.</a> 1v1 (Duel)</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-487-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-487-1" aria-label="Link to rule 487.1">487.1.</a> 2 Players</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-487-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-487-2" aria-label="Link to rule 487.2">487.2.</a> 1v1 1 opponent each No teams</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-487-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-487-3" aria-label="Link to rule 487.3">487.3.</a> Victory Score: 8</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-487-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-487-4" aria-label="Link to rule 487.4">487.4.</a> Battlefield Count: 2</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-487-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-487-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 487.4.a">487.4.a.</a> Each player provides three (3) Battlefields, included in their deck during deck building. Only 1 will be used, chosen during setup.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-487-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-487-5" aria-label="Link to rule 487.5">487.5.</a> Setup: Each player randomly selects one (1) of their three (3) Battlefields. The other two are removed and will not be used for this game. The selected Battlefields are placed simultaneously in the Battlefield Zone.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-487-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-487-6" aria-label="Link to rule 487.6">487.6.</a> Format: Best of 1. The first player to reach the Victory Score in Points wins the Match.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-487-7" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-487-7" aria-label="Link to rule 487.7">487.7.</a> First Turn Process: The player going second channels an extra Rune from their Rune Deck during their first Channel Phase of the game.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-488" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-488" aria-label="Link to rule 488">488.</a> 1v1 (Match)</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-488-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-488-1" aria-label="Link to rule 488.1">488.1.</a> 2 Players</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-488-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-488-2" aria-label="Link to rule 488.2">488.2.</a> 1v1 1 opponent each No teams</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-488-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-488-3" aria-label="Link to rule 488.3">488.3.</a> Victory Score: 8</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-488-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-488-4" aria-label="Link to rule 488.4">488.4.</a> Battlefield Count: 2</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-488-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-488-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 488.4.a">488.4.a.</a> Each player provides three (3) Battlefields, included in their deck during deck building. Only 1 will be used, chosen during setup.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-488-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-488-5" aria-label="Link to rule 488.5">488.5.</a> Setup: Each player selects one (1) of their three (3) Battlefields. The other two are set aside and will not be used for this round of play. The selected Battlefields are placed simultaneously in the Battlefield Zone. After this game, if a player won, the Battlefields that were used are to be removed and not selected again for this Match. One of the remaining Battlefields that were set aside must be chosen instead.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-488-5-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-488-5-a" aria-label="Link to rule 488.5.a">488.5.a.</a> If no player won a game, the battlefields presented for that game may be reused in a subsequent game.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-488-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-488-6" aria-label="Link to rule 488.6">488.6.</a> Format: Best of 3. The first player to reach the Victory Score in Points wins the game. The winner of that game earns One Game Win. Players then reset the game state, remove the Battlefields in play from the game, choose new Battlefields from those set aside, and play again. The first player to earn Two Game Wins wins the match.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-488-6-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-488-6-a" aria-label="Link to rule 488.6.a">488.6.a.</a> This format may be played as best of 5. In such a case, during games 4 and 5 of the match players may present a battlefield that has been removed from the game.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-488-6-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-488-6-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 488.6.a.1">488.6.a.1.</a> Players may only re-use a battlefield in this way if they have already presented each of their battlefields at least once during the match.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-488-6-a-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-488-6-a-2" aria-label="Link to rule 488.6.a.2">488.6.a.2.</a> Players may not present a battlefield more than twice in a match when re-using battlefields in this way.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-488-7" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-488-7" aria-label="Link to rule 488.7">488.7.</a> First Turn Process: The player going second channels an extra Rune from their Rune Deck during their first Channel Phase of the game.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-489" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-489" aria-label="Link to rule 489">489.</a> FFA3 (Skirmish)</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-489-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-489-1" aria-label="Link to rule 489.1">489.1.</a> 3 Players</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-489-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-489-2" aria-label="Link to rule 489.2">489.2.</a> FFA 2 opponents each No teams</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-489-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-489-3" aria-label="Link to rule 489.3">489.3.</a> Victory Score: 8</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-489-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-489-4" aria-label="Link to rule 489.4">489.4.</a> Battlefield Count: 3</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-489-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-489-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 489.4.a">489.4.a.</a> Each player provides three (3) Battlefields, included in their deck during deck building. Only 1 will be used, chosen during setup.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-489-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-489-5" aria-label="Link to rule 489.5">489.5.</a> Setup: Each player randomly selects one (1) of their three (3) Battlefields. The other two are discarded and will not be used for this game. The selected Battlefields are placed simultaneously between the three Players before play and will be used for this game.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-489-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-489-6" aria-label="Link to rule 489.6">489.6.</a> Format: Best of 1. The first player to reach the Victory Score in Points wins the Match.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-489-7" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-489-7" aria-label="Link to rule 489.7">489.7.</a> First Turn Process: The player going first does not draw a card during their first Draw Phase of the game. The player going last channels an extra Rune from their Rune Deck during their first Channel Phase of the game.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-490" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-490" aria-label="Link to rule 490">490.</a> FFA4 (War)</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-490-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-490-1" aria-label="Link to rule 490.1">490.1.</a> 4 Players</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-490-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-490-2" aria-label="Link to rule 490.2">490.2.</a> FFA 3 opponents each No teams</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-490-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-490-3" aria-label="Link to rule 490.3">490.3.</a> Victory Score: 8</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-490-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-490-4" aria-label="Link to rule 490.4">490.4.</a> Battlefield Count: 3</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-490-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-490-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 490.4.a">490.4.a.</a> Each player provides three (3) Battlefields, included in their deck during deck building.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-490-4-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-490-4-b" aria-label="Link to rule 490.4.b">490.4.b.</a> The player who is taking the first turn removes their Battlefields. They will not be used.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-490-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-490-5" aria-label="Link to rule 490.5">490.5.</a> Setup: Each player who is not going first randomly selects one (1) of their three (3) Battlefields. The other two are removed and will not be used for this game. The selected Battlefields are placed simultaneously between the players before play and will be used for this game.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-490-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-490-6" aria-label="Link to rule 490.6">490.6.</a> Format: Best of 1. The first player to reach the Victory Score in Points wins the Match.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-490-7" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-490-7" aria-label="Link to rule 490.7">490.7.</a> First Turn Process: The player going first does not draw a card during their first Draw Phase of the game. The player going last channels an extra Rune from their Rune Deck during their first Channel Phase of the game.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491" aria-label="Link to rule 491">491.</a> 2v2 (Magma Chamber)</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-1" aria-label="Link to rule 491.1">491.1.</a> 4 Players</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-2" aria-label="Link to rule 491.2">491.2.</a> 2v2 2 opponents each 1 teammate</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-3" aria-label="Link to rule 491.3">491.3.</a> Victory Score: 11</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-4" aria-label="Link to rule 491.4">491.4.</a> Battlefield Count: 3</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 491.4.a">491.4.a.</a> Each player provides three (3) Battlefields, included in their deck during deck building.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-5" aria-label="Link to rule 491.5">491.5.</a> Setup:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-5-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-5-a" aria-label="Link to rule 491.5.a">491.5.a.</a> Each player who is not going first randomly selects one (1) of their three (3) Battlefields. The other two are removed and will not be used for this game. The selected Battlefields are placed simultaneously between the players before play and will be used for this game.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-5-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-5-b" aria-label="Link to rule 491.5.b">491.5.b.</a> The player who is taking the first turn removes their Battlefields. They will not be used.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-5-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-5-c" aria-label="Link to rule 491.5.c">491.5.c.</a> Turn order alternates teams.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-5-c-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-5-c-1" aria-label="Link to rule 491.5.c.1">491.5.c.1.</a> The first player will be followed by an opponent, then the first player's teammate, then the first opponent's teammate, and so on.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-5-c-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-5-c-2" aria-label="Link to rule 491.5.c.2">491.5.c.2.</a> If allies are sitting across from each other, turn order proceeds clockwise as normal.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-5-c-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-5-c-3" aria-label="Link to rule 491.5.c.3">491.5.c.3.</a> If allies are sitting next to each other, turn order is passed across the table.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-6" aria-label="Link to rule 491.6">491.6.</a> Format: Best of 1. The first team to reach the Victory Score in Points wins the Match.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-6-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-6-a" aria-label="Link to rule 491.6.a">491.6.a.</a> Teammates win or lose together.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-6-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-6-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 491.6.a.1">491.6.a.1.</a> If one player Concedes, then the entire team loses.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-6-a-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-6-a-2" aria-label="Link to rule 491.6.a.2">491.6.a.2.</a> If one player Loses, then the entire team loses.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-6-a-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-6-a-3" aria-label="Link to rule 491.6.a.3">491.6.a.3.</a> If one player Wins, then the entire team wins.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-7" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-7" aria-label="Link to rule 491.7">491.7.</a> First Turn Process: The player going first does not draw a card during their first Draw Phase of the game. The player going last channels an extra Rune from their Rune Deck during their first Channel Phase of the game.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-8" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-8" aria-label="Link to rule 491.8">491.8.</a> Unique Rules</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-8-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-8-a" aria-label="Link to rule 491.8.a">491.8.a.</a> Players may play spells or activate abilities during their Teammate's Turn. In order to do so, their Teammate will invite them to play a spell or activate an ability using their own Priority.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-8-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-8-b" aria-label="Link to rule 491.8.b">491.8.b.</a> A Battlefield can only be Scored, via Conquering or Holding, once per team per turn.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-8-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-8-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 491.8.b.1">491.8.b.1.</a> Battlefields controlled during the Scoring Step in the Beginning Phase of a player's turn by that player's teammate are disqualified from being scored by that Team, that turn.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A player may not induce their partner to retreat, and then conquer a Battlefield their teammate was controlling.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-8-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-8-c" aria-label="Link to rule 491.8.c">491.8.c.</a> Control is not shared.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-8-c-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-8-c-1" aria-label="Link to rule 491.8.c.1">491.8.c.1.</a> Players may not Hide cards at Battlefields controlled by their Teammate.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-8-c-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-8-c-2" aria-label="Link to rule 491.8.c.2">491.8.c.2.</a> Players may not issue Standard Movement to their Teammate's Units.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-8-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-8-d" aria-label="Link to rule 491.8.d">491.8.d.</a> Points are shared by a team.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-8-d-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-8-d-1" aria-label="Link to rule 491.8.d.1">491.8.d.1.</a> Both players on a team have the same number of Victory Points.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-8-d-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-8-d-2" aria-label="Link to rule 491.8.d.2">491.8.d.2.</a> If a player gains or loses points, their team gains or loses those points.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-8-d-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-8-d-3" aria-label="Link to rule 491.8.d.3">491.8.d.3.</a> If a spell or ability references or checks a player gaining or losing points, that ability checks whether that player’s team gains or loses points.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-8-e" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-8-e" aria-label="Link to rule 491.8.e">491.8.e.</a> Friendly describes controlled Game Objects by a player or their Teammate.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: "When you play me, ready a friendly unit" could target a player's own Units or their teammate's Units.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-8-f" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-8-f" aria-label="Link to rule 491.8.f">491.8.f.</a> Hands are Private Information. Players may show their Private Information to one or more players at any time during play, including their teammate.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-8-f-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-8-f-1" aria-label="Link to rule 491.8.f.1">491.8.f.1.</a> Revealing Cards is still a game action and cannot be done unless instructed.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-425">425</a>. Reveal for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-8-g" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-8-g" aria-label="Link to rule 491.8.g">491.8.g.</a> The Final Point has an adjustment to the criteria when scoring.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-8-g-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-8-g-1" aria-label="Link to rule 491.8.g.1">491.8.g.1.</a> When scoring the Final Point from Conquer during their or a teammate’s turn, a player must Score each Battlefield in the same turn, excepting any that were occupied by their ally during the scoring step of that turn’s Beginning Phase.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-469">469</a>. Scoring for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-8-g-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-8-g-2" aria-label="Link to rule 491.8.g.2">491.8.g.2.</a> No other changes to Final Point restrictions.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-8-h" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-8-h" aria-label="Link to rule 491.8.h">491.8.h.</a> Teammates may not utilize the same Champion Legend.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-491-8-i" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-491-8-i" aria-label="Link to rule 491.8.i">491.8.i.</a> Teammates may not utilize the same Battlefields.</p>
</div>

### 649. Conceding {#rule-649}


<div id="rule-650" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-650" aria-label="Link to rule 650">650.</a> A player may concede at any time.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-651" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-651" aria-label="Link to rule 651">651.</a> When a player concedes, they are removed from the game in progress.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-651-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-651-1" aria-label="Link to rule 651.1">651.1.</a> If only one other player is remaining after a player has conceded, the player remaining Wins.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-651-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-651-2" aria-label="Link to rule 651.2">651.2.</a> If more than one player remains after a concession, follow the steps for the Removal of a Player.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-651-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-651-3" aria-label="Link to rule 651.3">651.3.</a> Removal of a player involves them no longer being able to make choices or otherwise influence the game.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-651-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-651-4" aria-label="Link to rule 651.4">651.4.</a> If the player has Teammates due to the Mode of Play, that player’s Teammates also lose and are removed from the game.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-652" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-652" aria-label="Link to rule 652">652.</a> If the game continues, follow these steps for Removal of a Player.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-652-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-652-1" aria-label="Link to rule 652.1">652.1.</a> Banish all permanents, runes, and facedown cards they currently control and all permanents, runes, and facedown cards they own.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-652-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-652-2" aria-label="Link to rule 652.2">652.2.</a> Remove the Battlefield they contributed to the game if it is in use.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-652-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-652-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 652.2.a">652.2.a.</a> If it was in use, Replace it with a token battlefield with no abilities.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-652-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-652-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 652.2.b">652.2.b.</a> Any units or hidden cards there do not move and are otherwise unaffected by this process.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-439">439</a>. Replace for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-652-2-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-652-2-c" aria-label="Link to rule 652.2.c">652.2.c.</a> If the removed battlefield was applying any continuous effects, those continuous effects immediately cease, which may cause changes in the characteristics of units or hidden cards there.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A battlefield reads "Units here have +1 [M]." If that battlefield's owner concedes and the battlefield is removed from the game as a result, units there immediately cease to get +1 [M].</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-652-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-652-3" aria-label="Link to rule 652.3">652.3.</a> Remove all cards they own from the game.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-652-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-652-4" aria-label="Link to rule 652.4">652.4.</a> Counter all spells and abilities of all types controlled by the player that has conceded.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-652-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-652-5" aria-label="Link to rule 652.5">652.5.</a> Proceed with the game.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-652-5-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-652-5-a" aria-label="Link to rule 652.5.a">652.5.a.</a> Turn</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-652-5-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-652-5-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 652.5.a.1">652.5.a.1.</a> If the removed player was the Turn Player, play proceeds in Turn Order to the next available player in order.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-652-5-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-652-5-b" aria-label="Link to rule 652.5.b">652.5.b.</a> Focus</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-652-5-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-652-5-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 652.5.b.1">652.5.b.1.</a> If the removed player had Focus in a Showdown, the next Player in order receives Focus.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-652-5-b-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-652-5-b-2" aria-label="Link to rule 652.5.b.2">652.5.b.2.</a> If the player being removed creates a state where all Players have Passed their Focus, the Showdown ends and play proceeds as necessary, E.G. Combat is resolved or a Cleanup is completed.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-652-5-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-652-5-c" aria-label="Link to rule 652.5.c">652.5.c.</a> Priority</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-652-5-c-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-652-5-c-1" aria-label="Link to rule 652.5.c.1">652.5.c.1.</a> If the removed player had Priority during a Chain, the next Player in order receives Priority.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-652-5-c-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-652-5-c-2" aria-label="Link to rule 652.5.c.2">652.5.c.2.</a> If the player being removed creates a state where all Players have passed Priority, then the most recent spell or ability on the Chain will resolve with Priority being re-established as appropriate for the state after the resolution of that spell or ability.</p>
</div>

## 700. Additional Rules {#rule-700}


### 701. Buffs {#rule-701}


<div id="rule-702" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-702" aria-label="Link to rule 702">702.</a> Buffs are counters placed on Units.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-702-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-702-1" aria-label="Link to rule 702.1">702.1.</a> Buffs can be tracked with a buff reminder card from a Riftbound booster pack or with any spare object in your surroundings.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-702-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-702-2" aria-label="Link to rule 702.2">702.2.</a> Buffs can be added or spent.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-702-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-702-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 702.2.a">702.2.a.</a> To Buff a Unit, a player chooses a Unit and then places a buff on it. That Unit is Buffed for as long as the buff remains on it.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-702-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-702-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 702.2.b">702.2.b.</a> Spending a Buff removes a single Buff counter from a Unit.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-702-2-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-702-2-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 702.2.b.1">702.2.b.1.</a> A buff cannot be spent from a Unit that does not have a buff.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-702-2-b-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-702-2-b-2" aria-label="Link to rule 702.2.b.2">702.2.b.2.</a> A player can only spend buffs on units they control.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-702-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-702-3" aria-label="Link to rule 702.3">702.3.</a> There can only be one Buff on a Unit at a time.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-702-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-702-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 702.3.a">702.3.a.</a> If a Buff is added, or instructed to be added, on a Unit that already has a Buff, it is not placed instead.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-703" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-703" aria-label="Link to rule 703">703.</a> Each Buff individually contributes +1 Might to a Unit.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-704" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-704" aria-label="Link to rule 704">704.</a> Buffs are Game Objects and may be referenced, counted, or affected by other effects as specified.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-704-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-704-1" aria-label="Link to rule 704.1">704.1.</a> Buffs are counters, and thus are not targeted by spells and abilities.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-741">741</a>. Counters for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-705" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-705" aria-label="Link to rule 705">705.</a> If a Unit leaves play, remove all Buffs from it.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-705-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-705-1" aria-label="Link to rule 705.1">705.1.</a> Champions do not retain Buffs in the Champion Zone, even if they return there somehow.</p>
</div>

### 706. Mighty {#rule-706}


<div id="rule-707" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-707" aria-label="Link to rule 707">707.</a> Mighty is a description that applies to some units. Other game effects can check whether a unit is Mighty.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-708" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-708" aria-label="Link to rule 708">708.</a> A Unit "is Mighty" as long as its Might is 5 or greater.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-709" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-709" aria-label="Link to rule 709">709.</a> A Unit "becomes Mighty" at the moment its Might changes from being less than 5 to being 5 or greater.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A Unit with Might 4 that gets +1 [M] becomes Mighty.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A Unit with Might 5 that gets +1 [M] does not become Mighty, because it was already Mighty.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-710" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-710" aria-label="Link to rule 710">710.</a> Units on the board are evaluated according to their current Might.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A unit with a base Might of 3 is targeted by a spell that reads "A unit gets +3 [M] this turn." As that spell resolves, its Might changes from 3 to 6, and it becomes Mighty. When that effect expires at the end of the turn, it will no longer be Mighty.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-711" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-711" aria-label="Link to rule 711">711.</a> Units in Non-Board Zones are evaluated according to their printed Might.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A unit in the trash is Mighty if its printed Might is 5 or greater. It doesn't matter if there were effects raising or lowering its might while it was on the board.</p></blockquote>
</div>

### 712. Bonus Damage {#rule-712}


<div id="rule-713" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-713" aria-label="Link to rule 713">713.</a> Bonus Damage is an intrinsic property that can be granted to Deal actions that influence the amount of Damage that the action is distributing.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-714" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-714" aria-label="Link to rule 714">714.</a> If more than one instance of Bonus Damage is applied or granted to a Deal action, all instances are summed and applied once.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-714-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-714-1" aria-label="Link to rule 714.1">714.1.</a> Bonus Damage can only be a positive value, and can only increase the amount of Damage being distributed.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-714-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
