<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-714-2" aria-label="Link to rule 714.2">714.2.</a> If, for any reason, Bonus Damage would be a negative number, then no Bonus Damage is applied to the action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-715" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-715" aria-label="Link to rule 715">715.</a> Bonus Damage applies to the total damage Dealt by one instance of the action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-715-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-715-1" aria-label="Link to rule 715.1">715.1.</a> If the Deal action has a single target, the amount of Damage to that target will be increased by the Bonus Damage granted to it.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-715-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-715-2" aria-label="Link to rule 715.2">715.2.</a> If the Deal action has multiple targets, the amount of Damage dealt to each target is increased by Bonus Damage individually and separately.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Singularity is a spell that says “Deal 6 to each of up to two units.” A player plays Singularity while they also control Annie, Fiery, a unit that says “Your spells and abilities deal 1 Bonus Damage.” Singularity deals 1 Bonus Damage to both of its targets, dealing 7 to each.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-715-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-715-3" aria-label="Link to rule 715.3">715.3.</a> If the Deal action Splits damage, then the Bonus Damage applies to the amount of Damage that will be Split. This can alter the number of targets eligible to be chosen.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Volibear, Furious is a unit that says in part “When I attack, deal 5 damage split among any number of enemy units here.” A player attacks with Volibear, Furious while they also control Annie, Fiery, a unit that says “Your spells and abilities deal 1 Bonus Damage.” Volibear, Furious now deals 6 damage split among any number of enemy units at its location, and can choose to split that damage among up to 6 units rather than the usual 5.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-715-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-715-4" aria-label="Link to rule 715.4">715.4.</a> If no damage was Dealt, then Bonus Damage will not apply.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Teemo, Strategist is a unit that reads in part “When I defend, choose an enemy unit here and reveal the top 5 cards of your Main Deck. Deal 1 to that unit for each card with Hidden revealed this way, then recycle the revealed cards.” He has Rabadon’s Deathcrown attached to him. An enemy unit moves to the battlefield where Teemo is located and a combat opens there. Teemo’s controller reveals the top 5 cards of their Main Deck and reveals no cards with Hidden. Although the ability has 3 Bonus Damage from Rabadon’s Deathcrown, no deal action is performed for the Bonus Damage to apply to.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-715-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-715-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 715.4.a">715.4.a.</a> If Damage is replaced or reduced by any means, the replacing or reducing action will include the Bonus Damage in the total damage when determining how much damage is to be dealt.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A unit has prevent 3 applied to them. The unit is located at the Void Gate battlefield. An opponent targets the unit with Hextech Ray. Hextech Ray deals 4 damage to the unit, including the Bonus Damage from Void Gate, which the prevent effect will prevent 3 of. The unit takes 1 damage.</p></blockquote>
</div>

### 716. Attachment {#rule-716}


<div id="rule-717" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-717" aria-label="Link to rule 717">717.</a> Attaching is a limited action that causes cards to become linked to each other to combine their effects in some way. This causes one card to become Attached and the other to become A Top-Most Card.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-435">435</a>. Attach for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-718" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-718" aria-label="Link to rule 718">718.</a> Attached is the state of a card being linked to another card in this way.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-718-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-718-1" aria-label="Link to rule 718.1">718.1.</a> A card remains in this state until Detached.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-718-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-718-2" aria-label="Link to rule 718.2">718.2.</a> While in this state, the card’s printed Rules Text is Inactive.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-720">720</a>. Inactive for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-718-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-718-3" aria-label="Link to rule 718.3">718.3.</a> While in this state, Abilities in the card’s Effect Text are appended to the Rules Text of the Top- Most Card.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-718-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-718-4" aria-label="Link to rule 718.4">718.4.</a> While in this state, the card’s Might Bonus modulates the Top-Most Card’s Might by the value listed.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-718-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-718-5" aria-label="Link to rule 718.5">718.5.</a> Attached cards still have all properties of being a card on the board while in this state.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-718-5-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-718-5-a" aria-label="Link to rule 718.5.a">718.5.a.</a> Attached cards still have all Types and Tags while Attached.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-718-5-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-718-5-b" aria-label="Link to rule 718.5.b">718.5.b.</a> Attached cards still can be chosen or targeted by game effects while Attached.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-718-5-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-718-5-c" aria-label="Link to rule 718.5.c">718.5.c.</a> Attached cards cannot be moved separately from the Top-Most Card they are Attached to.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-718-5-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-718-5-d" aria-label="Link to rule 718.5.d">718.5.d.</a> A card may be Attached only to a single Top-Most card at a time.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-718-5-e" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-718-5-e" aria-label="Link to rule 718.5.e">718.5.e.</a> Attached cards may have different Controllers from their Top-Most card.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-718-5-f" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-718-5-f" aria-label="Link to rule 718.5.f">718.5.f.</a> Changes in Control of the Top-Most card do not impact Control of Attached cards and vice versa.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-718-5-g" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-718-5-g" aria-label="Link to rule 718.5.g">718.5.g.</a> An Attached card still appends the abilities in its Effect Text to the Rules Text of the Top- Most card and modulates the Top-Most Card’s Might by its Might Bonus.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-718-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-718-6" aria-label="Link to rule 718.6">718.6.</a> Only non-Unit Game Objects may be Attached.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-718-6-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-718-6-a" aria-label="Link to rule 718.6.a">718.6.a.</a> If a non-Unit Game Object that is Attached becomes a Unit through any means, it detaches as it becomes a Unit.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-719" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-719" aria-label="Link to rule 719">719.</a> A Top-Most Card is a card that has one or more cards linked to it through the process of Attaching.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-719-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-719-1" aria-label="Link to rule 719.1">719.1.</a> The Effect Text of all cards Attached to this card are appended to the Rules Text of this card for as long as they remain Attached.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-719-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-719-2" aria-label="Link to rule 719.2">719.2.</a> This card ceases being a Top-Most Card when there are no longer any cards Attached to it.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-719-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-719-3" aria-label="Link to rule 719.3">719.3.</a> A Top-Most Card and all cards Attached to it are at the same location.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-719-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-719-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 719.3.a">719.3.a.</a> When the Top-Most Card changes locations, all Attached cards change locations with it.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-719-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-719-4" aria-label="Link to rule 719.4">719.4.</a> The Exhausted and Ready state of the Top-Most card does not affect nor change the status of the Attached cards and vice versa.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-719-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-719-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 719.4.a">719.4.a.</a> This is true of all statuses aside from location, Attached, and Top-Most.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: If the top-most card becomes stunned, it does not affect the state of any attached cards.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: If an attached card becomes empowered, it does not affect the state of its top-most card.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-719-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-719-5" aria-label="Link to rule 719.5">719.5.</a> When a Top-Most Card changes zones from a board zone to a non-board zone, all Attached cards Detach from it, remaining in their current zones.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-719-5-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-719-5-a" aria-label="Link to rule 719.5.a">719.5.a.</a> The player that controls the Top-Most Card that changed zones decides the order these cards Detach in, and thus the order of any relevant effects that occur due to the Detach occurring.</p>
</div>

### 720. Inactive {#rule-720}


<div id="rule-721" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-721" aria-label="Link to rule 721">721.</a> Card text can occasionally be assigned to be ignored, disregarded, or otherwise rendered as not applicable during the course of play. This state is referred to as Inactive.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-721-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-721-1" aria-label="Link to rule 721.1">721.1.</a> Text marked this way is not applied at all while in this state.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-721-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-721-2" aria-label="Link to rule 721.2">721.2.</a> Inactive Abilities do not trigger, do not apply, and cannot be activated. Inactive instructions are not processed.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-722" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-722" aria-label="Link to rule 722">722.</a> Inactive text is still present on cards.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-722-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-722-1" aria-label="Link to rule 722.1">722.1.</a> Cards with Inactive text still have keywords for the sake of Game Effects that want to reference or see if a card has a keyword.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-722-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-722-2" aria-label="Link to rule 722.2">722.2.</a> Game Effects that parse or interpret text to determine target eligibility may still parse Inactive text for the sake of eligibility.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Spinning Axe is a gear with [Temporary]. While it’s attached and its rules text is inactive, its [Temporary] ability doesn’t trigger. However, a spell that reads “Destroy a gear with [Temporary]” could still choose and destroy Spinning Axe.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-723" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-723" aria-label="Link to rule 723">723.</a> Rules Text is never Inactive by default.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-724" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-724" aria-label="Link to rule 724">724.</a> Effect Text is Inactive unless the card with the Effect Text is Attached.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-725" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-725" aria-label="Link to rule 725">725.</a> Inactive text can partially cease to be Inactive under specific circumstances and exceptions.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-725-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-725-1" aria-label="Link to rule 725.1">725.1.</a> If an Attached card has a Passive or Replacement ability that applies during the process of Attaching or a Triggered ability that triggers off of Attaching, that text exists and can be processed as it Attaches.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-725-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-725-2" aria-label="Link to rule 725.2">725.2.</a> If an Attached card has a Passive or Replacement ability that applies during the process of Detaching or a Triggered ability that triggers off of Detaching, that text exists and can be processed as it Detaches.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-725-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-725-3" aria-label="Link to rule 725.3">725.3.</a> If an Attached card has an Equip ability, the Weaponmaster keyword can reference that Equip ability and any abilities that passively modify that Equip ability.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-725-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-725-4" aria-label="Link to rule 725.4">725.4.</a> If a Dependent Ability is a Triggered Ability whose condition occurs at the same time as the Dependent Keyword’s condition being fulfilled, that text exists and can be processed as it is fulfilled.</p>
</div>

### 726. Dependent Keywords {#rule-726}


<div id="rule-727" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-727" aria-label="Link to rule 727">727.</a> Keywords can be Dependent Keywords</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-727-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-727-1" aria-label="Link to rule 727.1">727.1.</a> A Dependent Keyword is comprised of both a Condition that it is short for, and an ability of some format immediately after the Keyword itself.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Noxus Hopeful has “[Legion][&gt;] I cost [2] less.” [Legion] is short for the condition “if you have played another card this turn, this card gains [Text],” while “I cost [2] less” is the dependent ability.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-727-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-727-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 727.1.a">727.1.a.</a> A Dependent Keyword will always be functionally short for a Condition.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-727-1-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-727-1-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 727.1.a.1">727.1.a.1.</a> This Condition may also have a determined Duration, Time, or Limitation as part of its definition.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Legion specifies the duration of its condition: “if you have played another card this turn.” This turn is the duration.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A dependent keyword that is short for “Until you have spent [3] this turn” would include a limitation on its condition.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A dependent keyword that is short for “After you’ve attacked this turn” would include a time after which the condition is active.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-727-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-727-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 727.1.b">727.1.b.</a> The Dependent Ability associated with the Dependent Keyword is Inactive on the card with the Dependent Keyword until the Condition is met, when it becomes Active</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-727-1-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-727-1-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 727.1.b.1">727.1.b.1.</a> The Dependent Ability is present on the card for the sake of reference or evaluation until the Condition is met</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-720">720</a>. Inactive for more information</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-727-1-b-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-727-1-b-2" aria-label="Link to rule 727.1.b.2">727.1.b.2.</a> The Dependent Ability is Active exactly as written while the Condition is true</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Gustwalker has “[Level 3][&gt;] I have +1 [M] and Ganking.” As long as its controller has 3 XP, Gustwalker’s Ganking is active.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-727-1-b-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-727-1-b-3" aria-label="Link to rule 727.1.b.3">727.1.b.3.</a> If an ability has multiple Dependent Keywords, all of them must have their Condition met in order for the ability to be active.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A unit reads “[Level 11][&gt;&gt;][Legion][&gt;] When you conquer, gain 1 point.” In order for the conquer effect of the unit to be active, its controller must have 11 XP and have finalized a card other than the unit that turn.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-727-1-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-727-1-c" aria-label="Link to rule 727.1.c">727.1.c.</a> The Dependent Abilities of Dependent Keywords can be of any type</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-727-1-c-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-727-1-c-1" aria-label="Link to rule 727.1.c.1">727.1.c.1.</a> Triggered Abilities of Dependent Keywords must be Active for their trigger to be evaluated.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-727-1-c-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-727-1-c-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 727.1.c.1.a">727.1.c.1.a.</a> If a Triggered Ability becomes active at the same time as its trigger condition would be fulfilled, it triggers.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-727-1-c-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-727-1-c-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 727.1.c.1.b">727.1.c.1.b.</a> If the condition for the Dependent Keyword causes the Triggered Ability to become Inactive after it has been added to the chain as a Pending Item, that chain item will not be affected and will proceed with being played as normal.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-727-1-c-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-727-1-c-2" aria-label="Link to rule 727.1.c.2">727.1.c.2.</a> Passive Abilities begin applying at the same time the Dependent Keyword becomes true.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-727-1-c-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-727-1-c-3" aria-label="Link to rule 727.1.c.3">727.1.c.3.</a> Activated Abilities that become Active from Dependent Keywords can be activated at their associated timing after that ability has been granted</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-727-1-c-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-727-1-c-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 727.1.c.3.a">727.1.c.3.a.</a> If the condition for the Dependent Keyword causes the Activated Ability to become Inactive after it has been added to the chain as a Pending Item, that chain item will not be affected and will proceed with being played as normal.</p>
</div>

### 728. XP {#rule-728}


<div id="rule-729" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-729" aria-label="Link to rule 729">729.</a> XP is a resource that is accrued, spent, or otherwise modified by Players through the course of play.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-729-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-729-1" aria-label="Link to rule 729.1">729.1.</a> The amount of XP that a player has should be marked clearly.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-729-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-729-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 729.1.a">729.1.a.</a> XP is a value that can be tracked by counters, dice, cards, or any method that clearly displays the specific value of XP that is currently on a Player.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-729-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-729-2" aria-label="Link to rule 729.2">729.2.</a> The amount of XP a Player has is Public Information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-730" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-730" aria-label="Link to rule 730">730.</a> XP can be Gained and Spent.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-730-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-730-1" aria-label="Link to rule 730.1">730.1.</a> To Gain XP, increase the value of XP marked on the Player gaining it.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-730-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-730-2" aria-label="Link to rule 730.2">730.2.</a> To Spend XP, reduce the value of XP marked on the Player spending it.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-731" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-731" aria-label="Link to rule 731">731.</a> XP is not a Game Object.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-731-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-731-1" aria-label="Link to rule 731.1">731.1.</a> XP cannot be targeted, readied, or exhausted.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-732" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-732" aria-label="Link to rule 732">732.</a> XP is not shared between Allies in Game Modes with Teammates.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-733" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-733" aria-label="Link to rule 733">733.</a> There is no limit to an amount of XP a player can accrue.</p>
</div>

### 734. Additional Turns {#rule-734}


<div id="rule-735" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-735" aria-label="Link to rule 735">735.</a> Certain Game Effects will instruct a player to “take a turn after this.” These effects create a temporary Additional Turn owned by that player that is inserted into the turn queue after the current turn.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-736" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-736" aria-label="Link to rule 736">736.</a> Turn order is established when the game begins as a repeating set of players. This populates a looping queue of turns that each player will take, starting with the first turn taken by the First Player, and repeating indefinitely.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-737" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-737" aria-label="Link to rule 737">737.</a> When an Additional Turn is inserted into this queue, it does not change the Turn Order of the game. The owner of the Additional Turn just has the next queued turn. After that turn is completed, it will be removed and the queue will proceed with its previously queued turns.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-738" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-738" aria-label="Link to rule 738">738.</a> If multiple Additional Turns are queued, they are added to the queue in the order the Game Effects that generated them occurred.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: The First Player plays, through some means, two Time Warps during their turn. The Time Warps create two Additional Turns for their controller and insert them into the turn queue after the current turn. If the turn queue is represented as [&gt; A &gt; B &gt; C &gt; D &gt;], then these Additional Turns will appear as [&gt; A &gt; A* &gt; A* &gt; B &gt; C &gt; D &gt;]. After the last Additional Turn is played, the queue returns to its previously queued turns. The “*” denotes that a turn is an Additional Turn.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: The First Player plays Promising Future during their turn, during the resolution of which the Second and Fourth Player choose, banish, and play one Time Warp each. The Fourth Player’s Time Warp resolves first, inserting an Additional Turn for them in the queue as such: [&gt; A &gt; D* &gt; B &gt; C &gt; D &gt;]. The Second Player’s Time Warp resolves afterwards, inserting that turn: [&gt; A &gt; B* &gt; D* &gt; B &gt; C &gt; D &gt;]. When the First Player passes the turn, the Second Player will take their turn, followed by the Fourth Player, after which the queue returns to its previously queued turns.</p></blockquote>
</div>

### 739. Special Terms {#rule-739}


<div id="rule-740" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-740" aria-label="Link to rule 740">740.</a> Card text and this rules document use certain terms in specific ways that are different from their common usage.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-740-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-740-1" aria-label="Link to rule 740.1">740.1.</a> Some card text refers to Game Objects in particular ways:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-740-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-740-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 740.1.a">740.1.a.</a> Two Game Objects are friendly if they share a controller, or if one’s controller is teammates with the other’s.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-740-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-740-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 740.1.b">740.1.b.</a> Two Game Objects are enemies if one’s controller is an opponent of the other’s.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-740-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-740-2" aria-label="Link to rule 740.2">740.2.</a> Some card text refers to Units in particular ways:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-740-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-740-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 740.2.a">740.2.a.</a> A unit is alone when there are no other friendly units at the same location.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-740-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-740-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 740.2.b">740.2.b.</a> A unit is one on one when it and the enemy unit at the same location are both alone.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-740-2-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-740-2-c" aria-label="Link to rule 740.2.c">740.2.c.</a> A unit is in combat if it is occupying a battlefield where combat is ongoing and has a combat designation.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-740-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-740-3" aria-label="Link to rule 740.3">740.3.</a> Some card text refers to specific terminology:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-740-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-740-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 740.3.a">740.3.a.</a> A tie occurs when there are units controlled by different players located at a battlefield where combat is taking place during the step 3d of the combat cleanup.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-740-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-740-4" aria-label="Link to rule 740.4">740.4.</a> Some terms in this document are used in particular ways:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-740-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-740-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 740.4.a">740.4.a.</a> Costs within instructions are instructions that take a particular form (“[do X] to [do Y]”). [Do X] is the cost associated with the instruction, and [do Y] is the effect.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-740-4-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-740-4-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 740.4.a.1">740.4.a.1.</a> For spells, costs within instructions are paid on resolution of the spell. In order to get the effect, the cost must be paid.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-740-4-a-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-740-4-a-2" aria-label="Link to rule 740.4.a.2">740.4.a.2.</a> For triggered abilities, costs within instructions that appear in the first part of the trigger effect are paid on finalization of the triggered ability. In order to place the triggered ability on the chain, the cost must be paid.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-740-4-a-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-740-4-a-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 740.4.a.2.a">740.4.a.2.a.</a> Costs within instructions that appear in any later part of the trigger effect are paid on resolution.</p>
</div>

### 741. Counters {#rule-741}


<div id="rule-742" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-742" aria-label="Link to rule 742">742.</a> Counters are Game Objects generated by and given to other Game Objects on the board during play.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-742-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-742-1" aria-label="Link to rule 742.1">742.1.</a> Unlike other Game Objects, Counters are not targeted by spells and abilities. If an effect instructs a player to interact with a Counter, the Counter is chosen when the corresponding instruction executes.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-743" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-743" aria-label="Link to rule 743">743.</a> Counters serve to track semi-permanent effects on Game Objects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-744" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-744" aria-label="Link to rule 744">744.</a> Counters can have game effects themselves, or serve as the prerequisite for game effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-745" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-745" aria-label="Link to rule 745">745.</a> Counters can be spent by game effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-745-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-745-1" aria-label="Link to rule 745.1">745.1.</a> In order to do so, that player must remove that many counters of the specified type from the specified Game Objects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-745-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-745-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 745.1.a">745.1.a.</a> If no type of counter is specified, any counter can be removed this way.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-745-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-745-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 745.1.b">745.1.b.</a> If no type of Game Object is specified, then counters can be removed from any Game Objects with the specified counter.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-745-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-745-2" aria-label="Link to rule 745.2">745.2.</a> In order to spend a Counter, the spending player must control the Game Object the Counter is placed on.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-746" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-746" aria-label="Link to rule 746">746.</a> Some effects may move a Counter between two Game Objects. The Counter is either on the first Game Object or the second. There is no state for a Counter being between either object.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-747" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-747" aria-label="Link to rule 747">747.</a> Counters that leave a Game Object without being placed on another game object cease to exist.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-748" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-748" aria-label="Link to rule 748">748.</a> Game Objects that change zones to a non-board zone lose all of their Counters.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-124">124</a>. for more information on temporary modifications.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-749" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-749" aria-label="Link to rule 749">749.</a> Counters do not have a controller.</p>
</div>

### 750. Making New Choices {#rule-750}


<div id="rule-751" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-751" aria-label="Link to rule 751">751.</a> Some Game Effects will instruct a player to make new choices for a finalized item on the chain.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-752" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-752" aria-label="Link to rule 752">752.</a> This refers to the choices normally made during finalization, with some exclusions.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-355">355</a>. Make Relevant Choices for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-752-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-752-1" aria-label="Link to rule 752.1">752.1.</a> The relevant choices that can be remade for these Game Effects are locations to be played to, modes, destinations, and targets.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-752-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-752-2" aria-label="Link to rule 752.2">752.2.</a> This does not refer to any choices made “as you play this” or “as I am played,” or any choices made for Optional Additional Costs.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-753" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-753" aria-label="Link to rule 753">753.</a> When making new choices in this way, a player must choose to remake all choices made for a chain item, even if they choose to make the same choice that was previously made.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-753-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-753-1" aria-label="Link to rule 753.1">753.1.</a> A player may not make choices this way that would be illegal or that would lead to an illegal state, even if there are no other options.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-753-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-753-2" aria-label="Link to rule 753.2">753.2.</a> A player may not choose to make new choices for a chain item if there aren’t legal choices that they could make in this way. In such a case, the instruction to make new choices is negated.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-753-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-753-3" aria-label="Link to rule 753.3">753.3.</a> Restrictions and requirements applied to a chain item will also apply to new choices made for that chain item in this way.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: If a card is played from facedown, it gains the restriction that it can only target game objects at the battlefield it was played from unless its targeting is restricted in such a way that choosing a unit at that battlefield is impossible. If a player makes new choices for such a card, they must still choose from among objects at that battlefield.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-754" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-754" aria-label="Link to rule 754">754.</a> If, as a result of new choices, the chain item targets a Game Object, even if it previously targeted the same Game Object, any Targeting Effects of that Game Object will trigger as appropriate.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-755" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-755" aria-label="Link to rule 755">755.</a> Any costs “to play” the chain item that are incurred as a result of new choices made in this way are ignored.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-755-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-755-1" aria-label="Link to rule 755.1">755.1.</a> The chain item is already played and its costs paid. Any further costs incurred have no effect on the finalized chain item.</p>
</div>

### 756. Untargetability {#rule-756}


<div id="rule-757" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-757" aria-label="Link to rule 757">757.</a> Some Game Effects declare that a Game Object can’t be chosen by a certain set of spells and abilities. Those effects designate the Game Object as Untargetable.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-757-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-757-1" aria-label="Link to rule 757.1">757.1.</a> These passive abilities will usually take the form “[Game Object] can’t be chosen by [Category] spells and abilities.”</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Ruin Runner’s passive ability reads “I can’t be chosen by enemy spells and abilities.”</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-758" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-758" aria-label="Link to rule 758">758.</a> Untargetable Game Objects are not legal targets for the indicated spells and abilities.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-355-6">355.6</a>. Targeting for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-758-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-758-1" aria-label="Link to rule 758.1">758.1.</a> If a Game Object becomes untargetable for a spell or ability after becoming its target and before it resolves, the spell or ability will mistarget on resolution. Any instructions related to that Game Object will be ignored as the spell resolves.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-758-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-758-2" aria-label="Link to rule 758.2">758.2.</a> If the spell or ability changes for any reason such that it will no longer fall under the category of spells or abilities for which a Game Object is untargetable, the Game Object will become a legal target for that spell or ability.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A 1 [M] unit reads “I can’t be chosen by enemy spells or abilities with Energy cost less than my Might.” That unit is targeted by an enemy Falling Star; in reaction to the Falling Star, the unit’s controller plays Discipline targeting that unit, bringing its Might to 3 [M]. If Falling Star resolved at that point, the unit would be unaffected by it as it resolves. Falling Star’s controller plays Eclipse before Falling Star resolves, bringing the unit’s Might to -1 [M]. When Falling Star resolves now, the unit is affected by the spell and has 6 damage marked. It dies in the following cleanup.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-758-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-758-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 758.2.a">758.2.a.</a> This will also occur if the Game Object is no longer among the set of objects that are made untargetable by a Game Effect.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Alpha Wildclaw and Vilemaw are controlled by the same player and located at a battlefield. An opponent targets Vilemaw with Rebuke. Before Rebuke resolves, Vilemaw’s controller plays Discipline targeting Alpha Wildclaw, making Vilemaw no longer a legal target. Rebuke’s controller then targets Alpha Wildclaw with Star-Crossed. When Star-Crossed resolves, Alpha Wildclaw’s passive ability no longer applies to Vilemaw, making it a legal target for Rebuke. After all spells and abilities have resolved, both Vilemaw and Alpha Wildclaw will be returned to hand.</p></blockquote>
</div>

### 759. Naming Cards, Types, and Tags {#rule-759}


<div id="rule-760" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-760" aria-label="Link to rule 760">760.</a> Some Game Effects may instruct a player to name or guess a card, type, or tag.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-761" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-761" aria-label="Link to rule 761">761.</a> The act of naming a card in this way may be accomplished in one of two ways:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-761-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-761-1" aria-label="Link to rule 761.1">761.1.</a> 1. Stating the exact name of the card.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: “Kai’Sa, Evolutionary” identifies the card Kai’Sa, Evolutionary.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-761-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-761-2" aria-label="Link to rule 761.2">761.2.</a> 2. Identifying a combination of traits and characteristics that can identify that card uniquely.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: “The blue Kai’Sa,” “the 6 [M] Kai’Sa” or “the [6] Energy Kai’Sa” are sufficient to identify Kai’Sa, Evolutionary when there are no other cards that satisfy those traits.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: If there is only one unit with Kato in their name, saying “Kato” is sufficient to identify the card in question even if the exact name of the card is “Kato the Arm.”</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-762" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-762" aria-label="Link to rule 762">762.</a> When instructed to name a card, a player must name a card that is legal in the Format being played.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-762-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-762-1" aria-label="Link to rule 762.1">762.1.</a> They cannot name a card that doesn’t exist.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-762-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-762-2" aria-label="Link to rule 762.2">762.2.</a> A player cannot choose the name of a token when instructed to name a card.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-763" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-763" aria-label="Link to rule 763">763.</a> When instructed to name a tag, a player cannot choose to name a tag that does not exist on cards or tokens in Riftbound.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-763-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-763-1" aria-label="Link to rule 763.1">763.1.</a> The following tags exist in Riftbound: Ahri, Akali, Akshan, Ambessa, Anivia, Annie, Aphelios, Ashe, Azir, Bandle City, Bard, Bilgewater, Bird, Blitzcrank, Bomb, Caitlyn, Cat, Darius, Demacia, Demon, Diana, Dog, Dr. Mundo, Dragon, Draven, Ekko, Elite, Equipment, Evelynn, Ezreal, Fae, Fiora, Fizz, Freljord, Galio, Gangplank, Garen, Graves, Heimerdinger, Hwei, Icathia, Illaoi, Ionia, Irelia, Ivern, Ixtal, Janna, Jarvan IV, Jax, Jayce, Jhin, Jinx, K'Sante, Kai'Sa, Karma, Karthus, Katarina, Kathkan, Kayle, Kayn, Kennen, Kha'Zix, Kog'Maw, LeBlanc, Lee Sin, Leona, Lillia, Lucian, Lulu, Lux, Malzahar, Master Yi, Mech, Mel, Miss Fortune, Mordekaiser, Morgana, Mount Targon, Nami, Nasus, Neeko, Nidalee, Nilah, Nocturne, Noxus, Orianna, Ornn, Piltover, Pirate, Poppy, Poro, Pyke, Qiyana, Recruit, Rek'Sai, Rell, Renata Glasc, Renekton, Rengar, Riven, Rumble, Sentinel, Seraphine, Sett, Shadow Isles, Shen, Shurima, Sivir, Sona, Soraka, Spider, Spirit, Swain, Syndra, Taric, Teemo, The Void, Trifarian, Tryndamere, Twisted Fate, Udyr, Vayne, Vex, Vi, Viktor, Volibear, Warwick, Xerath, Xin Zhao, Yasuo, Yone, Yordle, Yuumi, Zaun, Zed, Ziggs, Zilean.</p>
</div>

### 764. Ignoring Effects {#rule-764}


<div id="rule-765" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-765" aria-label="Link to rule 765">765.</a> Some Game Effects may instruct players to ignore abilities while performing a game action or procedure.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-766" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-766" aria-label="Link to rule 766">766.</a> Any abilities ignored in this way are treated as inactive for the purposes of the game action or procedure.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A spell reads “Ignore Deflect while paying this spell’s costs.” Any instances of the Deflect keyword on relevant units are treated as inactive while paying the costs of the spell.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A unit reads “You ignore Backline while assigning combat damage here.” While assigning combat damage in the combat damage step with this unit present, any instances of Backline on enemy units is treated as inactive. The unit’s controller can assign damage to any units with Backline as if they didn’t have it.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-767" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-767" aria-label="Link to rule 767">767.</a> Ignored abilities are only treated as inactive for the specific game action or procedure described, and only by the players directed by the ability.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A spell reads in part “Ignore Deflect while paying this spell’s costs.” If another player attempts to play a spell targeting the same units that the above spell does, that other spell will not ignore Deflect.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A unit reads “You ignore Backline while assigning combat damage here.” Any other player assigning combat damage at the same location as that unit will not be able to ignore Backline.</p></blockquote>
</div>

### 800. Keywords {#rule-800}


<div id="rule-801" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-801" aria-label="Link to rule 801">801.</a> A Keyword is a specific term that appears on Cards that acts as a shorthand for a specific game effect, or ability of any variety.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-801-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-801-1" aria-label="Link to rule 801.1">801.1.</a> A Keyword can be an ability.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-801-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-801-2" aria-label="Link to rule 801.2">801.2.</a> Keywords can be identified by having a colored highlight behind them.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-801-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-801-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 801.2.a">801.2.a.</a> The color of the highlight has no effect on gameplay.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-801-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-801-3" aria-label="Link to rule 801.3">801.3.</a> Keywords can be referenced or specified by other Game Effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-801-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-801-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 801.3.a">801.3.a.</a> Other effects may grant Keywords.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-801-3-a-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-801-3-a-1" aria-label="Link to rule 801.3.a.1">801.3.a.1.</a> The definition and rules of the specific Keyword will determine the behavior if a Keyword is granted while it is already present.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-801-3-a-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-801-3-a-2" aria-label="Link to rule 801.3.a.2">801.3.a.2.</a> The effect that granted the Keyword will specify the duration for which it is granted.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-801-3-a-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-801-3-a-3" aria-label="Link to rule 801.3.a.3">801.3.a.3.</a> If an effect that grants a Keyword does not specify a duration, the duration is as long as that Game Object remains on the Board or in its current Non-Board Zone.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-801-3-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-801-3-b" aria-label="Link to rule 801.3.b">801.3.b.</a> Other effects may remove Keywords.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-801-3-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-801-3-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 801.3.b.1">801.3.b.1.</a> The effect that removed the Keyword will specify the duration it is removed.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-801-3-b-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-801-3-b-2" aria-label="Link to rule 801.3.b.2">801.3.b.2.</a> If an effect that removes a Keyword does not specify a duration, the duration is as long as that Game Object remains on the Board or in its current Non-Board Zone.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-802" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-802" aria-label="Link to rule 802">802.</a> A card can have any number of Keywords.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-803" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-803" aria-label="Link to rule 803">803.</a> Similar to other rules text, execute any effects of Keywords in the order listed when reading the card from top to bottom of the rules text.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-804" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-804" aria-label="Link to rule 804">804.</a> Keyword Glossary</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-805" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-805" aria-label="Link to rule 805">805.</a> Accelerate</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-805-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-805-1" aria-label="Link to rule 805.1">805.1.</a> Accelerate is an Optional Additional Cost keyword.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-805-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-805-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 805.1.a">805.1.a.</a> Accelerate is present on Units.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-805-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-805-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 805.1.b">805.1.b.</a> Accelerate is functionally short for "As you play me, you may pay [1][C] as an additional cost. If you do, I enter ready."</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-805-1-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-805-1-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 805.1.b.1">805.1.b.1.</a> If the unit has one or more domains, the Power portion of the Accelerate cost can be paid only with a Power that matches one of the domains of the unit.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-805-1-b-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-805-1-b-2" aria-label="Link to rule 805.1.b.2">805.1.b.2.</a> If the unit has no domain, the Power portion of the Accelerate cost can be paid with [A] (a Power of any domain).</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-805-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-805-2" aria-label="Link to rule 805.2">805.2.</a> Accelerate is an Optional Additional Cost to be paid as a player plays the unit with the ability.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-805-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-805-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 805.2.a">805.2.a.</a> Accelerate costs cannot be paid while the unit is on the board, only as part of the steps of playing a card.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-805-2-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-805-2-b" aria-label="Link to rule 805.2.b">805.2.b.</a> Paying the cost generates a Delayed Replacement Effect. Even if the unit loses the Accelerate keyword during the finalization process, as long as the cost was paid, that unit will still enter ready.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-805-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-805-3" aria-label="Link to rule 805.3">805.3.</a> Accelerate has no function while on the board.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-805-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-805-4" aria-label="Link to rule 805.4">805.4.</a> Multiple instances of Accelerate are redundant.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-805-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-805-5" aria-label="Link to rule 805.5">805.5.</a> Accelerate, and whether or not a unit has Accelerate, is a characteristic of the Unit and may be checked or referenced by other Game Effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-805-6" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-805-6" aria-label="Link to rule 805.6">805.6.</a> Accelerate generates a delayed replacement effect that replaces a unit entering the board exhausted with it entering ready. It does not enter exhausted and then become ready.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-805-6-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-805-6-a" aria-label="Link to rule 805.6.a">805.6.a.</a> Accelerate will not interact with, or trigger, abilities that are affected by units becoming ready.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-806" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-806" aria-label="Link to rule 806">806.</a> Action</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-806-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-806-1" aria-label="Link to rule 806.1">806.1.</a> Action is a Permissive keyword.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-806-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-806-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 806.1.a">806.1.a.</a> It is present on Cards, Rune Abilities, Legend Abilities or Permanent Abilities.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-806-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-806-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 806.1.b">806.1.b.</a> Action grants the corresponding card or effect permission to be played or activated during Showdowns, even when it is not the Controlling player's turn.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-806-1-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-806-1-c" aria-label="Link to rule 806.1.c">806.1.c.</a> Action is functionally short for the following:</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-806-1-c-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-806-1-c-1" aria-label="Link to rule 806.1.c.1">806.1.c.1.</a> On Cards: "This can be played during showdowns on any player's turn."</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-806-1-c-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-806-1-c-2" aria-label="Link to rule 806.1.c.2">806.1.c.2.</a> On Activated Abilities: "This can be activated during showdowns on any player's turn."</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-806-1-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-806-1-d" aria-label="Link to rule 806.1.d">806.1.d.</a> Action is formatted as “[Action]” on spells, or “[Action][&gt;]” on abilities.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-806-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-806-2" aria-label="Link to rule 806.2">806.2.</a> The card or effect with this keyword is not restricted to showdowns. This permission is inclusive of all other timings and options available to the ability as written or by default.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-806-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-806-3" aria-label="Link to rule 806.3">806.3.</a> Action does not alter the function of any instruction of the corresponding card or effect it is on. It is only permission.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Playing a Unit with Action still has the inherent restrictions of playing Units without Action. It can only be played to the controlling player's base or a battlefield they control.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-806-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-806-4" aria-label="Link to rule 806.4">806.4.</a> Some passive abilities may grant a card or ability Action under certain conditions. The card or ability does not have the Action keyword unless and until those circumstances are true.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-806-4-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-806-4-a" aria-label="Link to rule 806.4.a">806.4.a.</a> Those conditions might only be fulfilled while the card or ability is on the chain. In such a case, it can still be played or activated at the appropriate timing as long as doing so could fulfill the conditions.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-806-4-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-806-4-b" aria-label="Link to rule 806.4.b">806.4.b.</a> If the chain item does not fulfill the conditions by the time step 5: check legality has been reached, the actions taken while playing it are undone and it is returned to the zone it was played from if it is a card.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-806-5" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-806-5" aria-label="Link to rule 806.5">806.5.</a> Action is a referenceable characteristic.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-806-5-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-806-5-a" aria-label="Link to rule 806.5.a">806.5.a.</a> Whether or not a Game Object has Action is a characteristic of that Game Object and may be checked or referenced by other Game Effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-806-5-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-806-5-b" aria-label="Link to rule 806.5.b">806.5.b.</a> Whether or not a Spell has Action is a characteristic of that Spell and may be checked or referenced by other Game Effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-806-5-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-806-5-c" aria-label="Link to rule 806.5.c">806.5.c.</a> Whether or not an Ability has Action is a characteristic of that Ability and may be checked or referenced by other Game Effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-807" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-807" aria-label="Link to rule 807">807.</a> Assault</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-807-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-807-1" aria-label="Link to rule 807.1">807.1.</a> Assault is a Passive Ability keyword.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-807-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-807-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 807.1.a">807.1.a.</a> It is present on Units.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-807-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-807-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 807.1.b">807.1.b.</a> Assault is formatted as "Assault [X]".</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-807-1-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-807-1-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 807.1.b.1">807.1.b.1.</a> The X is referenced in the functional text of the ability.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-807-1-b-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-807-1-b-2" aria-label="Link to rule 807.1.b.2">807.1.b.2.</a> The X is referred to as the Assault Value.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-807-1-b-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-807-1-b-3" aria-label="Link to rule 807.1.b.3">807.1.b.3.</a> If X is omitted, it is presumed to be 1.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-807-1-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-807-1-c" aria-label="Link to rule 807.1.c">807.1.c.</a> It is functionally short for "While I am an attacker, I have +X [M]."</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-807-1-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-807-1-d" aria-label="Link to rule 807.1.d">807.1.d.</a> Being an attacker means the Unit has gained the Attacker designation during Combat.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-461">461</a>. Combat for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-807-1-d-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-807-1-d-1" aria-label="Link to rule 807.1.d.1">807.1.d.1.</a> Assault remains in effect as long as the Unit maintains the Attacker designation.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-807-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-807-2" aria-label="Link to rule 807.2">807.2.</a> If a Unit has Assault or has been granted Assault and is granted Assault by an additional source, the Assault Value of all granted Assault keywords is summed.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Petty Officer has Assault. It is chosen as the target of Cleave, which says "Give a unit [Assault 3] this turn." After Cleave resolves, Petty Officer has Assault 4 this turn.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-807-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-807-3" aria-label="Link to rule 807.3">807.3.</a> Assault, and whether or not a unit has Assault, is a characteristic of the Unit and may be checked or referenced by other Game Effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-808" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-808" aria-label="Link to rule 808">808.</a> Deathknell</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-808-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-808-1" aria-label="Link to rule 808.1">808.1.</a> Deathknell is a Triggered Ability keyword.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-808-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-808-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 808.1.a">808.1.a.</a> It is present on Permanents.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-808-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-808-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 808.1.b">808.1.b.</a> It is formatted as "[Deathknell][&gt;] [Effect]".</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-808-1-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-808-1-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 808.1.b.1">808.1.b.1.</a> [Effect] is the rules text for the specific instance of Deathknell. This is referred to as the Deathknell effect.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-808-1-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-808-1-c" aria-label="Link to rule 808.1.c">808.1.c.</a> It is functionally short for "When I die, [Effect]."</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-808-1-c-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-808-1-c-1" aria-label="Link to rule 808.1.c.1">808.1.c.1.</a> [Effect] is the rules text of the Deathknell effect.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-808-1-c-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-808-1-c-2" aria-label="Link to rule 808.1.c.2">808.1.c.2.</a> [Effect] may be written in the past tense. In doing so, it refers to the moment the Deathknell trigger was added to the chain, not any previous moment.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-808-1-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-808-1-d" aria-label="Link to rule 808.1.d">808.1.d.</a> The Trigger for this and similar effects that trigger on their source’s death is the Permanent being Killed and sent to the Trash.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-808-1-d-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-808-1-d-1" aria-label="Link to rule 808.1.d.1">808.1.d.1.</a> If the Permanent with the effect is not sent to the Trash, for example because its "killed" event was replaced with a recall, the triggered ability will be removed from the chain.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Draven, Audacious is killed in combat. Draven reads in part “When I die in combat, choose an opponent. They gain 1 point.” Draven’s controller has a Zhonya’s Hourglass in base. Draven’s death is replaced by him being healed, recalled, and exhausted. Draven’s triggered ability will be removed from the chain.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-808-1-d-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-808-1-d-2" aria-label="Link to rule 808.1.d.2">808.1.d.2.</a> The trigger will be added to the chain as a Pending Item before the card with an ability that triggers on its own death is moved to the trash due to a Kill instruction or a Cleanup.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-808-1-d-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-808-1-d-3" aria-label="Link to rule 808.1.d.3">808.1.d.3.</a> Before the card is moved to the Trash, note its location, its attributes, and any other details related to the effect of its triggered ability to process the trigger after it has been Finalized.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-808-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-808-2" aria-label="Link to rule 808.2">808.2.</a> Each instance of Deathknell a Permanent may have will trigger separately.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-808-2-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-808-2-a" aria-label="Link to rule 808.2.a">808.2.a.</a> The controller will choose the order to add these Triggers to the chain.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-808-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-808-3" aria-label="Link to rule 808.3">808.3.</a> Deathknell, and whether or not a permanent has Deathknell, is a characteristic of the permanent and may be checked or referenced by other Game Effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-809" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-809" aria-label="Link to rule 809">809.</a> Deflect</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-809-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-809-1" aria-label="Link to rule 809.1">809.1.</a> Deflect is a Passive Ability keyword.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-809-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-809-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 809.1.a">809.1.a.</a> It is normally present on Permanents.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-809-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-809-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 809.1.b">809.1.b.</a> It is formatted as "Deflect [X]".</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-809-1-b-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-809-1-b-1" aria-label="Link to rule 809.1.b.1">809.1.b.1.</a> The X is referenced in the functional text of the ability.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-809-1-b-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-809-1-b-2" aria-label="Link to rule 809.1.b.2">809.1.b.2.</a> The X is referred to as the Deflect Value.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-809-1-b-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-809-1-b-3" aria-label="Link to rule 809.1.b.3">809.1.b.3.</a> If X is omitted, it is presumed to be 1.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-809-1-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-809-1-c" aria-label="Link to rule 809.1.c">809.1.c.</a> It is functionally short for "Spells and abilities an opponent controls that target [me/this] cost an amount of Power equal to [Deflect Value] more to play as an additional cost for each time they choose [me/this]."</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-809-1-c-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-809-1-c-1" aria-label="Link to rule 809.1.c.1">809.1.c.1.</a> The Power used to pay this cost may always be of any Domain.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A Fury spell targets an Order unit with Deflect. The Power used to pay the Deflect cost can be any Domain; it does not need to match the Domain of the spell or the target.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-809-1-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-809-1-d" aria-label="Link to rule 809.1.d">809.1.d.</a> It is an effect that imposes a Mandatory Additional Cost on Spells and Abilities that choose the Game Object that has this ability.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-349">349</a>. Playing Cards for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-809-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-809-2" aria-label="Link to rule 809.2">809.2.</a> If a Game Object has Deflect, or has been granted Deflect, and is granted Deflect by an additional source, the Deflect Value of all granted Deflect keywords is summed.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-809-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-809-3" aria-label="Link to rule 809.3">809.3.</a> Deflect, and whether or not a Game Object has Deflect, is a characteristic of the permanent and may be checked or referenced by other Game Effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-810" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-810" aria-label="Link to rule 810">810.</a> Ganking</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-810-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-810-1" aria-label="Link to rule 810.1">810.1.</a> Ganking is a Passive Ability keyword.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-810-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-810-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 810.1.a">810.1.a.</a> It is present on Units.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-810-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-810-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 810.1.b">810.1.b.</a> It is functionally short for "I may move to a battlefield from another battlefield with a standard move."</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-810-1-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-810-1-c" aria-label="Link to rule 810.1.c">810.1.c.</a> It is a passive ability that adds permissions to the Unit's Standard Move.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-810-1-c-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-810-1-c-1" aria-label="Link to rule 810.1.c.1">810.1.c.1.</a> It does not restrict or remove options from the Unit's Standard Move.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-810-1-c-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-810-1-c-2" aria-label="Link to rule 810.1.c.2">810.1.c.2.</a> It does not have an activation cost.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-810-1-c-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-810-1-c-3" aria-label="Link to rule 810.1.c.3">810.1.c.3.</a> It does not give additional abilities or activations of Movement, only new options for the Standard Move.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-810-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-810-2" aria-label="Link to rule 810.2">810.2.</a> Multiple instances of Ganking are redundant.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-810-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-810-3" aria-label="Link to rule 810.3">810.3.</a> Ganking, and whether or not a unit has Ganking, is a characteristic of the Unit and may be checked or referenced by other Game Effects.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-811" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 0">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-811" aria-label="Link to rule 811">811.</a> Hidden</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-811-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 1">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-811-1" aria-label="Link to rule 811.1">811.1.</a> Hidden is a keyword that acts as a prerequisite to perform the Hide Discretionary Action.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-811-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-811-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 811.1.a">811.1.a.</a> It is present on Spells, Units, and Gear.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-811-1-b" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-811-1-b" aria-label="Link to rule 811.1.b">811.1.b.</a> It is functionally short for "While this card is in your hand or in your Champion Zone on your turn during an Open State, you may pay [A] to hide this facedown at a battlefield you control that doesn't already have a facedown card hidden there for as long as you control that battlefield. Beginning on the next turn, this gains [Reaction] and you may play this, ignoring its base cost."</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-811-1-c" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-811-1-c" aria-label="Link to rule 811.1.c">811.1.c.</a> It allows the player to take the Discretionary Action Hide.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-811-1-c-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-811-1-c-1" aria-label="Link to rule 811.1.c.1">811.1.c.1.</a> Hide is not a subset of Play.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-811-1-c-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-811-1-c-2" aria-label="Link to rule 811.1.c.2">811.1.c.2.</a> Hiding a card does not open a chain.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-811-1-c-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-811-1-c-3" aria-label="Link to rule 811.1.c.3">811.1.c.3.</a> Playing a card from facedown (or "from Hidden") does open a chain.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-811-1-d" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 2">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-811-1-d" aria-label="Link to rule 811.1.d">811.1.d.</a> Some choices made while playing a card from facedown are restricted to the battlefield where it was hidden. A card cannot be played from facedown if it is a spell with no valid targets under these restrictions.</p>
<p class="rule-reference">See rule <a href="#rule-355-6">355.6</a>. Targeting for more information.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-811-1-d-1" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-811-1-d-1" aria-label="Link to rule 811.1.d.1">811.1.d.1.</a> A hidden permanent must be played to that battlefield.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-811-1-d-1-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-811-1-d-1-a" aria-label="Link to rule 811.1.d.1.a">811.1.d.1.a.</a> This includes hidden gear, and overrides the normal restriction that gear have in only being allowed to be played to base.</p>
</div>

<div id="rule-811-1-d-2" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-811-1-d-2" aria-label="Link to rule 811.1.d.2">811.1.d.2.</a> If a hidden spell chooses any targets, those targets must be chosen from among options at the battlefield it was played from, unless it explicitly restricts targeting in such a way that makes this impossible.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Emperor’s Divide is a spell with hidden that reads in part “Move any number of friendly units at a battlefield to their base.” When this spell is played from facedown, all units chosen for the spell must be located at the same battlefield it was played from.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-811-1-d-3" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-811-1-d-3" aria-label="Link to rule 811.1.d.3">811.1.d.3.</a> If a play effect of a hidden permanent chooses any targets, those targets must be chosen from among options at the battlefield it was played to, unless the ability explicitly restricts targeting in a way that makes this impossible.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Blastcone Fae is a unit with Hidden and “When you play me, give a unit -2 [M] this turn, to a minimum of 1 [M].” Because this is a play effect, its target must be chosen from among units at the battlefield it was played to if Blastcone Fae was played from facedown.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: Tideturner is a unit with Hidden and “When you play me, you may choose a unit you control at another location. Move me to its location and it to my original location.” Because its play effect has a targeting restriction that can never be fulfilled by a unit at its battlefield, its target may be chosen freely from among the available options.</p></blockquote>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A spell reads in part “The next unit you play this turn can be played to any battlefield.” After playing that spell, a player plays Blastcone Fae from facedown to a battlefield other than the one where it was hidden. Blastcone Fae’s play effect must choose targets from among units at the battlefield Blastcone Fae was played to, not the one it was played from.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-811-1-d-3-a" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 4">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-811-1-d-3-a" aria-label="Link to rule 811.1.d.3.a">811.1.d.3.a.</a> A play effect of a permanent played from facedown references information from its source when determining what battlefield it was played from and what targets are legal.</p>
<blockquote class="rule-example"><p>Example: A unit reads in part “When you play me, kill another friendly unit.” If it is played from the facedown zone, the play effect must choose a friendly unit at the battlefield the unit was played from. If the unit is no longer on the board when the play effect attempts to finalize, it will not be able to choose a legal target. If the unit is no longer on the board when the play effect attempts to resolve, the play effect will not be able to reference the information of where the unit was played from, and it will mistarget.</p></blockquote>
</div>

<div id="rule-811-1-d-4" class="rule-entry" style="--rule-depth: 3">
<p><a class="rule-number" href="#rule-811-1-d-4" aria-label="Link to rule 811.1.d.4">811.1.d.4.</a> Each target is treated separately and individually when processing this rule.</p>
